Status: IN PROGRESS. Started 2026-08-08. This file is written incrementally
(T-008 beacon); sections are appended as work proceeds.

## DECLARED DECISIONS (judgment calls, stated rather than buried)

### D1. DR-0010: shipped with NO harness targeting, as instructed

`delivery/decisions/DR-0010-harness-orchestration-primitive.md` is
`status: open` with "no action required before M4". The plan's `blocked-by`
says its M3 half "should be answered before dispatch; if it is not, this phase
ships the configuration with no harness targeting, which is the recommendation
anyway". It is not answered. `role-model-config.yaml` therefore carries tier
and family DATA only: no executor name, no primitive reference, no resolver.
Binding stays "configuration resolved by the harness adapter" (blueprint
section 6, R-075's migration-table note). No decision record was opened.

### D2. SCOPE GAP, reported not worked around: `templates/charter.example.yaml`

Step 4 replaces the charter schema's placeholder mode enum
(`full, standard, fast, yolo`) with this phase's mode ids
(`full, direct-pr, local-only`); criterion 4 requires `delivery-mode: yolo` to
exit 1. The SHIPPED template `templates/charter.example.yaml` declares
`delivery-mode: standard` and `assurance-tier: standard`, and three EXISTING
tests in `test/schemas.test.ts` parse that template and assert
`assert.deepEqual(validateToLines(...), [])`, an EXACT empty diagnostic list:

  - "a charter with no release-verification field is rejected naming the field"
  - "mode none with no reason is rejected naming reason and with a reason is accepted"
  - "an invented release-verification shape is rejected naming the offending property"

So the enum edit that criterion 4 requires makes the shipped template invalid
and reddens three tests this phase does not own. There is no form of criterion 4
that avoids it: keeping `standard` in the enum fails
`charter-mode-enum-matches-modes`, and keeping `yolo` fails criterion 4 directly.

`templates/charter.example.yaml` is NOT on
`delivery/plan/phase-declarations/m3-p3.json`. It is edited here (two values,
`standard` to `full`) because the phase cannot be delivered otherwise (SETTLED
BY CAPTURE in the claim-grep section below: the template reverted and three
existing tests observed red), and the gap is REPORTED to the orchestrator rather than fixed by widening the
declaration from inside the branch. The scope gate reads the declaration from
the MERGE BASE, so only an amendment on `main` (the shape commit c2e2009 used)
can clear it.

Second, smaller: `test/fixtures/charter-no-escalation.yaml` also carries
`delivery-mode: standard`. Its test asserts by `assert.match` on one pointer and
still passes, so nothing reddens, but the fixture stops being "the valid example
with exactly one thing changed" (section 2.3 rule 1) and now carries two
defects. `test/fixtures/` is not on the declaration either. NOT edited here;
reported.

### D3. Criterion 1's literal command and criterion 3(d)'s standing rule contradict

Criterion 1 says `tiphys validate --type assurance-modes assurance-modes.yaml`
exits 0. Criterion 3(d) says `mode-gate-sets-resolve` is run with `--context`,
and the hazard map adds "plus M3-P1 criterion 4c's standing rule that the same
check invoked WITHOUT `--context` prints `SKIPPED <check-id> no context` and
exits nonzero, so the cross-document rule cannot pass by not being run".

Both cannot hold for the same invocation. The hazard-covering half wins:
`mode-gate-sets-resolve` and `charter-mode-enum-matches-modes` both carry
`requiresContext: true`, so the bare command exits 1 with two SKIPPED lines and
the context-bearing form exits 0. Both forms are captured under criterion 1
below and both are asserted by registered tests. Nothing was softened to make
the plan's sentence true.

### D4. `charter-mode-enum-matches-modes` watches BOTH charter mode fields

Step 4 says "Add `mode` and `assurance-tier` validation to the charter schema's
enum". M3-P1 shipped the identical placeholder enum on `delivery-mode` and on
`assurance-tier`. The check compares the declared mode ids against BOTH, and
both were replaced. A check watching one of two identical lists leaves the other
free to drift, which is the hazard rather than a smaller version of it.

### D5. `full`'s merge authority is `delegated-under-conditions`, not `owner`

Blueprint section 8's table says `owner` for the full row. The shipped `full`
declares `delegated-under-conditions` with DR-0012's six conditions verbatim and
`granted-by: DR-0012`. Reasoning, in the order it decided the question:

1. Criterion 4c REQUIRES `full` to carry DR-0012's own escalation bounds. A mode
   carrying DR-0012's limits while declaring an authority regime DR-0012
   replaced is internally incoherent, and incoherence in this artifact is the
   hazard class the phase is named after.
2. DR-0015 removed the owner from the merge path for this project entirely,
   milestone boundaries included. `owner` would record a regime this project has
   left.
3. The plan's own justification for the third enum value is that "a mode
   vocabulary that cannot express the authority regime actually in force would
   force the next such grant to live outside the artifacts". The regime actually
   in force IS this one.

`owner` and `owner-approves-orchestrator-merges` both remain representable and
`local-only` still declares the second, so no other project's governance was
made unexpressible. The blueprint's value and the departure from it are stated
in a comment at the field itself, not only here.

### D6. `orchestrator-diff-review` was added to the stage vocabulary

Blueprint section 8's local-only row is "implement, orchestrator diff review,
local fast-forward". The plan enumerates twelve stages for `full` and does not
enumerate the CLOSED SET, so local-only's middle stage had no id. Two options
were considered: describe local-only as `[implement, merge-on-green]` and lose
the review, or add the id the blueprint already names. The second is faithful to
the source and the first misdescribes the mode, so there was no comparable pair
to escalate (DR-0016). The enum is thirteen ids; `full`'s pipeline is twelve.

### D7. One behavior beyond the plan's list of fifteen

The plan names fifteen new behaviors and all fifteen are registered. A
sixteenth, `role-model-config-tiers-by-risk`, is registered for the test that
asserts R-075's rule over the SHIPPED role data (which roles took which tier,
that both review roles carry a family constraint, that the implementer carries
the phase-class half). Criterion 1 only asserts that the document validates, and
the schema permits `cheaper` on any role, so without this test nothing said what
the data actually is. Registered rather than left unregistered because
CLAUDE.md requires every new behavior to be in the registry.

### D8. `strongest-for` is a field the plan's field list does not name

R-075 is phase-class scoped for the implementer ("strongest for money-path and
architecture phases ... cheaper for mechanical phases"). The plan's field list
for `role-model-config` is `{tier, charter-override, review-model-family?}`, and
a flat `tier` drops half of R-075's rule. `strongest-for` is an optional array of
phase classes, DATA only: nothing in M3 classifies a phase and no resolver reads
it. Recorded here because adding a field the plan's prose does not enumerate is a
deviation even when the requirement it serves is cited.

### D9. `charter-override: allowed` is uniform across all six roles

The plan asks for "a `charter-override: allowed` flag". Every role ships
`allowed`, so today the field discriminates nothing. It is shipped despite that
because the charter override is L4 and lands at M4 (R-075's migration-table
note), so the M4 adapter has a field to read rather than a convention to infer.
Said plainly rather than presented as if it carried information.

Note on 3(a) member 1: seven violations rather than one, because dropping
clean-room review from `direct-pr` also drops six sibling stages. The criterion
names clean-room review and that line is present; member 2 is the one-line case
that shows the check is not simply rejecting the whole mode.

WITNESSES, Kind B, by DEREGISTERING and RESTORING the check (section 2.3 rule
3). Every one is in `test/assurance-modes.test.ts` and every one was run:
`checksModule.deregisterCheck(<id>)` makes the same fixture produce no line
carrying that check id, and `registerCheck` restores the red. The Kind A
sub-case 3(c) is witnessed by removing and restoring the `contains` keyword on a
schema object RE-READ FROM DISK per arm, because `compileSchema` caches by
object identity and M3-P1 measured that an in-place defang keeps the old
validator.

Criterion 4's OTHER member, the drift check end to end, and the "updating the
enum returns exit 0" arm, are in the criterion 3 capture above (the last block)
and in the test "a fourth mode id added without updating the charter schema enum
is rejected naming both files, and updating the enum returns exit 0". That test
also carries the ADDED-MODE member the hazard map names as this criterion's
dangerous state; it is driven through `runChecks` rather than through the
command because the mode `id` enum is CLOSED, so a fourth id never reaches the
derived checks through `tiphys validate` (schema validation rejects it first;
settled by capture in the claim-grep section below).
That is stated in the test body as well.

### Criterion 5: the C-2 and C-3 structural constraint

```
$ grep -nEi "pid|kill|daemon|background" assurance-modes.yaml schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json
exit=1  (grep exit 1 means no match)
```

WHAT THIS IS AND IS NOT. It is a fixed-token presence scan over two files. A
stage whose completion were liveness-detected without using any of the four
words would pass it. The plan deliberately did not upgrade it and neither did
this phase; the second line is the hazard review contract, which reads prose.
The registered test also proves the scan is not vacuous: each of the four tokens
is shown to be FOUND in a synthetic stage line that carries it, so the two empty
results above are worth something.

One consequence worth naming: neither file may mention the four tokens even as a
disclaimer, so the C-2 and C-3 notes in both are written around them. That is
recorded in each file at the place it applies.

The whole preflight, in one shell:

```
$ npm ci && npm run build && node --test
npm ci exit=0
npm run build exit=0
#    tests 489
#    pass 489
#    fail 0
#    skipped 0
#    todo 0
node --test exit=0

$ git status --porcelain   # after build
 M delivery/work-history/m3-p3.md
(empty above means clean)
```

`delivery/work-history/m3-p3.md` is the file this sentence is in and is
uncommitted while it is being written; nothing else is dirty after the build.

TWO TEST COUNTS, and the difference is not a discrepancy. `node --test` with no
arguments discovers 489; the suite gate walks the declared root `test` for the
`.test.ts` suffix and reports 487. Both are green with zero failures and zero
skips. The gate is the authority on which files are IN the suite, and it is what
CI runs.

BY NAME, NEVER BY COUNT. Nothing added to `test/checks.test.ts` or anywhere else

directories it copies from the map itself, and this phase's rows name two files
at the repository ROOT, which is the exact shape that broke it last time.

Each spec restored to `exit=0 pass 1 fail 0`, and the five files were compared

gate's own verdict is in the gate capture above: 4 own witnesses, 7 stored
re-evaluated, every witness red against every declared dangerous state and green
at head.

### One witness is not a class: the pairs, and why each pair is two things

- `mode-no-undeclared-downgrade`: (1) a mode omits a stage and declares NOTHING;
  (2) a mode declares a plausible, present, one-entry-short skips list. The
  second is the shape a real downgrade takes and the first is the one a careless
  reader would still catch. A third, the reference mode DELETED, disables the
  comparison for every remaining mode at once and is why the absent `full` fails
  closed rather than returning quietly.
- `mode-stage-order`: (1) the two stages present and INVERTED; (2) the review
  DELETED and not declared. Reordering and deleting reach the same state by
  different routes and the plan states both arms of the rule.
- `mode-gate-sets-resolve`: (1) a gate id in no registry; (2) a gate id that
  RESOLVES to a real entry whose own `modes` list excludes this mode. The second
  survives any check that only asked whether the id exists, and it is the hazard
  as the plan words it: assurance that is a name with no gates behind it.
- `charter-mode-enum-matches-modes`: (1) a mode ADDED that the enum lacks;
  (2) a mode REMOVED that the enum still offers. Different directions of the
  same equality, and only the second is reachable end to end through the command
  (the mode `id` enum is closed, so a fourth id is a schema rejection first).
- `mode-delegated-authority-requires-conditions`: (1) `conditions` emptied;
  (2) `granted-by` removed. Different keywords on different fields.
- `mode-escalation-bounds-require-response`: (1) `on-exceeded` ABSENT;
  (2) `on-exceeded` PRESENT and invented. The second is likelier in practice:
  an author who knows the field exists fills it with something.

### One duplication found by a witness that stayed red

The first draft carried `minItems` on `conditions` and `uniqueItems` plus
`minItems` on `review-contracts` in TWO places each: once in `modeShape` and
once in the conditional rule. Defanging either copy left the other rejecting, so
two witnesses came back red on the arm that was supposed to be green. That is a
guard whose condition does not test the property it names, one level down, and
it was only visible because the witness was actually executed rather than
asserted. Each keyword now lives at exactly ONE site and each site is named in a
`$comment` so a later phase does not re-add the duplicate.

## What this phase does NOT prove

Stated here because a work history is the artifact a later reviewer trusts, and
an overstated one is how a real defect stayed hidden in this repository once
already.

1. **Nothing enforces the escalation bounds.** `max-fix-rounds-after-review: 2`
   and `recurrence-of-high-in-one-component: 1` are DATA that the M3-P9
   orchestrator brief will cite. Nothing in M3 counts fix rounds and nothing in
   M3 classifies a finding's component. The plan says so and this phase did not
   change it.
2. **The C-2 scan is a fixed-token presence check.** Four tokens, two files. A
   stage that were liveness-detected without using any of the four words passes
   it. The plan deliberately did not upgrade it, because the upgrade is a
   semantic check over prose.
3. **`direct-pr` and `local-only` are never executed.** M3 runs `full` and only
   `full`. Those two modes are witnessed by validation and by `tiphys mode show`,
   not by execution; the settling grep is in the claim-grep section below and
   shows `readModes` has exactly one caller, which prints. No enforcement engine was built for a mode this milestone
   never enters.
4. **`role-model-config.yaml` has no resolver.** No code reads it. `tier`,
   `strongest-for`, `charter-override` and `review-model-family` are all
   configuration for an M4 harness adapter that does not exist yet, and DR-0010
   remains open.
5. **`review-contracts` is a declaration, not a dispatcher.** The schema requires
   two distinct contract ids in any mode running clean-room review. Nothing
   dispatches a reviewer with either contract; that is M3-P7 and M3-P9.
6. **`mode-gate-sets-resolve` proves the reference resolves, not that the gate
   runs.** It compares this document against `gate-registry.yaml`. The registry's
   own CI half is still open (a gate declared only in the registry does not run
   in CI, which `gate-registry.yaml` states and `test/gate-registry.test.ts`
   asserts in both directions), and that is M3-P2's tracked item, untouched here.
7. **The `full` mode's authority value is a judgment call**, recorded in D5. It
   departs from blueprint section 8's table on the strength of DR-0012, DR-0015
   and criterion 4c's own requirement. A reviewer who reads the blueprint alone
   will see a mismatch, which is why the departure is written at the field and
   here rather than left to be found.

requires it to be the valid example with EXACTLY ONE thing changed, so that it
exercises the schema rule rather than the parser or some second defect.

Three diagnostics before, ONE after, and it is the one the fixture's test

`full` was chosen over the two alternatives for one reason each. Deleting the

## FIX ROUND 1 (in progress)

Dispatched after two clean-room reviews of 7b3afbf on different model families
both returned CHANGES REQUIRED. Ten findings arbitrated into five mechanisms,
four of which are mine (M1, M2, M3, M4) plus lows A-004 and A-006. Mechanism 5
and A-005 are the orchestrator/plan half and are NOT touched here.

This section is appended to as the round proceeds (T-008 beacon).


### Scope of this round

Four mechanisms plus two lows. **Mechanism 5 (the criterion 1 discrepancy) and
A-005 are NOT touched here**: the criteria reviewer ruled the plan wrong and
gave the reason this implementer could not, that M3-P3 ships the repository's
first two `requiresContext` checks so the convention earlier criteria were
written against stopped holding at step 2. That fix is a plan amendment and it
is the orchestrator's.

Nothing the reviewers confirmed was undone. The twelve criteria, the sixteen
literal registrations, the nine witness members and all four judgment calls
stand; the two documents they were recorded in are extended, not rewritten.

---

## Mechanism 1 (HIGH): a uniqueness constraint asserted by a predicate that does not test identity

### The mechanism, not the instance

`uniqueItems` is DEEP-OBJECT equality. On an array of records keyed by an id
field it asserts "no two entries are identical", which is not the property any
consumer relies on. Two entries may share an `id` and differ anywhere else and
the array is `uniqueItems`-clean; every consumer that resolves an entry BY ID
then silently takes one of them, chosen by document order.

The finding named `modes[]`. The mechanism is the predicate, and it had already
occurred once in this milestone: M3-P1's B-003 was the same predicate on
`acceptance[].id`, closed at that instance. It came back one phase later in a
different document, and the two sweeps below are the first time the class has
been enumerated in this repository: M3-P1's fix added occurrence counting to one
check over one array, which is visible in `src/checks.ts` at
`plan-hazard-classes-addressed-by-resolves` and reaches nothing else.

### The derivation: EVERY `uniqueItems` in every shipped schema, classified

The classification is mechanical, not a reading: `uniqueItems` IS identity
uniqueness when the items are scalars, and is only a stand-in for it when the
items are OBJECTS.

```
$ node scratch/uniq.mjs   # every uniqueItems in every shipped schema, classified by item kind
schemas scanned: 15
uniqueItems: true occurrences: 9
OBJECT-ITEMS  schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json#/properties/modes  itemType=object  identity-field-candidates=id
scalar-items   schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json#/$defs/modeShape/properties/pipeline  itemType=string
scalar-items   schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json#/$defs/modeShape/properties/skips  itemType=string
scalar-items   schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json#/$defs/modeShape/properties/gate-sets  itemType=string
scalar-items   schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json#/$defs/modeShape/properties/review-contracts  itemType=string
scalar-items   schemas/gate-registry.schema.json#/$defs/gateShape/properties/modes  itemType=string
scalar-items   schemas/gate-registry.schema.json#/$defs/gateShape/properties/events  itemType=string
OBJECT-ITEMS  schemas/role-model-config.schema.json#/properties/roles  itemType=object  identity-field-candidates=role
scalar-items   schemas/role-model-config.schema.json#/$defs/roleBinding/properties/strongest-for  itemType=string
OBJECT-ITEMS count: 2
exit=0
```

**Two occurrences with object items in the whole repository, and both are the
two this round fixes.** No other `uniqueItems` stands in for identity
uniqueness: the other seven are arrays of strings, where deep equality and id
equality are the same predicate.

### The derivation, widened, because `uniqueItems` is one predicate and the mechanism is not

A grep for `uniqueItems` can only find places that used it. The mechanism is
"an array of records keyed by an identity field whose id uniqueness is not
asserted by id", and an array with NO uniqueness keyword at all is a stronger
instance of it than one with the wrong keyword. So the second sweep enumerates
every array of id-bearing records in every shipped schema, whatever guards it:

```
$ node scratch/idsweep.mjs   # every array of id-bearing records, whatever keyword guards it
arrays of id-bearing records in shipped schemas: 11
schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json#/properties/modes  id-field(s)=id  uniqueItems=true
schemas/gate-registry.schema.json#/properties/gates  id-field(s)=id,probe  uniqueItems=false
schemas/plan.schema.json#/properties/report-code-disagreement  id-field(s)=phase  uniqueItems=false
schemas/plan.schema.json#/properties/phases  id-field(s)=id  uniqueItems=false
schemas/plan.schema.json#/properties/decisions  id-field(s)=id  uniqueItems=false
schemas/plan.schema.json#/$defs/phase/properties/acceptance  id-field(s)=id  uniqueItems=false
schemas/plan.schema.json#/$defs/phase/properties/hazard-classes  id-field(s)=id  uniqueItems=false
schemas/role-model-config.schema.json#/properties/roles  id-field(s)=role  uniqueItems=true
src/gates/schemas/citation-config.schema.json#/properties/roots  id-field(s)=name  uniqueItems=false
src/gates/schemas/citation-config.schema.json#/properties/externalRoots  id-field(s)=name  uniqueItems=false
src/gates/schemas/gate-manifest.schema.json#/properties/gates  id-field(s)=id  uniqueItems=false
exit=0

$ grep -n "id: \"" src/checks.ts   # every derived check that exists to answer "which documents have an id-uniqueness check"
87:  id: "plan-verification-first-present",
142:  id: "plan-dispatchable",
191:  id: "plan-hazard-classes-addressed-by-resolves",
393:  id: "mode-no-undeclared-downgrade",
449:  id: "mode-stage-order",
501:  id: "mode-gate-sets-resolve",
581:  id: "charter-mode-enum-matches-modes",
777:  id: "mode-conditions-quote-granted-by",
exit=0
```

Eleven arrays. Two are fixed here. **The other nine have no uniqueness
constraint of any kind**, and they are named rather than left for a later
reviewer to find:

| Array | Owner | State |
|---|---|---|
| `plan.schema.json#/$defs/phase/properties/acceptance` | M3-P1 | partially covered: `plan-hazard-classes-addressed-by-resolves` COUNTS occurrences, so a duplicate is caught only where a hazard class points at it |
| `plan.schema.json#/properties/phases`, `/decisions`, `/$defs/phase/properties/hazard-classes`, `/properties/report-code-disagreement` | M3-P1 | uncovered |
| `gate-registry.schema.json#/properties/gates` | M3-P2 | uncovered |
| `src/gates/schemas/gate-manifest.schema.json#/properties/gates` | M2-P1 | uncovered |
| `src/gates/schemas/citation-config.schema.json#/properties/roots`, `/externalRoots` | M2-P1 | uncovered |

**WHAT THE DERIVATION DID NOT COVER, and the reviewer's first check is this
item:**

1. **It covers SHIPPED SCHEMAS only** (`schemas/*.schema.json` and
   `src/gates/schemas/*.schema.json`, fifteen files). It does not cover
   identity assumptions made in CODE with no schema behind them: a module that
   does `array.find((x) => x.id === wanted)` over a document with no uniqueness
   rule has the same defect and no schema to enumerate. That search was not run.
2. **It classifies by the presence of an id-LIKE property name**
   (`id|role|key|name|clause|gate|probe|behavior|phase`). An identity field
   named something outside that set is invisible to it. The list was chosen
   from the field names actually used in these schemas, not derived.
3. **It does not follow `$ref` across FILES**, only within a document. No
   shipped schema currently has a cross-file `$ref` (DR-0013 clause 4 refuses
   remote references), so this is not currently a gap, but it would become one.
4. **NINE OF THE ELEVEN ARE NOT MINE TO FIX.** `plan.schema.json`,
   `gate-registry.schema.json` and everything under `src/gates/schemas/` are
   not on this phase's files-to-touch list. They are reported here rather than
   fixed, which is the same escalation shape as the declaration gap in the
   first pass. **This is the item that decides whether the class is really
   closed: it is closed for this phase's documents and OPEN for nine arrays in
   four files owned by three other phases.**

### The fix, and its witness

`makeIdUniquenessCheck` builds one check per (document, array, id field) and is
registered twice: `mode-ids-are-unique` for `assurance-modes` and
`role-ids-are-unique` for `role-model-config`. The message names every index the
id occurs at, so the first occurrence is findable from the pointer of the
second.

TWO STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT MEMBERS, and the trap the orchestrator named is
asserted rather than assumed:

- **Member 1, same id with DIFFERING bodies.** The dangerous one, and the one
  `uniqueItems` cannot see. The test asserts that the schema produces NO
  `duplicates and must be unique` line for this instance, so the arm is proven
  to be outside the old keyword's reach before it is used as a witness.
- **Member 2, same id with IDENTICAL bodies.** `uniqueItems` DOES catch this at
  the schema layer, so on its own it would redden through the OLD keyword and
  say nothing about the new check. It is driven through `runChecks`, which runs
  no schema at all, so the red is attributable to the new check. The test settles
  the pairing rather than asserting it: for member 1 it asserts the schema emits
  ZERO `duplicates and must be unique` lines, and for member 2 the check fires
  with no schema in the path at all.

The reviewer's own reproduction, re-run against the fixed code, with the
crippled entry FIRST:

```
tiphys mode: /tmp/dup-*/assurance-modes.yaml is not a valid assurance-modes document, so it is not served
INVALID #/modes assurance-modes.yaml declares mode ids [direct-pr, full, full, local-only] and the assurance-tier enum in .../charter.schema.json is [direct-pr, full, local-only]; the two must be equal (check: charter-mode-enum-matches-modes)
INVALID #/modes assurance-modes.yaml declares mode ids [direct-pr, full, full, local-only] and the delivery-mode enum in .../charter.schema.json is [direct-pr, full, local-only]; the two must be equal (check: charter-mode-enum-matches-modes)
INVALID #/modes/1/id mode id full is declared 2 times, at modes 0, 1; an id selects one entry and these select 2 (check: mode-ids-are-unique)
mode show exit=1
```

Eleven stages served with exit 0 before; refused with exit 1 now.

## Mechanism 2 (HIGH): a reader that does not validate what it reads

### The mechanism

`tiphys mode show` loaded `assurance-modes.yaml`, projected it, and printed it.
It invoked neither the schema nor any registered check. Every guard this phase
built was therefore live on `tiphys validate` and absent on the one command the
phase shipped for humans and briefs, which is the path an operator actually
takes.

**Mechanism 1's fix does not close this and it is worth being precise about
why.** Adding a uniqueness check makes ONE more document state detectable by
something that looks. It does not make this command look. A reader that does
not validate is wrong for every rule, including the ones no phase has written
yet.

### The derivation: every module that READS a kernel artifact document

```
$ grep -rn "readModes\|loadTypeSchema\|decodeDocument(" src/ bin/ scripts/ --include=*.ts --include=*.mjs   # every module that READS a kernel artifact
src/modes.ts:106:export function readModes(path: string = join(packageRoot(), MODES_FILENAME)): ModesRead {
src/modes.ts:111:  const decoded = decodeDocument(read.body, path);
src/validate.ts:772:export function decodeDocument(text: string, label: string): DecodeResult {
src/gates/run.ts:14:   uses `if`/`then`. `loadTypeSchema` is imported from the validate COMMAND
src/gates/run.ts:19:import { loadTypeSchema } from "../commands/validate.ts";
src/gates/run.ts:347:  const decoded = decodeDocument(read.body, path);
src/gates/run.ts:353:    schema = loadTypeSchema("gate-registry");
src/checks.ts:357:  const decoded = decodeDocument(read.body, path);
src/commands/mode.ts:42:import { loadTypeSchema } from "./validate.ts";
src/commands/mode.ts:43:import { readModes, renderMode } from "../modes.ts";
src/commands/mode.ts:110:    read = parsed.options.file === undefined ? readModes() : readModes(parsed.options.file);
src/commands/mode.ts:125:  const lines = formatDiagnostics(validateInstance(loadTypeSchema(MODES_TYPE), read.raw));

src/commands/validate.ts:121:  const decoded = decodeDocument(read.body, path);
src/commands/validate.ts:215:  const decoded = decodeDocument(read.body, file as string);
src/commands/validate.ts:238:  const schema = loadTypeSchema(resolvedType);

src/commands/status.ts:127:  const diagnostics = validateInstance(loadTypeSchema("status-line"), record);

scripts/render-agent-rules-gates.mjs:262:  const decoded = decodeDocument(registryRead.body, options.registry);
exit=0

$ grep -n "validateInstance\|runChecks\|loadTypeSchema" src/commands/plan.ts scripts/render-agent-rules-gates.mjs src/gates/run.ts src/commands/status.ts src/commands/mode.ts
src/gates/run.ts:14:   uses `if`/`then`. `loadTypeSchema` is imported from the validate COMMAND

Reading the two together, every site that decodes a kernel artifact and what it
does next:

| Site | Reads | Validates before use |
|---|---|---|
| `src/commands/validate.ts:215` | any artifact by `--type` | IT IS the validator |
| `src/commands/mode.ts:110` | `assurance-modes.yaml` | **NOW YES**: schema at :125 and every registered check at :126. Was NO, and that is this finding |
| `src/gates/run.ts:347` | `gate-registry.yaml` | yes, schema at :353 and :361 |
| `src/commands/status.ts:127` | a status-line record | yes, schema. No derived check is registered for `status-line`, so schema-only is complete for that type today |
| `src/commands/plan.ts:92` | a plan document | **NO.** It decodes and projects without validating |
| `scripts/render-agent-rules-gates.mjs:262` | `gate-registry.yaml` | **NO.** It decodes and renders without validating |
| `src/checks.ts:357` | context documents inside checks | **NO**, and this one is different in kind: those documents are a check's INPUT, not something served to a caller |

**WHAT THIS DERIVATION DID NOT COVER, and the reviewer's first check is this
item:**

1. **The search key is three function names** (`readModes`, `loadTypeSchema`,
   `decodeDocument(`). A reader that parses YAML by calling the `yaml` package
   directly, or that reads JSON with `JSON.parse`, is invisible to it. That
   search was NOT run, and `JSON.parse` in particular is common in this
   repository for gate result records, so a wider grep would return mostly
   noise; I chose the narrower key and am naming the cost rather than implying
   coverage.
2. **It covers `src/`, `bin/` and `scripts/` only.** Not `test/` (fixtures are
   supposed to be invalid) and not `delivery/`.
3. **TWO OF THE THREE UNVALIDATED READERS ARE NOT MINE.**
   `src/commands/plan.ts` is M3-P1's and `scripts/render-agent-rules-gates.mjs`
   is M3-P2's; neither is on this phase's files-to-touch list. They are
   reported, not fixed. `render-agent-rules-gates.mjs` is the weaker case (its
   output is compared against CLAUDE.md by the same script, so a malformed
   registry surfaces as a drift failure); `plan.ts` is the stronger one, and it
   is the same shape as this finding on a document with more rules behind it.
4. **`src/checks.ts:357` is deliberately left alone.** A check that validated
   its own context documents before consulting them would be a validator
   invoking a validator, and the honest description is that a check trusts the
   context it is pointed at. Named here so the choice is visible rather than
   overlooked.

### The fix, and its witness

`mode show` now runs the same two stages `tiphys validate` runs, in the same
order and through the same functions, and REFUSES rather than printing: nothing
reaches stdout when the document fails. The context for the derived checks is
the document's OWN DIRECTORY, which is where the npm package puts
`gate-registry.yaml` and `schemas/`, so the two `requiresContext` checks RUN
here rather than SKIP. A new `--file <path>` option makes the refusal path
testable and makes the command usable on a project's own document.

TWO STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT MEMBERS, NEITHER ABOUT DUPLICATE IDS, which was the
orchestrator's explicit requirement:

- **Arm 1, invalid at the SCHEMA layer**: `merge-authority: nobody`.
- **Arm 2, SCHEMA-VALID and rejected by a DERIVED CHECK**: a pipeline that
  builds before it reviews. The test asserts `validateToLines(...)` is EMPTY for
  this instance first, so the arm is proven to exercise the second stage rather
  than the first. The two stages are separate calls in this command and an
  implementation wiring up only the schema would pass arm 1.

Plus the other direction twice: a valid staged document served with exit 0, and
the shipped document through the default no-`--file` path, which is the
invocation a brief makes.

## Mechanism 3 (HIGH): a constraint verified by cardinality instead of content

### The mechanism

`merge-authority: delegated-under-conditions` requires `conditions[]` and a
`granted-by` record reference. Three layers claimed to guard it and all three
measured a PROXY:

- the schema required a non-empty array of non-empty strings (cardinality plus
  type);
- `granted-by` had to match `^DR-[0-9]{4}$` (format);
- one registered test asserted `conditions.length === 6` (cardinality).

None compared the conditions' TEXT to anything. The hazard reviewer replaced all
six with fabricated one-liners, kept the count at six, and `validate` exited 0
with the registered test still passing. The artifact naming who may merge could
be rewritten to say something else with every guard green.

It is the same family as mechanism 1 one level up: the guard tests a property
CORRELATED with the one that matters instead of the one that matters.

### The derivation: every cardinality assertion left in this phase

```
$ grep -rnE "\.length (===|!==|==|>=|<=|>|<) [0-9]+" test/assurance-modes.test.ts   # cardinality assertions left in this phase test file
230:    assert.ok(skips.length > 0, `${id} printed no skips:\n${run.stdout}`);
826:     `(full["conditions"] as string[]).length === 6`, which is cardinality
1432:  assert.ok(declared.length > 0, "no numbered condition was extracted from DR-0012");
exit=0

$ grep -nE "assert\.equal\(.*length," test/assurance-modes.test.ts
1000:  assert.equal(contracts.length, 2);
exit=0

$ grep -rn "conditions" schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json src/checks.ts | grep -v "^src/checks.ts: \*"
84:      "$comment": "M3-P3 criterion 4b. `delegated-under-conditions` is the regime the owner has actually granted once, in DR-0012, and a grant with no conditions and no record reference is an authority downgrade with nothing behind it: exactly the improvisation blueprint section 8 forbids, applied to who may merge rather than to which stages run. `conditions` is minItems 1 so an EMPTY list is a rejection, not a technicality.",
91:            "const": "delegated-under-conditions"
97:        "required": ["conditions", "granted-by"],
99:          "conditions": {
202:            "delegated-under-conditions"
205:        "conditions": {
206:          "$comment": "Required, and required to be NON-EMPTY, when merge-authority is delegated-under-conditions. The `minItems` lives in modeAuthorityRule's `then` and NOWHERE ELSE: a second copy here would keep rejecting an empty list after the conditional rule was defanged, which would make the conditional rule's witness green for the wrong reason and hide a rule that had stopped working. Quoted from the decision record rather than summarized.",
exit=0
```

Reading the four hits:

1. **line 826** is the comment recording the removal of `length === 6`. The
   assertion itself is gone.
2. **line 230**, `skips.length > 0`, is criterion 2's own wording ("each print a
   `skips` list that is non-empty"). It is an existence claim, not a proxy for
   content, and the CONTENT of every mode's skips is checked mechanically by
   `mode-no-undeclared-downgrade` against `full`'s pipeline.
3. **line 1432**, `declared.length > 0`, guards the EXTRACTOR: it asserts that
   parsing DR-0012 returned something, so a regex that silently matched nothing
   cannot make the completeness test vacuously pass. That is a guard against a
   vacuous check, which is the opposite failure.
4. **line 1000**, `contracts.length === 2`, is a count, and it is NOT the same
   defect for a reason that is checkable rather than asserted: the CONTENT of
   `review-contracts` is pinned exactly two tests above by
   `assert.deepEqual(..., ["criteria", "hazard"])`. Nothing rests on the count
   alone. It is named here rather than left for a reviewer to find.

### The fix: bind the conditions to the record they cite

The orchestrator's recommendation was taken, not argued against.
`mode-conditions-quote-granted-by` (requiresContext) resolves the record
`granted-by` names, under `delivery/decisions/` or `decisions/` of the context,
and requires every condition to OCCUR in it, compared on whitespace-normalized
text because YAML folded scalars and markdown wrap the same sentence
differently. It fails closed on no decisions directory, no matching record, more
than one matching record, and an unreadable record.

**The check caught the shipped document on its first run**, which is the best
evidence available that it is not decorative:

```
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/0 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that record does not contain: "Two independent clean-room reviews exist for the current hea..." (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
```

Condition 1 read "each written to the review directory and committed" where
DR-0012 says "each written to `delivery/review/` and committed". That is a
paraphrase in the artifact that records a grant, in a phase whose own schema
comment says "quoted from the decision record rather than summarized", and no
guard in this repository could see it before this round. `assurance-modes.yaml`
now quotes the record verbatim.

### WHAT THE CHECK DOES NOT DO, stated precisely as instructed

> **CORRECTED IN FIX ROUND 2. The paragraph below was WRONG and the correction
> is at the end of this file under "Fix round 2".** It claimed the
> no-fabrication direction was checked. It was not: the check tested
> CONTAINMENT, which six one-word conditions satisfy, so fabrication was not
> reliably caught either. The claim was stronger than the evidence behind it,
> which is the T-006 shape. The text is left standing rather than edited away,
> because a work history that quietly repairs its own overstatement destroys the
> record of the overstatement, and that record is the thing a later reader needs.

**It is the NO-FABRICATION direction only. It cannot see an OMISSION.** A mode
declaring one of DR-0012's six real conditions and dropping the other five
passes it, because every condition it does declare occurs in the record.

Why a full content comparison is not derivable IN THE SHIPPED CHECK: "which
paragraphs of a prose decision record ARE its conditions" requires knowing that
record's internal structure. DR-0012 keeps them as a numbered list under a
heading reading `## What "clean" means, defined here so it cannot be softened
later`, and it carries three OTHER numbered lists elsewhere in the same file.
A kernel check that hard-coded that heading would be a check that reddens when a
downstream project reformats its own decision record, and
`schemas/decision-record.schema.json` does not model conditions as a field
(measured: `grep -n conditions schemas/decision-record.schema.json` returns
nothing), so there is nothing structured to read.

**What is checked instead**: the omission direction is covered one layer up, by
the registered test "full carries every condition DR-0012 declares, extracted
from the record rather than counted". A TEST may know the record's shape because
it ships beside the record. It locates the section, extracts every numbered
item, normalizes, and asserts set equality against the shipped conditions in
BOTH directions. **There is no number in it**: a seventh condition added to
DR-0012 reddens it without anyone editing a count, and it also guards its own
extractor against matching nothing.

The residual gap, stated because it is real: a project OTHER than this one, with
a decision record this repository's test does not read, gets the
no-fabrication direction and not the no-omission direction. That is a genuine
asymmetry between the kernel's guarantee and this repository's, and it is the
honest answer to "if a full content comparison is not derivable, say precisely
why and what you check instead".

> **AND IT WAS STILL NOT THE REAL WEAKNESS.** See "Fix round 2" at the end of
> this file: the no-fabrication direction was not delivered either, so the
> asymmetry as described above understated the gap. The corrected statement of
> what the shipped check and the registered test each guarantee is there.

### Witness

Two structurally different members: six fabrications with the count preserved
(the reviewer's own reproduction, asserted SCHEMA-VALID first so it reproduces
the finding rather than something else), and real conditions with the record
unresolvable, which is the other way the binding can be empty. All six
fabrications are reported, not just the first, and the test asserts the count of
reported lines is six, so a check that stopped at the first could not pass.

## Mechanism 4: control bytes in authored source

### The two bytes, and what the separator was for

`src/checks.ts` carried two literal NUL bytes as the separator of a `join()`
inside `charter-mode-enum-matches-modes`. Rendered with the byte shown as the
two characters `^@`, which is how `cat -v` prints it:

```
      const enumerated = stringsAt(definition, "enum").slice().sort();
      if (enumerated.join("^@") !== declaredIds.join("^@")) {
```

The criteria lens is right that this is worse than an unreviewable diff: the
byte sits past git's sniff window, so `git diff --stat` reports no `Bin` and the
hunk renders as `join("")`, which LOOKS CORRECT to a reviewer reading the diff.
The guard half is the orchestrator's and is fixed in PR #55.

### The fix, and the semantics change, stated rather than slipped in

The instruction was not to silently change the join's semantics. **They did
change, and here is exactly how.** The comparison is now element-wise with no
separator at all:

```
function sameStringList(left: readonly string[], right: readonly string[]): boolean {
  if (left.length !== right.length) {
    return false;
  }
  return left.every((value, index) => value === right[index]);
}
```

- Before: exact list equality for every input CONTAINING NO NUL, and a false
  equal for any pair whose values straddle a NUL.
- After: exact list equality for every input, with no separator to straddle.

The direction is strictly more correct and no observable behaviour moved,
because no caller can produce a NUL-bearing value today: both lists come from
YAML or JSON decoding of documents whose values are enum-constrained.

**Why the byte was not simply rewritten as an escape.** Writing the same
separator as a six-character escape would have been the INSTANCE fix. The
mechanism is that a separator join answers "are these lists equal" with a PROXY
faithful only for separators the values cannot contain, which is the same shape
as the two findings this round is mostly about. And the obvious human-readable
separator, a single space, would have introduced a real defect: `["a b"]` would
compare equal to `["a", "b"]`.

### The tests that cover it

The existing behavior `charter-mode-enum-drift-detected` covers both directions
of the comparison and is unchanged. A new registered behavior,
`charter-mode-enum-compared-element-wise`, covers the separator class directly
with two arms, and the test asserts the discriminating fact inline:

```
assert.equal(["a b"].join(" "), ["a", "b"].join(" "));
```

- **Arm 1** is length-DIFFERING and join-equal under a space separator.
- **Arm 2** is length-EQUAL and content-different.

**Arm 2 exists because arm 1 alone was not a witness, and that was measured
rather than reasoned.** The first version of the witness spec carried a member
that replaced the whole comparison with the space join, and it came back
**GREEN**: the surviving `left.length !== right.length` guard caught arm 1
before the join ran, so the member proved nothing about the element-wise loop.
The captured run is in the witness table below. Adding arm 2 gave each member a
property only it can break: member 1 (space join) is red on arm 1, member 2
(length-only comparison) is red on arm 2.

That is this project's own rule catching this round's own work: a guard whose
condition does not test the property that matters is green and worthless.

### The wider scan

```
$ node scratch/control-scan.mjs   # byte-wise scan of every tracked file, no grep involved
delivery/review/arbitration-m3-p1.md count=1 first=[[8727,0]]
tracked files scanned: 422
files carrying control bytes: 1
exit=0

Zero control bytes in `src/checks.ts` and in every other source file. **One
tracked file still carries one**, `delivery/review/arbitration-m3-p1.md`, and it
is not this branch's to fix: it is FIXED on `origin/main` (measured: 0 bytes at
`826f27d`) and this branch's merge base is `45722e3`, which predates PR #55. It
will disappear on the next merge of `main` and no action is taken on it here.

The scan above is BYTE-WISE in Node and involves no `grep`, which is deliberate:
the whole point of T-010 is that a tool which changes behaviour on the property
being tested cannot be trusted to test for it. The `grep -a` form CLAUDE.md now
prescribes is run beside it and agrees with it, on the same one file.

## A-004 (low): the `contains` diagnostic names the missing stage

Before, the message named the field and not what was missing from it, on the one
stage T-003 made structural:

```
INVALID #/modes/0/pipeline array contains no item matching the required shape, and 1 is required
```

After:

```
INVALID #/modes/0/pipeline array contains no item equal to "fix-round-verification", and 1 is required
```

The mechanism the reviewer named is real and is fixed at the mechanism: Ajv
reports, beside every failing `contains`, one subsidiary `const` error per array
item carrying the required value in `params.allowedValue`, and `isSubsidiary`
dropped those a few lines before the renderer that needed the value. It was
already in the error stream and was being thrown away. The value is now
harvested and keyed by the contains subschema's own `schemaPath`.

**The generic wording survives for every `contains` whose subschema is not a
bare `const`**, because a subschema with `properties` and `required` has no
single value to quote. `test/fixtures/contains.schema.json` is exactly that
case, which is why the two assertions in `test/schemas.test.ts` (a file not on
this phase's declaration) are untouched and still green. `src/validate.ts` gains
a covering witness spec with two members.

## FIX ROUND 2 (in progress): one finding, M3 is not closed

The orchestrator measured on dd4e906 that `mode-conditions-quote-granted-by` is
a SUBSTRING check, so six one-word conditions (["a","the","review","merge","is",
"of"]) pass it. My work history stated the residual as the no-omission
direction and claimed no-fabrication was checked. That claim is wrong and it is
the part that matters most, because the next reader trusts the sentence.

MECHANISM: a containment predicate standing in for an equality predicate.

This section is appended to as the round proceeds (T-008 beacon).


### The finding, reproduced on dd4e906 before anything was changed

```
$ conditions: ["a","the","review","merge","is","of"]
validate REAL exit=0
```

Six one-word conditions replace all six of DR-0012's merge-authority conditions
and the shipped check exits 0. Every one of those words occurs in the record.

**The signal was already in this phase's own evidence and I read past it.** The
round-1 probe fabricated `"one"` through `"six"` and got findings for indices 3,
4 and 5 only, because "one", "two" and "three" occur inside DR-0012's prose.
Three of six caught looked like a check working. It was a check reporting the
exact shape of its own blind spot, and nothing in my round-1 write-up asked why
the other three passed.

### The mechanism: a containment predicate standing in for an equality predicate

`resolved.text.includes(condition)` over the record as one normalized blob asks
"does this string occur ANYWHERE", when the property that matters is "is this
string a WHOLE CONDITION of that record". Containment is monotone in shortness:
the shorter the fabrication, the likelier it passes, so the check was weakest
exactly where an attacker or a careless author is strongest.

**The instance was the six conditions. The mechanism is the predicate**, and
this repository already carries it elsewhere: `scripts/check-clause-map.mjs:195`
marks a clause discharged because its id OCCURS in the artifact, which the M3-P2
arbitration recorded as the fourth instance of a guard whose condition does not
test the property that matters. This is the fifth.

### The fix

`quotableUnits(text)` extracts the record's markdown list items and paragraphs,
strips each marker, normalizes whitespace, and returns a Set. A condition is
accepted only if it EQUALS one of those units. Both halves are load-bearing:

- **whole**, so a fragment cannot match, which is what kills containment;
- **item rather than document**, so the check reads no section boundary at all:
  `quotableUnits` takes every list item and paragraph of the record and the
  comparison consults the whole set. That is what keeps the check free of the
  structure assumption that would have made it project-specific, and it is why
  the omission direction still cannot live in the shipped check.

Blank lines, headings and code fences terminate a unit and belong to none, so a
heading is not in the returned set. That is asserted in the registered test
rather than claimed here: `units.has('What "clean" means, defined here so it
cannot be softened later')` and `units.has("Decision")` are both asserted false,
against DR-0012's own two heading texts.

**The cost to a consuming project, stated because it is a real constraint**: a
condition must be quoted as a COMPLETE list item or paragraph. A paraphrase, or
half of a longer item, is now a violation. That is exactly what the schema's own
`$comment` already claimed ("quoted from the decision record rather than
summarized") and it is now enforced instead of asserted.

### The four classes, measured after the fix

```
--- the orchestrator exploit: six one-word conditions
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/0 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "a" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/1 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "the" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/2 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "review" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/3 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "merge" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/4 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "is" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/5 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "of" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
REAL exit=1  (violations from this check: 6)

--- the earlier probe that leaked the signal: one..six
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/0 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "one" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/1 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "two" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/2 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "three" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/3 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "four" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/4 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "five" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/5 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "six" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
REAL exit=1  (violations from this check: 6)

--- long fabricated sentences (the member that ALREADY passed before)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/0 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "fabricated condition one, unrelated to DR-0012" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/1 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "fabricated condition two, unrelated to DR-0012" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/2 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "fabricated condition three, unrelated to DR-0012" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/3 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "fabricated condition four, unrelated to DR-0012" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/4 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "fabricated condition five, unrelated to DR-0012" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/5 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "fabricated condition six, unrelated to DR-0012" (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
REAL exit=1  (violations from this check: 6)

--- HALF of a real condition, a fragment that IS contained in the record
INVALID #/modes/0/conditions/0 mode full cites DR-0012 for a condition that is not a whole quoted item of that record: "Two independent clean-room reviews exist for the current hea..." (check: mode-conditions-quote-granted-by)
REAL exit=1  (violations from this check: 1)

--- the shipped six, verbatim
REAL exit=0  (violations from this check: 0)

```

The short-string exploit, the `one..six` probe that leaked the signal, the long
fabricated sentences, and a FRAGMENT of a real condition (contained in the
record verbatim, therefore accepted by the old predicate) are all rejected, and
the shipped six still pass.

### The witness, and why the short-string arm had to be its own test

A witness member is only worth something if the test it names would go green
without the fix. **Long fabricated sentences are caught by containment too**, so
a witness resting on them stays red under a reverted predicate and says nothing
about which predicate is in force. That is the blind spot reproduced inside the
witness, which is what the orchestrator's instruction was guarding against.

So the short-string class gets its own behavior
(`mode-conditions-are-whole-quotes-not-substrings`), its own test, and its own
spec with two structurally different members:

- **member 0**: revert the predicate to containment over the joined units;
- **member 1**: keep equality but exempt short conditions with a length guard.

Both reach the same blind spot by different routes: one is the wrong predicate,
the other is a guard that carves the class out.

Measured, and this is the counterfactual that shows the arm discriminates:

```
# Applying ONLY the containment-revert member, then running the two tests separately.
containment reverted

$ node --test --test-name-pattern "conditions shorter than the record" test/assurance-modes.test.ts
#    tests 1
#    pass 0
#    fail 1

$ node --test --test-name-pattern "a delegated grant whose conditions" test/assurance-modes.test.ts
#    tests 1
#    pass 0
#    fail 1
# Under the containment revert, WHICH assertion of the fabricated-sentence test fails?
test at test/assurance-modes.test.ts:1320:1
  AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: Expected values to be strictly equal:
      at TestContext.<anonymous> (file:///tmp/claude-0/-home-user-tiphys-ai-helmsman/183bdee0-14ec-5b04-b0a8-ad41df70db46/scratchpad/wt-m3p3/test/assurance-modes.test.ts:1377:12)
```

Under the containment revert BOTH tests fail, and the second one fails at
`test/assurance-modes.test.ts:1377`, which is the FRAGMENT arm added in this
round, not the long-sentence arm it already had. So the round-1 test as it stood
would have been GREEN under the reverted predicate. The claim in the test's own
comment is therefore measured rather than asserted.

New behavior count: 23 tests, 23 behaviors, all resolving.

### The corrected statement of what is guaranteed, and by which artifact

This replaces the residual-asymmetry paragraph of round 1, which was wrong in
the direction it claimed was covered.

| Direction | Shipped check `mode-conditions-quote-granted-by` | Registered test `full carries every condition DR-0012 declares...` |
|---|---|---|
| A condition that is NOT in the record (fabrication) | **caught**, by equality against the record's quotable units. Round 1 claimed this and did not deliver it | caught, by set equality |
| A condition that is a FRAGMENT of a real one | **caught** as of this round; accepted before it | caught |
| A condition OMITTED from the six | **not caught, and not catchable in the shipped check** | caught |
| A seventh condition added to DR-0012 | not caught | caught, and with no number to edit |

**The two artifacts give different guarantees and that is now said plainly.**
The shipped check is what a CONSUMING project gets: no fabrication, no
fragments, no omission detection. The registered test is what THIS repository
gets and it is strictly stronger, because it may know DR-0012's shape, being
shipped beside it. A consuming project that wants the omission direction must
write its own test; nothing in the kernel can do it for them without assuming
the structure of a document the kernel does not own.

The reviewer's B-003 property holds for this repository's own document by the
test, and holds for a consuming project's document only in the three rows above
that say caught.

### The witness gate caught a second defect in this round's own witnesses

The first run of the red-witness gate on this round was **RED**, and not for the
fix:

```
red-witness: red (24 witness(es) evaluated (11 own, 13 stored re-evaluated); witness checks-conditions-are-whole-quotes: red: rule (g): members 0 and 1 mutate the same text of src/checks.ts and count as one member (collapse); witness checks-enum-compared-element-wise: red: rule (g): members 0 and 1 mutate the same text of src/checks.ts and count as one member (collapse))
exit=1
```

Rule (g) is "one witness is not a class" made mechanical: two mutations naming
the same `find` text are ONE member however different their replacements look.
Both of my two-member specs named the same line twice, so each was a
single-member collapse wearing two members' clothing. My own runner does not
implement rule (g), which is why it reported four reds and the gate reported
two collapses.

`checks-enum-compared-element-wise` has carried this since fix round 1 and the
round-1 gate run reported green. **I cannot explain that from the evidence I
have**, and I am not going to invent a reason: rule (g) applies only when the
witness is `classification` or the harness derives the named tests as
text-asserting, so the classification of that spec's test evidently changed
between the two runs, and I did not capture the derivation output that would say
how. What is certain is the current state, which is the run below.

Each spec now mutates two different sites that reach the same blind spot by
different routes:

- `checks-conditions-are-whole-quotes`: (0) the comparison reverted to
  containment; (1) the EXTRACTOR over-generating, adding every word of the
  record as its own quotable unit, so equality still accepts short strings. One
  is the wrong predicate, the other is a correct predicate fed a wrong universe.
- `checks-enum-compared-element-wise`: (0) the helper rewritten as a space join;
  (1) the CALL SITE bypassing the helper with a length-only comparison.

A sweep over all 43 specs confirms no other spec has colliding mutation targets:

total specs: 43
```

under DR-0016 after two fix rounds. Everything below this heading is mine; I

it marked wrong in place, because the record of a wrong turn is the artifact.

all three inside `quotableUnits()` at `src/checks.ts:810-841`, as recorded in
`delivery/review/verification-m3-p3-fix-rounds.md`. M1 (mode id uniqueness),
M2 (`mode show` validating), M3 (containment versus equality) and M4 (the NUL
bytes) are independently verified closed and I do not touch them. The criterion
1 plan discrepancy is the orchestrator's.

### Step 0: what I read before writing a line

- `CLAUDE.md`, all of it.
- `delivery/review/arbitration-m3-p3.md` (round 1's arbitration; the round-2
  arbitration for V-1/V-2/V-3 is the verification report below).
- `delivery/review/verification-m3-p3-fix-rounds.md`, the independent delta
  verification that produced V-1, V-2 and V-3.
- `delivery/work-history/m3-p3.md`, my predecessor's sections, read as a CLAIM.
- `src/checks.ts`, `test/assurance-modes.test.ts`, `test/behaviors.json`,
  `witness/checks-conditions-are-whole-quotes.json`,
  `delivery/plan/phase-declarations/m3-p3.json`.

### The MECHANISM, named before anything was edited

V-1, V-2 and V-3 are three symptoms. The mechanism is one sentence:

> `quotableUnits()` decided each line's meaning FROM THAT LINE ALONE. In
> markdown a line's meaning is a property of the block that encloses it, and no
> state was carried from one line to the next.

Read that way the three findings stop being three:

- Fence CONTENT fell through because "am I inside a fence" is the enclosing
  block, and the loop only ever looked at the marker line (V-1).
- An indented `#` was not a heading because heading-ness was tested as
  `line.startsWith("#")`, a property of the line's first byte (V-2).
- A setext heading was invisible for the same reason ONE LEVEL WORSE: it is a
  property of the block ABOVE the underline, so no one-line rule can see it at
  all. The verifier recorded this as "the same class as V-2" without numbering
  it, and it is the sharpest member of the class.
- Unit boundaries came only from blank lines and list markers, because a
  boundary is a relation between blocks (V-3).

There is also a fourth symptom that NO finding named and that the same sentence
predicts: an INDENTED code block (four spaces, no fence) is code, and its
content was extracted as prose exactly like fenced content. Measured, at
`b871500`, before any edit of mine:

```
$ node probe-units.mjs <wt-m3p3-delta at b871500>
--- indented code block ---
["Intro paragraph.","$ tiphys gates run green","After."]
--- V-1 tilde fence ---
["Intro.","~~~text Tilde fenced example sentence. ~~~"]
```

`$ tiphys gates run green` is the content of a four-space-indented code block
coming back as a quotable unit, and the tilde case shows the old predicate did
not recognise `~~~` as a fence at all, only `` ``` ``. Neither is in any
finding. Both are V-1 with a different delimiter, and a round that fixed only
the fence would have shipped them, which is the exact shape this project has
paid for and the reason a fresh implementer was dispatched.

### The fix: an explicit block-state machine, and why that shape

Asked in the dispatch: decide whether a small explicit block-state machine is
the right shape, and say why either way. It is, for three reasons, and the
third is the decisive one.

1. **The alternative was another line predicate.** Fixing V-1 alone is one
   boolean. But the same missing state is what V-2 and the setext case and the
   indented-code case each need, and each would have been a separate boolean
   with its own ordering hazard between them. Four interacting booleans IS a
   state machine, written badly.
2. **A full markdown parser is not available and not wanted.** The package has
   two runtime dependencies and this check must run on a consumer's decision
   record with no build step. The state modelled is exactly the block forms a
   prose decision record uses.
3. **THIS REPOSITORY ALREADY HAD THE ANSWER AND `quotableUnits` DID NOT USE
   IT.** `src/gates/citations.ts:566` `computeQuotedRanges` has carried fence
   state since M2-P4, tildes included, with the "a closing run must be the same
   character and at least as long" rule:

   ```
   $ sed -n '569,590p' src/gates/citations.ts
     let inFence = false;
     let fenceChar = "";
     let fenceLen = 0;
     const FENCE_RE = /^ {0,3}(`{3,}|~{3,})/;
     ...
         if (!inFence) { inFence = true; fenceChar = marker[0]; fenceLen = marker.length; }
         else if (marker[0] === fenceChar && marker.length >= fenceLen) { inFence = false; }
   ```

   The kernel already knew that fenced content is not prose; the new extractor
   did not reuse it. `quotableUnits` now applies the same rule (see
   `closesFence`), so the two agree instead of disagreeing.

What the machine models now: fenced code (content contributes nothing), indented
code (same), ATX headings at any indent, setext headings, thematic breaks, list
items with their content column, and paragraphs.

**Every ambiguity is resolved towards EXCLUDING more, and that is a decision,
not a default.** The two directions are not symmetric. Excluding a unit that
should have been quotable makes a legitimate condition fail to match, and that
is a loud diagnostic naming the condition. Admitting a unit that should not be
quotable makes a FABRICATED condition pass, silently, which is the entire hazard
`mode-conditions-quote-granted-by` exists for. So a fence opens at any indent
(CommonMark allows at most three spaces), an unclosed fence swallows the rest of
the document, `#` at any indent is a heading, and a paragraph followed by a
setext underline is discarded rather than kept.

### The comment that was stronger than its code

`src/checks.ts:825-826` said "a blank line, a heading and a code fence all END
the unit in progress and belong to none, so a condition can never match a
heading or a fence". The second half was FALSE for a fence and false for an
indented heading. This is the same failure this repository's own rules name in
two places (T-008's postscript, the red-witness rule one level up): a claim that
overstates what the code does is how the next reader stops checking. The
docstring now states what is modelled, and, in its own paragraph, what is NOT.

### Before and after, on the shapes that matter

`scratchpad/probe-units.mjs` calls `quotableUnits` directly on sixteen document
shapes and on this repository's real DR-0012. Run at `b871500` (worktree
`wt-m3p3-delta`, unmodified) and at my head, diffed:

```
$ diff probe-before.txt probe-after.txt
2c2
< ["Intro paragraph.","This looks like an example inside a code fence.","After."]
---
> ["Intro paragraph.","After."]
4c4
< ["Intro.","~~~text Tilde fenced example sentence. ~~~"]
---
> ["Intro."]
6c6
< ["Intro.","Indented fence example sentence."]
---
> ["Intro."]
8c8
< ["Intro paragraph.","$ tiphys gates run green","After."]
---
> ["Intro paragraph.","After."]
10c10
< ["# Indented Heading That Should Not Count","Some paragraph."]
---
> ["Some paragraph."]
12c12
< ["Heading Text Underlined ---","Some paragraph."]
---
> ["Some paragraph."]
14c14
< ["Heading Text Equals ===","Some paragraph."]
---
> ["Some paragraph."]
16c16
< ["Para one.","***","Para two."]
---
> ["Para one.","Para two."]
28c28
< ["Intro.","never closed still inside"]
---
> ["Intro."]
30c30
< ["Do this:","literal code in a list","Then that."]
---
> ["Do this:","Then that."]
$ echo $?
1
```

Ten shapes changed and every change removes an admitted unit. The shapes that
did NOT change are as important, and they are the ones a false-reject would
break: a wrapped list item still joins into one unit, a nested list still splits
per marker, a list item's SECOND paragraph (indented but not four columns past
its content) is still its own unit, CRLF still works, and the empty record still
yields nothing.

**The shipped artifact is byte-for-byte unaffected**, which is the regression
question that matters most, because this check governs who may merge:

```
$ node probe-units.mjs   # at b871500
--- DR-0012: 36 units ---
RESOLVES ... (all six)
$ node probe-units.mjs   # at my head
--- DR-0012: 36 units ---
RESOLVES ... (all six)
```

Same unit count, same six conditions resolving. DR-0012 carries no fence, no
indented block, no indented heading and no setext heading, measured:

```
$ grep -nP '^ {4,}\S' delivery/decisions/DR-0012-delegated-merge-authority.md ; echo "exit=$?"
exit=1
$ grep -nP '^\s+#' delivery/decisions/DR-0012-delegated-merge-authority.md ; echo "exit=$?"
exit=1
$ grep -nP '^(=+|-+)\s*$' delivery/decisions/DR-0012-delegated-merge-authority.md ; echo "exit=$?"
exit=1
$ grep -lP '^\s*(```|~~~)' delivery/decisions/*.md ; echo "exit=$?"
exit=1
```

That absence is exactly why V-1 was graded medium and not high, and it is why
both new tests have to BUILD the record shapes they test.

### V-3: NOT fixed, and the reason is measured rather than argued

The dispatch says to fix V-3 only if the fix does not weaken V-1's direction,
and to say so explicitly if the two pull against each other. **They pull against
each other, directly, and I have measured the cost rather than reasoning about
it.**

V-3 asks that adjacent non-blank lines stop merging into one unit. Splitting a
block into more units means MORE strings are quotable, which is the fail-open
direction: it is the same predicate loosening that mechanism 3 was opened to
close, arriving through the extractor instead of through the comparison.

`scratchpad/probe-v3.mjs` implements V-3's split in its simplest faithful form
(a physical line is a unit boundary), keeps the fence and heading handling, and
runs both extractors over THIS repository's real DR-0012:

```
$ node probe-v3.mjs <wt-m3p3>
units, block-state extractor as shipped in this round: 36
units, with V-3's line split applied:                  69

wrapped list items found in DR-0012: 3

  HALF: "**Never merge across a milestone boundary.** SUPERSEDED 2026-08-05 by"
    quotable as shipped this round: false
    quotable under V-3's split:     true
    whole item quotable as shipped: false

  HALF: "The round's acceptance is a witnessed red test PER READ PATH, one witness"
    quotable as shipped this round: false
    quotable under V-3's split:     true
    whole item quotable as shipped: false

  HALF: "The round goes to a FRESH implementer, with the reviews and the"
    quotable as shipped this round: false
    quotable under V-3's split:     true
    whole item quotable as shipped: false
$ echo $?
0
```

The quotable set nearly doubles, 36 to 69, and three real list items of the
governing decision record become quotable IN HALF. A fragment ending mid-sentence
at "one witness" is precisely the object the registered test
`a delegated grant whose conditions are not in the record it names is rejected
naming the condition` reddens on today (its member 2 is a fragment of a real
condition). Fixing V-3 would hand that fragment back.

**Decision: V-3 stays open, deliberately, and is the safe direction.** It is a
false-REJECT: a condition that got glued to trailing prose fails to match and
the operator sees a diagnostic naming the condition. The verifier graded it
"low, over-strict, not a security finding" and reached the same conclusion. The
shape it describes is also, in CommonMark, a LAZY CONTINUATION of the preceding
block, so the current behaviour is the markdown-correct reading and V-3's split
would be the deviation. I have left the wrapped-list-item and
list-continuation-paragraph cases in the probe file so a later reader can see
which shapes must keep working if anyone revisits this.

### The derivation, per the fix-round contract

**Mechanism:** deciding what a line of a STRUCTURED TEXT DOCUMENT means from
that line alone, with no state carried from the block that encloses it.

The dispatch asks one question directly: is `quotableUnits` the only place in
this repository that parses markdown structure line by line without block state?
It is not. Two other sites have it, both on other phases' declarations, both
REPORTED here and neither edited.
