**Classification. Three sites are the mechanism, one of them mine.**

| Site | Verdict | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `src/checks.ts` `quotableUnits` | THE MECHANISM, fixed here | the subject of V-1 to V-3 |
| `src/gates/coverage.ts:356` `extractIdRows` | THE MECHANISM, REPORTED not fixed | classifies a markdown TABLE row by `startsWith("\|")` with no fence state |
| `scripts/check-clause-map.mjs:139` `parseInventory` | THE MECHANISM, REPORTED not fixed | matches `ROW_PATTERN` per line with no fence state |
| `src/gates/citations.ts:566` `computeQuotedRanges` | NOT the mechanism | carries `inFence`/`fenceChar`/`fenceLen`; the precedent this fix now matches |
| `src/witness/spec.ts:306` | NOT the mechanism | parses a unified DIFF, whose line prefixes are positional by format |
| `src/witness/run.ts:316`, `src/gates/suite.ts:201` | NOT the mechanism | parse TAP and the pinned JSON reporter, machine formats with a pinned producer |
| `src/validate.ts:624` | NOT the mechanism | `startsWith("#")` on a JSON POINTER, not on markdown |
| `src/task.ts:431`, `src/gates/scope.ts:364`, `src/gates/run.ts:640`, `src/pool.ts` (3), `src/watcher.ts:756`, `src/commands/doctor.ts` (2), `src/teardown.ts:156`, `src/gates/citations.ts:1092,1133`, `src/witness/run.ts:229,245,649,650,1327,1675` | NOT the mechanism | split COMMAND OUTPUT or count lines; no document structure is being classified |
| `scripts/render-agent-rules-gates.mjs:194-195` | NOT the mechanism | compares two renderings line by line; equality rather than classification, read at the site (see the capture below) |
| `src/plan.ts:87` | NOT the mechanism | a comment; the file's only line-level parse is `TRAILING_GLOSS` on one string |

**The two reported instances are DEMONSTRATED, not asserted.** Both are on other
phases' declarations, so per the dispatch I probed them and did not edit them.

```
$ node -e '<call extractIdRows on a document with a real row and a FENCED example row>'
[{"id":"CR-001","cells":["CR-001","open"],"line":5},{"id":"CR-999","cells":["CR-999","addressed"],"line":10}]
```

`CR-999` is inside a `` ``` `` fence, labelled in the document as "An
ILLUSTRATION of the format, which is not a real row", and `extractIdRows`
returns it as a row. The `coverage` gate counts finding ids.

```
$ node -e '<call parseInventory on a document with a real row and a FENCED example row>'
[{"id":"R-001","phase":"M3-P1"},{"id":"R-999","phase":"M3-P9"}]
```

Same shape. `R-999` inside a fence enters the clause-map inventory. Note this
compounds with the defect the round-2 derivation already reported at
`scripts/check-clause-map.mjs:195` (a clause "discharged" because its id occurs
anywhere in the artifact, including inside a sentence saying it is NOT
implemented): the same file both admits fenced rows and discharges by
containment.

**Owners:** `src/gates/coverage.ts` is M2-P3's, `scripts/check-clause-map.mjs`
is named in my dispatch as another phase's. Neither is on
`delivery/plan/phase-declarations/m3-p3.json`. Reported to the orchestrator,
not edited.

**What this derivation did NOT cover, and the reviewer's first check is this
paragraph.**

1. **`test/` and `delivery/` were not searched.** Steps 1 and 2 are scoped to
   `src/`, `scripts/` and `bin/`. A TEST that parses markdown line by line has
   the same defect and would not appear above. This is the SAME exclusion the
   round-2 derivation made and that the independent verifier recorded as
   uncovered, so it is now two rounds old and I am naming it rather than
   inheriting it silently.
2. **YAML and JSON documents were not considered**, only markdown. The mechanism
   sentence says "structured text document", and a hand-rolled line-wise YAML
   read would be an instance. Both greps key on markdown tokens, so such a site
   would return empty and the empty would look like an absence of defects, which
   is the failure this repository has been bitten by three times.
3. **Step 2's token list is finite and I chose it.** A site that classifies
   structure using a token not in that list (a definition list, a footnote
   marker, an HTML block, a front-matter `---` delimiter) is invisible to it.
   The mitigation is that step 1 is token-free and every one of its hits was
   classified by reading, so a site would have to escape BOTH.
4. **Inside `quotableUnits` itself, four block forms are still not modelled and
   they are the same class as V-1 at a different block:** block quotes (a `> `
   line keeps its marker, so quoting it requires quoting the marker, which is
   mitigation and not exclusion), HTML blocks, tables, and link reference
   definitions. None is excluded from the quotable set. I did not close them
   because each needs its own two-member witness and none appears in any
   decision record in this repository (`grep -c '^\s*>' delivery/decisions/*.md`
   was not run as a gate, only the four shapes above were measured); they are
   named in the function's own docstring so the next reader does not have to
   rediscover them.
5. **No CI run was observed on either arm.** T-009's both-arms requirement is
   not discharged by anything below; every capture in this section is local
   execution on Node v26.6.0.

### Witnesses for this round

Two new behaviors, two new specs, four members. Every member was taken by hand
with `scratchpad/take-witness.sh`, which copies `src/checks.ts` OUT OF TREE
first and restores it on every exit path including a signal. That trap fired
during this round and is recorded below rather than tidied away.

Registrations, added to `test/behaviors.json` as the EXACT `test("...")`
literal, character for character (`npm test` cannot catch a mismatch; only
`src/gates/suite.ts` can):

- `mode-conditions-code-block-content-not-quotable` = "code block content in the
  cited record is not a quotable unit, in the fenced form and in the indented
  form"
- `mode-conditions-heading-forms-not-quotable` = "heading text in the cited
  record is not a quotable unit, for an indented ATX heading and for a setext
  heading"

**Both classes have two STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT members, and the two-member rule
is applied because CLAUDE.md binds it, not because the gate would force it.**
Noted in the dispatch and confirmed by reading the code: `src/witness/run.ts:1291`
gates the collision check on `spec.class === "classification" || derivation.textAsserting`,
so an `additive` non-text-asserting spec is exempt. Both of mine are `additive`.
They are not exempt in practice, because `test/assurance-modes.test.ts` already
contains a direct text assertion at line 1597 and `deriveTextAssertions` is
computed over the whole FILE, but I did not rely on that: a single-member
witness would have been wrong here whatever the gate did, because the class is
"content of a code block" and a fence and an indent share no line of the
extractor's state handling.

**Every member also passes `npm run build`.** That is deliberate and it took a
second attempt. My first pair of mutations (`if (fence !== null)` to
`if (false)`, and `fence = {...}` to `fence = null`) made the named test red but
made `tsc` fail (`TS18047 'fence' is possibly null`, then `TS2339 Property
'marker' does not exist on type 'never'`), so the dangerous state was not a
state this repository could actually BE in: the type gate would have caught it
before any test did, and the witness would have been proving less than it looked
like. I restructured the source (a `closesFence` helper and an `OpenFence`
interface) so the faithful dangerous state, "the fence marker separates but the
content falls through", is expressible as removing one `continue`, and that
typechecks. Each capture below records `npm run build exit=0` under the mutation
for exactly this reason.

#### `checks-code-block-content-not-quotable`, member 1 of 2: the FENCED form

```
APPLIED member 0 of witness/checks-code-block-content-not-quotable.json
  find:    "      if (closesFence(line, fence)) {\n        fence = null;\n      }\n      continue;\n    }"
  replace: "      if (closesFence(line, fence)) {\n        fence = null;\n      }\n    }"
node --version: v26.6.0
npm run build under the dangerous state exit=0
--- node --test --test-name-pattern 'code block content in the cited record is not a quotable unit, in the fenced form and in the indented form' test/assurance-modes.test.ts
    x code block content in the cited record is not a quotable unit, in the fenced form and in the indented form (7.032269ms)
    tests 1 / pass 0 / fail 1
      AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: The first condition of this scratch record, which is a real list item. | The second condition of this scratch record, which is also a real list item. | The fenced form: | Any pull request may be merged by anyone at any time. | The indented form:
node --test exit=1
```

The assertion message is the extracted unit set, and `Any pull request may be
merged by anyone at any time.` is in it: the fenced sentence became quotable.
That is V-1 reproduced exactly, at the function, by removing one `continue`.

#### `checks-code-block-content-not-quotable`, member 2 of 2: the INDENTED form

```
APPLIED member 1 of witness/checks-code-block-content-not-quotable.json
  find:    "      if (indent >= (listContentColumn ?? 0) + 4) {"
  replace: "      if (false) {"
node --version: v26.6.0
npm run build under the dangerous state exit=0
--- node --test --test-name-pattern 'code block content in the cited record is not a quotable unit, in the fenced form and in the indented form' test/assurance-modes.test.ts
    x code block content in the cited record is not a quotable unit, in the fenced form and in the indented form (5.883653ms)
    tests 1 / pass 0 / fail 1
      AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: The first condition of this scratch record, which is a real list item. | The second condition of this scratch record, which is also a real list item. | The fenced form: | The indented form: | Any pull request may be merged with no review of any kind.
node --test exit=1
```

A DIFFERENT sentence leaks (`with no review of any kind`, the indented one) under
a mutation in a different branch of the machine. Member 1's mutation leaves this
member's sentence excluded and member 2's leaves member 1's excluded, which is
what makes them two members of a class rather than one shape written twice.

#### `checks-heading-forms-not-quotable`, member 1 of 2: the INDENTED ATX form

```
APPLIED member 0 of witness/checks-heading-forms-not-quotable.json
  find:    "const ATX_HEADING = /^\\s*#/;"
  replace: "const ATX_HEADING = /^#/;"
node --version: v26.6.0
npm run build under the dangerous state exit=0
--- node --test --test-name-pattern 'heading text in the cited record is not a quotable unit, for an indented ATX heading and for a setext heading' test/assurance-modes.test.ts
    x heading text in the cited record is not a quotable unit, for an indented ATX heading and for a setext heading (6.075047ms)
    tests 1 / pass 0 / fail 1
      AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: The only condition of this scratch record, which is a real list item. | # An indented heading, which is not a condition | A paragraph under the indented heading. | A paragraph under the setext heading.
node --test exit=1
```

The mutation is LITERALLY the old predicate, `/^#/`, so this member is V-2 as
the verifier stated it, not a paraphrase of it.

#### `checks-heading-forms-not-quotable`, member 2 of 2: the SETEXT form

```
APPLIED member 1 of witness/checks-heading-forms-not-quotable.json
  find:    "      if (currentIsListItem) {"
  replace: "      if (true) {"
node --version: v26.6.0
npm run build under the dangerous state exit=0
--- node --test --test-name-pattern 'heading text in the cited record is not a quotable unit, for an indented ATX heading and for a setext heading' test/assurance-modes.test.ts
    x heading text in the cited record is not a quotable unit, for an indented ATX heading and for a setext heading (7.206684ms)
    tests 1 / pass 0 / fail 1
      AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: The only condition of this scratch record, which is a real list item. | A paragraph under the indented heading. | A setext heading, which is not a condition | A paragraph under the setext heading.
node --test exit=1
```

This member turns the DISCARD into a FLUSH, so the block above the underline
becomes a unit instead of vanishing. Note what it does NOT do: it does not stop
the underline from ending the block. That distinction is why the naive
mutation, making `SETEXT_UNDERLINE` match nothing, leaves the test GREEN, which
is the trap the dispatch names as number one: a member that lands where the
named test does not look. **RUN, not reasoned about**, because a claim about a
mutation I did not apply is exactly what the claim grep exists to catch:

```
APPLIED member 0 of /tmp/probe-setext.json in src/checks.ts
  find:    "const SETEXT_UNDERLINE = /^\\s*(?:=+|-{2,})\\s*$/;"
  replace: "const SETEXT_UNDERLINE = /^SETEXT_NEVER_MATCHES$/;"
node --version: v26.6.0
npm run build under the dangerous state exit=0
--- node --test --test-name-pattern 'heading text in the cited record is not a quotable unit, for an indented ATX heading and for a setext heading' test/assurance-modes.test.ts
    ok heading text in the cited record is not a quotable unit, for an indented ATX heading and for a setext heading (1034.537273ms)
    tests 1 / pass 1 / fail 0
node --test exit=0
```

Green, exit 0. The reason is that with the underline unrecognised it joins the
block, so the unit becomes
`A setext heading, which is not a condition -----------------------------------------`
and the test's assertion (`units.has("A setext heading, which is not a
condition") === false`) still holds. That probe is a scratch spec at
`/tmp/probe-setext.json`, not a shipped witness, and `src/checks.ts` was
restored afterwards (`grep -n 'const SETEXT_UNDERLINE' src/checks.ts` shows the
real predicate at line 800 and `git status --porcelain` lists no source file).

#### Every member reddens the NAMED test, verified with `--test-name-pattern`

Each capture above shows `tests 1`, so the pattern selected exactly one test and
the redness is that test's, not a neighbour's. The flag precedes the positional
path in every command (CLAUDE.md warning 7).

#### All four reverted, and one thing that went wrong

```
$ grep -n 'if (closesFence\|ATX_HEADING = \|if (currentIsListItem)\|if (indent >= ' src/checks.ts
792:const ATX_HEADING = /^\s*#/;
939:      if (closesFence(line, fence)) {
972:      if (currentIsListItem) {
1004:      if (indent >= (listContentColumn ?? 0) + 4) {
$ npm run build ; echo "exit=$?"
exit=0
$ node --test --test-name-pattern 'not a quotable unit' test/assurance-modes.test.ts
ok code block content in the cited record is not a quotable unit, in the fenced form and in the indented form (1067.31666ms)
ok heading text in the cited record is not a quotable unit, for an indented ATX heading and for a setext heading (1130.157843ms)
tests 2 / pass 2 / fail 0
```

**What went wrong, recorded because a work history that only records the clean
path is worth less.** The first version of `take-witness.sh` piped its output to
`head`, and `head` closing the pipe killed the script with SIGPIPE BEFORE its
restore step ran. `src/checks.ts` was left carrying `if (false) {`. I found it
because the next `npm run build` exited 2 with four TS errors, not because I
noticed the script's exit code 141. That is warning 8's family: a mutation left
in a working tree is uncommitted work silently altered. The script now restores
via a `trap ... EXIT INT TERM` and writes to files instead of pipes.

## FIX ROUND 3, SECOND ITEM: CR-004 (medium), the disclosures that never shipped

Reported separately from V-1 to V-3 because it is a different finding from a
different review contract, arrived mid-round from the orchestrator, and is a
DISCLOSURE change rather than a logic change. Nothing below alters what
validates: I ran the full suite before and after and the pass count moves only
by the three tests I added.

Read first: `delivery/decisions/DR-0019-closed-vocabulary-at-v0-1-0.md` (on
`claude/reviews-m3-p3`, not yet on my branch; read with
`git show claude/reviews-m3-p3:delivery/decisions/DR-0019-closed-vocabulary-at-v0-1-0.md`)
and the CR-004 section of `delivery/review/clean-room-m3-p3-consumer.md`.

The decision is the orchestrator's and is settled: the mode, stage and role
vocabularies ship CLOSED at v0.1.0, because widening a closed enum later is
backward compatible and closing an open one is breaking, so closed-then-open is
the reversible direction and M4's pilot is the first consumer that could say
what to open them TO. I did not open any enum, did not touch
`charter-mode-enum-matches-modes`, and did not touch the charter-shape coupling
(CR-002). All three are decided in that record and are not mine.

**What made the decision undefended, in one measurement the consumer reviewer
took and I did not repeat:** `npm pack --dry-run` ships 123 files with no
`delivery/` in them, so every disclosure written in `delivery/` is invisible to
a consumer, and source comments are stripped from the shipped `.d.ts`. So the
limit existed and was written down three times, in places the shipped package
does not carry. That measurement is the consumer reviewer's, taken at their
head; I did not re-run `npm pack`, and it is in my non-coverage list below. That is the shape of the finding: not "the limit is wrong" but "the
limit is undisclosed where it binds".

### Item 1: the schemas say what their enums do

Five `$comment` values gained a `CLOSED VOCABULARY AT v0.1.0 (DR-0019)`
paragraph, appended to the existing text rather than replacing it:

| File | Node | Vocabulary |
|---|---|---|
| `schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json` | `$defs.modeShape.properties.id` | mode ids |
| `schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json` | `$defs.stageId` | pipeline stage ids |
| `schemas/role-model-config.schema.json` | `$defs.roleBinding.properties.role` | role ids |
| `schemas/charter.schema.json` | `properties.delivery-mode` | mode ids |
| `schemas/charter.schema.json` | `properties.assurance-tier` | mode ids |

**THE WORDING WAS CONSTRAINED BY MY OWN V-1 FINDING, and this is not a
rhetorical link.** V-1 is exactly a comment claiming more than its code
(`src/checks.ts:825-826` said a condition can never match a fence, and it can).
So each new sentence says only what the enum does: "this enum rejects every
other value, so a consuming project cannot declare X of its own name at
v0.1.0". Everything beyond that (whether it will open, and when) is attributed
to DR-0019 rather than asserted as a property of the schema.

**And the claim is checked by EXECUTION, not by reading.** The registered test
below asserts both halves: that the disclosure is present, AND that a document
carrying the consumer reviewer's own three ids is really rejected. A disclosure
with no behaviour behind it would be the defect it warns about.

```
$ node --test --test-name-pattern 'the shipped schemas disclose the closed vocabulary at v0.1.0 and the enums really are closed' test/assurance-modes.test.ts
ok the shipped schemas disclose the closed vocabulary at v0.1.0 and the enums really are closed (174.445822ms)
tests 1 / pass 1 / fail 0
```

**One discrepancy I am reporting rather than fixing:** `package.json` says
`"version": "0.0.0"`, and every disclosure I wrote says `v0.1.0`, which is the
term DR-0019 uses for the first published release. The two are consistent only
if the version is bumped before publishing. Bumping it is a release decision and
is not mine; the orchestrator should either bump it or tell me to reword. I did
not silently change either side to match the other.

### Item 2: `mode show` annotates a mode that is validated but never exercised

The output gains a second line, `execution-status:`, immediately under
`mode: <id>`, so a reader who reads only the first two lines still gets it. The
`head -2` captures below are exactly that reader.

**DERIVED, NOT A HARDCODED LIST OF TWO IDS.** The dispatch asked for a
derivation if one was available; one is, from two facts that are both present in
the data and both checkable by the reader:

1. **Is this the kernel's own document?** `cmdMode` knows: `--file` absent means
   `readModes()` walked to the package root and read the shipped file. Passed to
   `renderMode` as a REQUIRED `RenderContext` parameter, not an option with a
   default, because a default would let a caller make a claim about a document
   by omission.
2. **Does the mode declare any skipped stage?** Blueprint section 8 is what
   makes this mean something: "The current proven process is the definition of
   `full`. Downgrades are declared, never improvised." A mode with a non-empty
   `skips` list is BY ITS OWN DECLARATION a downgrade of the un-downgraded
   process.

Three outcomes, and the wording of each was chosen against a specific
overclaim:

```
$ node bin/tiphys.ts mode show --mode full | head -2
mode: full
execution-status: this mode declares no skipped stage, so it is the un-downgraded process, and it is the one the tiphys project follows for its own delivery.

$ node bin/tiphys.ts mode show --mode direct-pr | head -2
mode: direct-pr
execution-status: DECLARED AND VALIDATED, NEVER EXERCISED. This mode declares 7 skipped stage(s), so it is a declared downgrade of the un-downgraded process, and no phase of the tiphys project has ever been delivered under it. Its pipeline and its gate selection are checked by validation only (DR-0019).
```

Note what the `full` line does NOT say: it does not say tiphys RUNS anything.
Nothing runs on Tiphys before M4 (settled owner decision), so "tiphys executes
full" would have been false. What is true is that the tiphys PROJECT follows
that process for its own delivery, and that is what it says.

The third outcome is the one no finding asked for and the derivation forces:

```
$ node bin/tiphys.ts mode show --mode full --file <a copy of the same document> | head -2
mode: full
execution-status: not determinable here. This is not the kernel's own assurance-modes.yaml, so nothing tiphys ships records whether any phase has been delivered under this mode (DR-0019).
```

The same mode, byte-identical content, read the two ways, and the annotation
differs. It has to: "no phase has been delivered under this mode" is a claim
about the KERNEL'S OWN delivery and is not knowable for a document a consumer
supplied. Printing the shipped document's answer for a consumer's document
would have been a new instance of the same overclaim this finding is about.

### Item 3: the escalation bounds are data, not an engine

```
$ node bin/tiphys.ts mode show --mode full | grep -n 'escalation-bounds'
escalation-bounds (data an orchestrator brief cites; nothing in this release counts fix rounds, detects recurrence, or enforces these):
```

The values are unchanged and still indented beneath it, so the disclaimer costs
the reader nothing. Every mode also ends with a standing line:

```
limits: the mode, stage and role vocabularies in the shipped schemas are this repository's own closed enums, so a document naming any other id is rejected and a consuming project cannot extend them at v0.1.0; whether to open them is an M4 question (DR-0019). This command SHOWS a declared mode: nothing in this release resolves a project into a mode, enforces one, or runs one.
```

It is on EVERY mode rather than only on the one carrying bounds, because a
consumer reading about a downgraded mode is the reader most likely to be misled
about what this release can do.

### Witnesses for CR-004: three behaviors, seven members

Registered in `test/behaviors.json` as the exact `test("...")` literal:

- `mode-show-annotates-execution-status`
- `schemas-disclose-closed-vocabulary`
- `mode-show-bounds-are-data-not-enforcement`

**"A test that would go green whatever `mode show` printed is not a guard", so
each test carries an assertion a constant string cannot satisfy.** For item 2 it
is the SKIP COUNT: the annotation must contain this mode's own number of skipped
stages, derived by the test from the printed `skips` section, so a fixed
sentence fails. The test also walks every mode the shipped document declares
rather than naming two, asserts that both arms are non-empty (so neither branch
is vacuous), and asserts the two arms DIFFER.

`witness/modes-execution-status-derived.json`, member 1 of 2: the downgrade
branch is never taken.

```
find:    "  if (mode.skips.length === 0) {"     replace: "  if (true) {"
npm run build under the dangerous state exit=0
x mode show says which mode is the un-downgraded process and which is a declared downgrade never exercised (645.69573ms)
tests 1 / pass 0 / fail 1
  AssertionError: The input did not match the regular expression /DECLARED AND VALIDATED, NEVER EXERCISED/
node --test exit=1
```

Member 2 of 2, structurally different: the kernel's-own-document distinction
collapses, so a consumer's document is annotated with the kernel's answer.

```
find:    "  if (!context.shippedDocument) {"    replace: "  if (false) {"
npm run build under the dangerous state exit=0
x mode show says which mode is the un-downgraded process and which is a declared downgrade never exercised (1421.000292ms)
tests 1 / pass 0 / fail 1
  AssertionError: The input did not match the regular expression /not determinable here/
node --test exit=1
```

`witness/schemas-closed-vocabulary-disclosed.json`, three members, and the
second one is the point: it defeats the BEHAVIOUR the disclosure describes
rather than the disclosure itself, which is the arm that stops this from
becoming another comment nobody checks.

```
member 1: strip the disclosure from schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json
  find: " CLOSED VOCABULARY AT v0.1.0 (DR-0019): these three are the tiphys kernel's OWN mode ids."   replace: ""
  npm run build exit=0 ; node --test exit=1
  AssertionError: The input did not match the regular expression /CLOSED VOCABULARY AT v0\.1\.0/

member 2: OPEN the enum the disclosure says is closed
  find: "\"enum\": [\"full\", \"direct-pr\", \"local-only\"]"
  replace: "\"enum\": [\"full\", \"direct-pr\", \"local-only\", \"standard\"]"
  npm run build exit=0 ; node --test exit=1
  AssertionError: a consumer's own mode id was accepted

member 3: strip the disclosure from the ROLE vocabulary, a different document
  find: " CLOSED VOCABULARY AT v0.1.0 (DR-0019): these six are the tiphys kernel's OWN role ids."   replace: ""
  npm run build exit=0 ; node --test exit=1
  AssertionError: The input did not match the regular expression /CLOSED VOCABULARY AT v0\.1\.0/
```

`witness/modes-bounds-are-data-not-enforcement.json`, two members:

```
member 1: the bare header returns
  find: "      \"escalation-bounds (data an orchestrator brief cites; nothing in this release counts fix rounds, detects recurrence, or enforces these):\","
  replace: "      \"escalation-bounds:\","
  npm run build exit=0 ; node --test exit=1
  AssertionError: The input did not match the regular expression /data an orchestrator brief cites/

member 2: the standing limits line is dropped
  find: "  lines.push(RELEASE_LIMITS);\n"   replace: ""
  npm run build exit=0 ; node --test exit=1
  AssertionError: no limits line for full:
```

Every one of the seven shows `tests 1`, so `--test-name-pattern` selected
exactly the named test, and every one shows `npm run build exit=0`, so each
dangerous state is a state the type gate would have let through: the test is the
only thing standing between it and a merge.

### What CR-004 did NOT cover

1. **I did not re-run `npm pack --dry-run`.** The 123-files-no-`delivery/`
   measurement is the consumer reviewer's, taken at their head, and I assert it
   only through `package.json`'s `files` list, which the new test does check
   (schemas in, nothing beginning with `delivery` present). If the `files` list
   and the actual tarball ever disagree, this test would not see it.
2. **I did not build a scratch consuming project.** The consumer lens's method
   is what found CR-001 to CR-004 and I did not repeat it; my evidence is the
   kernel repository plus a `--file` document, which is weaker in exactly the
   way the third review contract's whole existence says it is.
3. **The `.d.ts` surface is untested.** The finding notes that source comments
   are stripped from the shipped `.d.ts`; my disclosures live in schema
   `$comment`s and in `mode show` output, both of which ship, but nothing here
   asserts that a consumer reading only the TypeScript types learns the limit.
   They do not.
4. **`gate-registry.schema.json`'s `modes` item enum is the third copy of the
   mode-id list and I did not touch it**, because that file is not on this
   phase's declaration. It therefore carries no CLOSED VOCABULARY disclosure
   while the other three do. That is the same open item the hazard reviewer
   recorded (the leg with no cross-check) and it stays with the orchestrator.
5. **No CI run was observed on either arm** (T-009).

### A transliteration note on every capture in this round's sections

`node --test`'s human reporter prints a non-ASCII check glyph for a passing test
and a cross for a failing one. CLAUDE.md requires authored files to be pure
ASCII and its exemptions are BY PATH, and this path is not one of them, so those
two glyphs are rendered `ok` and `x` in every capture above. Nothing else in any
capture is altered. I found this with the check itself rather than by
remembering it: the first run of ASCII check 1 at my head named
`delivery/work-history/m3-p3.md`, at three lines, all of them mine, all of them
that glyph.

```
$ grep -nP '[^\x00-\x7F]' delivery/work-history/m3-p3.md | cut -c1-60
3025:<glyph> code block content in the cited record is not a q
3026:<glyph> heading text in the cited record is not a quotabl
3096:<glyph> the shipped schemas disclose the closed vocabular
$ grep -coP '[^\x00-\x7F]' delivery/work-history/m3-p3.md
3
```

Both checks after the fix are in the final block below.

### The one classification in the derivation table that rested on reading

The claim grep flagged the `render-agent-rules-gates.mjs` row, correctly: every
other NOT-the-mechanism verdict rests on a captured line from step 1 or step 2,
and that one rested on my having read the file. Settled with a command:

```
$ sed -n '190,200p' scripts/render-agent-rules-gates.mjs
```

```
 * rather than counted: criterion 5 requires the check to NAME the added gate,
 * and "the block differs" names nothing.
 */
function describeDrift(expected, observed) {
  const want = expected.split("\n");
  const have = observed.split("\n");
  const wantSet = new Set(want);
  const haveSet = new Set(have);
  const missing = want.filter((line) => line.trim() !== "" && !haveSet.has(line));
  const extra = have.filter((line) => line.trim() !== "" && !wantSet.has(line));
  const differences = [];
```

Both splits feed set membership between two renderings of the SAME block, one
expected and one observed. No line is assigned a kind, so there is no block
state to be missing. That is the verdict, now resting on the code rather than on
my reading of it.

## Status after fix round 3

Handed back to the orchestrator. I opened no pull request and merged nothing.

**Closed by this round, each with a two-member witness and captured red output:**

- V-2 (low), only column-zero ATX headings excluded.

  numbering, and the INDENTED CODE BLOCK case no finding named at all, which the
  mechanism predicted and the before/after probe measured.

- V-3 (low, over-strict). Fixing it nearly doubles DR-0012's quotable set and
  makes three of its real wrapped list items quotable in half, which is the
  fragment fail-open the previous round was opened to close. The two findings
  pull against each other and I chose the safe direction.

**Reported to the orchestrator, not edited, because they are on other phases'
declarations:**

  inside a code fence is extracted as a real row. Demonstrated.

## FIX ROUND 4: a list item's unit is the WHOLE item, and the DR-0019 renumber

Same fresh implementer as round 3. Two items, both from the orchestrator after
round 3 was independently verified. I opened no pull request and merged nothing.

`origin/main` was merged into this branch first (`a9ab9bd`, merge commit
`0c34d2f`), which brings the round-3 review evidence, DR-0019's record and
T-010, and which clears the inherited control byte:

```
$ grep -acP '[\x00-\x08\x0B\x0C\x0E-\x1F]' delivery/review/arbitration-m3-p1.md
0
(grep exit 1)
```

The ASCII check has been correctly reporting that file since round 3; it is gone
now for the reason predicted, the merge base moving past PR #55's fix.

### ITEM ONE (V-1, medium): my invariant was false, and that is the finding

The verifier settled what round 3 left open. A list item written as

```
1. item text
<blank>
   continuation indented to the item's content column
```

produced TWO units: the item's first paragraph, and the continuation. So the
FIRST PARAGRAPH of an item stood as a whole quotable unit while the item itself
carried more. **That is a fragment passing as a whole quote**, which is the
exact defect `mode-conditions-quote-granted-by` exists to prevent, arriving
through the extractor instead of through the comparison. It is the same shape
round 2 closed on the comparison side and the same shape round 3's own
registered fragment test guards.

**It was LIVE, unlike V-1's fenced form.** `delivery/decisions/DR-0004-elevated-permissions.md`
is written exactly that way. Measured at the round-3 head, before any edit of
mine this round:

```
$ node probe (quotableUnits on DR-0004) # round-3 head
DR-0004 units: 22
  ...
  | Confirm the default branch is main, and flip it if it is not (the repository's ...
  | gh api repos/ThomasHendrickx/tiphys-ai-helmsman --jq .default_branch gh api --method PATCH ...
  | Protect main: require a pull request before merging and require the CI check to pass. ...
  | gh api --method POST repos/ThomasHendrickx/tiphys-ai-helmsman/rulesets --input - <<'JSON' { "name": ...
  | Verify the ruleset took effect:
  | gh api repos/ThomasHendrickx/tiphys-ai-helmsman/rulesets --jq '.[].name'
  ...
```

Six of those 22 are the halves of three items. A mode citing DR-0004 could have
had a "condition" of `Verify the ruleset took effect:` and passed.

#### The invariant, and why restating it is the part that matters

Round 3 wrote, in `src/checks.ts` and again in this file: **"EVERY AMBIGUITY IS
RESOLVED TOWARDS EXCLUDING MORE."** Here it resolved towards INCLUDING, so the
sentence was false when it was written. I have done both halves of what the
orchestrator asked: made the code match for this case, AND restated the sentence
so it says what is actually true. It now reads, in the docstring:

- It governs AMBIGUOUS structure only. Where markdown is UNAMBIGUOUS the
  extractor follows markdown, and markdown really does split: a blank line
  between two top-level paragraphs, a thematic break, and two sibling list items
  each end a unit. Round 3's sentence read as universal and those three are
  counterexamples to it as written, independently of the bug.
- **It does not hold at all for the block forms that are not modelled**: block
  quotes, HTML blocks, tables and link reference definitions. Their content is
  admitted as ordinary prose, which is the INCLUDING direction. Round 3 named
  these as "not modelled" but did not say that they falsify the invariant, which
  is the precision the orchestrator asked for. Measured 2026-08-09:
  `grep -clP '^\s*>' delivery/decisions/*.md` returns nothing (exit 1), so all
  four are latent in this repository in the way V-1's fenced form was.

The general lesson is one this repository already has a written rule for, and I
broke that rule while applying it: a comment that overstates its code is how the
next reader stops checking. Round 3's whole first item was closing exactly that
sentence-versus-code gap at `src/checks.ts:825-826`, and it opened a new one in
the same function in the same round. Both halves of that sentence are checkable
in this branch's own diff: `68162b0` adds the universal invariant, and the probe
diff in this section is what falsifies it.

#### The fix, and one thing I extended beyond what was asked

The orchestrator's reading, which I agree with and did not argue against: a
continuation paragraph is CommonMark-correct as list-item content, so the bug is
that it is a SEPARATE unit, not that it is a unit. An item with two paragraphs
is one unit.

Mechanically, a blank line is no longer an unconditional unit boundary. Inside
an open list item it is DEFERRED (`blankPending`), and the next content line's
indent decides what it meant: indent 0 means the list ended and the item closes,
four or more past the item's content column means an indented code block inside
the item, anything else is another paragraph of the same item.

**I extended it to NESTED SUB-ITEMS, and that was not in the instruction, so
here is the reasoning and the evidence.** A nested list is item content in
exactly the way a continuation paragraph is, so flushing at a nested marker
leaves the parent's first paragraph standing as a whole unit while the parent
carries more. That is the same sentence as the finding with one noun changed.
Fixing the named instance and leaving the structurally identical sibling in the
same function is the failure mode that produced this round's dispatch in the
first place. It is also live, in one place, found by enumerating rather than by
guessing:

```
$ (script: every list item indented under another, across delivery/decisions/*.md)
delivery/decisions/DR-0013-schema-validator-implementation.md:27: NESTED under content column 3:    - strict mode enabled
nested list items found: 1
```

At the round-3 head that record yielded `The Draft 2020-12 Ajv implementation is
instantiated with these policies, ... casually:` as a unit, which is item 4's
first paragraph without any of its ten sub-bullets, plus each sub-bullet
separately.

**THE COST, stated rather than buried: a nested sub-item is no longer separately
quotable.** A record whose conditions are sub-bullets must quote the enclosing
item whole. That is a false-REJECT with a loud diagnostic, so it is the safe
direction, but it is a real constraint on a consuming project and it is now in
the function's docstring.

#### Before and after, on every shape

```
$ diff probe-after.txt probe-r4.txt      # round-3 head vs this head, 16 shapes
20c20
< ["First condition.","A second paragraph of the same item.","Second condition."]
---
> ["First condition. A second paragraph of the same item.","Second condition."]
24c24
< ["Top","sub one","sub two"]
---
> ["Top sub one sub two"]
$ echo $?
1
```

Two shapes changed, both towards fewer units, and nothing else moved.

**This CORRECTS a claim I made in round 3.** That section listed "a list item's
SECOND paragraph is still its own unit" among the shapes that "must keep
working". It was wrong, and it was wrong in the fail-open direction. I am
leaving the round-3 text where it is rather than editing it, because the record
of a wrong turn is the artifact; this paragraph is the correction.

The three live records, measured at this head:

```
$ node probe-r4-records.mjs <wt-m3p3>
DR-0004-elevated-permissions units: 18
DR-0012-delegated-merge-authority units: 36
DR-0013-schema-validator-implementation units: 38
RESOLVES Two independent clean-room reviews exist for the current hea
RESOLVES Neither review carries an unresolved finding at high or medi
RESOLVES Both reviewers were given the phase's acceptance criteria as
RESOLVES CI is green on the exact head being merged, not on an earlie
RESOLVES The scope audit passes: changed files are on the phase's fil
RESOLVES Where the reviews disagree, the orchestrator arbitrates with
--- DR-0004 ---
bare command block still a unit: false
item 1 first paragraph alone a unit: false
item 1 whole (paragraph + commands) a unit: true
--- DR-0013 ---
sub-bullet 'strict mode enabled' still a unit: false
parent prefix alone a unit: false
$ echo $?

```

DR-0004 goes 22 to 18, DR-0013's prefix and sub-bullets are gone, and **DR-0012
is untouched at 36 units with all six shipped conditions still resolving**,
which is the regression that would matter most since that record governs who may
merge.

#### Witness: two structurally different members, one of them the DR-0004 shape

Behavior `mode-conditions-list-item-content-belongs-to-the-item`, registered as
the exact test literal. Spec
`witness/checks-list-item-content-belongs-to-the-item.json`.

Member 1 of 2, THE DR-0004 SHAPE: the deferred blank is removed, so a blank line
is an unconditional boundary again.

```
find:    "      if (listContentColumn === null) {\n        flush();\n      } else {\n        blankPending = true;\n      }"
replace: "      flush();"
node --version: v26.6.0
npm run build under the dangerous state exit=0
x a list item's continuation paragraph and its nested sub-items are part of the item, not quotable units of their own (9.495751ms)
tests 1 / pass 0 / fail 1
  AssertionError: Run the first command, which opens this item. | tiphys gates run --registry gate-registry.yaml --mode full | The validator uses these policies: strict mode enabled all errors enabled | A flat item with no second block at all.
node --test exit=1
```

The assertion message is the extracted set: the item's first paragraph and its
continuation are two separate units, which is DR-0004's defect reproduced at the
function.

Member 2 of 2, THE NESTED SHAPE, and structurally different because it is a
MARKER line rather than a blank line that does the damage:

```
find:    "      if (listContentColumn !== null && markerIndent >= listContentColumn) {"
replace: "      if (listContentColumn !== null && markerIndent < 0) {"
node --version: v26.6.0
npm run build under the dangerous state exit=0
x a list item's continuation paragraph and its nested sub-items are part of the item, not quotable units of their own (6.711432ms)
tests 1 / pass 0 / fail 1
  AssertionError: Run the first command, which opens this item. tiphys gates run --registry gate-registry.yaml --mode full | The validator uses these policies: | strict mode enabled | all errors enabled | A flat item with no second block at all.
node --test exit=1
```

Here the DR-0004 half is correctly ONE unit and the nested half has split, which
is what makes the two members members of a class rather than one shape twice:
each leaves the other's arm intact.

**`npm run build exit=0` under both**, and getting there took a second attempt
again. My first replacement for member 2 was `if (false) {`, which reddened the
test but failed the type gate (`src/checks.ts(1037,22): error TS18047: 'item' is
possibly 'null'`), so the dangerous state was not one this repository could be
in. `markerIndent < 0` typechecks and is the faithful round-3 behaviour. That
second half is a claim, so it is measured rather than reasoned about: with the
mutation applied, the sixteen-shape probe's output is compared against the
capture taken at the ROUND-3 HEAD, and the nested-list line is identical.

```
$ diff probe-after.txt probe-mut.txt      # round-3 head vs round-4 head UNDER the mutation
20c20
< ["First condition.","A second paragraph of the same item.","Second condition."]
---
> ["First condition. A second paragraph of the same item.","Second condition."]
$ echo $?
1
```

The ONLY difference is the continuation-paragraph shape, which is member 1's
territory and is fixed independently of this branch; the nested-list line
(`["Top","sub one","sub two"]`) matches the round-3 head exactly, so the mutation
does reproduce round-3's nested behaviour. `markerIndent` is `columnWidth` of a
whitespace run and so cannot be negative, which is why the branch is dead, but
the measurement above is what settles it rather than that argument.

This is the second time in two rounds that the naive `if (false)` mutation was
not a legal state; recording it because it is now a pattern rather than an
accident.

The test also asserts against the two REAL records rather than only the scratch
fixture, so a reformatting of DR-0004 or DR-0013 into a shape the extractor
mishandles reddens it. A fixture alone cannot do that.

The orchestrator's defect, not mine to explain, but I verified the history
myself before changing fourteen citations on its say-so:

```
$ git show 719f04f --stat | grep DR-00

$ git show f775c56 --stat | grep DR-00

Confirmed: 719f04f created a DR-0019, f775c56 deleted it ("Re-attribute
citations-scope change as an orchestrator decision, not a fabricated owner DR"),
and DR-0020 has never been allocated. Same collision CLAUDE.md records for the
`A-n` scheme, one namespace over.

Fourteen citations changed, in five files, plus two inside a witness spec's
`find` strings which had to move with them or the mutation would no longer
match:

```
$ grep -rn 'DR-0019' schemas/ src/ test/ bin/ scripts/ *.yaml *.json | grep -v node_modules
(no output; grep exit 1)
```

**Nothing the disclosures SAY was changed**, only the id they cite. Verified by
reading the rendered output rather than the diff:

```
$ node bin/tiphys.ts mode show --mode direct-pr | sed -n '2p;$p'
execution-status: DECLARED AND VALIDATED, NEVER EXERCISED. This mode declares 7 skipped stage(s), so it is a declared downgrade of the un-downgraded process, and no phase of the tiphys project has ever been delivered under it. Its pipeline and its gate selection are checked by validation only (DR-0020).
limits: the mode, stage and role vocabularies in the shipped schemas are this repository's own closed enums, so a document naming any other id is rejected and a consuming project cannot extend them at v0.1.0; whether to open them is an M4 question (DR-0020). This command SHOWS a declared mode: nothing in this release resolves a project into a mode, enforces one, or runs one.
```

**ONE THING THE ORCHESTRATOR SHOULD KNOW, because I could not close it from
here.** The renumbered record is NOT on `origin/main` yet. `origin/main` is

```
$ git merge-base --is-ancestor bec92d2 origin/main && echo YES || echo NO
NO
```

So at my head the branch carries a file named DR-0019 while every citation of
mine says DR-0020. That is the orchestrator's commit to land, and it must land
before merge or the citations point at nothing. I did not rename the record
myself: a decision record is owner-reserved territory and it is not on this
phase's declaration.

### The round-4 refactor silently invalidated a round-3 witness, and the gate caught it

Recorded because it is the most useful thing that happened in this round and it
was not a thing I did on purpose.

Round 4 restructured the tail of the loop, so the line round 3's
`checks-code-block-content-not-quotable` member 2 mutates moved and changed
shape: `      if (indent >= (listContentColumn ?? 0) + 4) {` became
`    if (atBoundary && indent >= (listContentColumn ?? 0) + 4) {`. The spec still
named the old text, which now occurs ZERO times. **`npm run build`, `node --test`
and the `suite` gate were all green in that state**: nothing that runs the tests
can see a witness whose dangerous state no longer exists, so the behaviour looked
guarded and was not.

The red-witness gate saw it:

```
$ node src/gates/red-witness.ts --result <f> --evidence <d> --base origin/main --head HEAD --phase m3-p3
red-witness: red (30 witness(es) evaluated (17 own, 13 stored re-evaluated in 123816ms); witness checks-code-block-content-not-quotable: red: rule (d): declared dangerous state does not intersect the phase diff (member 1, mutation of src/checks.ts touches no line inside a changed hunk))
exit=1
```

I then swept every spec in the repository for the same shape rather than fixing
only the one the gate named, because "the gate found one" is not evidence that
there is one:

```
$ node -e '<for every witness/*.json mutation member, count occurrences of find in its file>'
PROBLEM checks-code-block-content-not-quotable.json member 1 src/checks.ts occurrences= 0
scan done
```

One, and only one. The member was repointed at the line's new text and **RE-TAKEN
from scratch**, both members, rather than assumed still valid: the captures are
below, and both still show `npm run build exit=0` and the named test red with
the correct sentence leaking in each.

```
member 1 (fenced):  find "      if (closesFence(line, fence)) {\n        fence = null;\n      }\n      continue;\n    }" -> drop the continue
  build exit=0 ; node --test exit=1 ; tests 1 / pass 0 / fail 1
  AssertionError: ... | The fenced form: | Any pull request may be merged by anyone at any time. | The indented form:

member 2 (indented): find "    if (atBoundary && indent >= (listContentColumn ?? 0) + 4) {" -> "    if (false) {"
  build exit=0 ; node --test exit=1 ; tests 1 / pass 0 / fail 1
  AssertionError: ... | The fenced form: | The indented form: | Any pull request may be merged with no review of any kind.

$ node src/gates/red-witness.ts ... --phase m3-p3
red-witness: green (30 witness(es) evaluated (17 own, 13 stored re-evaluated in 110087ms); every witness red against every declared dangerous state and green at head)
exit=0
```

**The general shape, and it is a standing hazard rather than a one-off: a witness
spec's `find` is a LITERAL COUPLING to source text, and any refactor can break it
without breaking a single test.** A green suite is not evidence that a witness
still witnesses anything. Only the red-witness gate is, which is an argument for
running it on every round rather than only before merge, and it is the fourth
time in this phase that "green" has turned out to be scoped to something narrower
than it looked (T-009's own lesson, one level down).

## FIX ROUND 5: every `flush()` site, argued one at a time (V-4)

Same fresh implementer. No pull request opened, nothing merged.

The orchestrator's framing is accepted without argument, and repeating it here
because it is the finding: **V-4 is the same defect I was dispatched to fix, in
the function I hardened, reached by an interrupter I did not consider.** Round 4
found the blank-line route and the nested-marker route and stopped there. There
are six `flush()` sites in that loop and I fixed two. That is the fix-round
contract's own failure mode, for the third time in this phase, and my
nested-sub-item extension was reaching for the mechanism and stopping one step
short of it.

So this round's DELIVERABLE IS THE ENUMERATION, not the diff. The code change
follows from it.

Starting state:

```
$ node --version
v26.6.0
$ git rev-parse HEAD
6af8e81515183602c60df93161895cc8da901f48
$ git branch --show-current
claude/m3-p3-assurance-modes
$ ls delivery/decisions/ | grep -i '0019\|0020'
DR-0020-closed-vocabulary-at-v0-1-0.md
```

The orchestrator's merge of `origin/main` landed the DR-0020 rename, so round 4's
one open dependency is closed: the record my fourteen citations name now exists.

(Written incrementally from the first minutes, T-008. This file's mtime is the
beacon.)

### 1. THE ENUMERATION, which is this round's deliverable

`flush()` is one spelling. A unit can end by any route that empties the
accumulator or emits from it, so the enumeration uses FOUR keys, not one, and
the key set is finite because a unit is only ever emitted in one place. Scoped
to the function by computing its line span rather than eyeballing it:

```
$ S=$(grep -n 'export function quotableUnits' src/checks.ts | cut -d: -f1)
$ E=$(awk -v s="$S" 'NR>s && /^}/ {print NR; exit}' src/checks.ts)
$ echo "quotableUnits spans lines $S to $E"
quotableUnits spans lines 925 to 1071

$ awk -v s="$S" -v e="$E" 'NR>=s && NR<=e' src/checks.ts | grep -n 'flush('   # KEY 1
991:        flush();
1000:      flush();
1006:      flush();
1013:        flush();
1022:      flush();
1040:      flush();
1056:      flush();
1069:  flush();

$ awk -v s="$S" -v e="$E" 'NR>=s && NR<=e' src/checks.ts | grep -n 'discard(' # KEY 2
1015:        discard();

$ awk -v s="$S" -v e="$E" 'NR>=s && NR<=e' src/checks.ts | grep -n 'current = \[\]'  # KEY 3
943:    current = [];
958:    current = [];

$ awk -v s="$S" -v e="$E" 'NR>=s && NR<=e' src/checks.ts | grep -n 'units.add'       # KEY 4
951:      units.add(unit);
```

(Line numbers are rewritten from the awk-relative output to absolute file lines;
the text of every hit is verbatim.)

**Key 3 and key 4 close the enumeration rather than extending it.** The only two
`current = []` assignments are the bodies of `flush` (943) and `discard` (958),
and the only `units.add` is inside `flush` (951). So there is no third way to end
a unit and no second way to emit one: keys 1 and 2 really are the complete site
list, and that is measured rather than assumed.

### 2. EVERY SITE, ARGUED. Nine sites, four were wrong.

| # | Line | Trigger | Must it respect list-continuation state? | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 991 | blank line | **YES.** A blank inside an item separates two paragraphs OF THE ITEM. | Already guarded (round 4). Correct. |
| 2 | 1000 | fenced code opens | **YES.** A fence indented under an item is item content; its own content is excluded by the fence state either way, but the ITEM must not end. | **WRONG. Fixed.** |
| 3 | 1006 | ATX heading | **YES.** An aside heading inside an item is item content. At column zero it genuinely ends the list. | **WRONG. Fixed.** |
| 4 | 1013 | setext underline, list arm | **YES.** Same as 3, and it is the subtler one: the underline makes the block above it a heading, and inside an item that block is the item's own text. | **WRONG. Fixed.** |
| 5 | 1015 | setext underline, `discard()` arm | **NO, and this is the one that legitimately does not.** It runs only when no list item is open, which the guard added at 1013 now makes explicit rather than incidental. A top-level paragraph followed by `===` is a heading and belongs to no unit; there is no item whose state could matter. | Correct as written, now for a stated reason. |
| 6 | 1022 | thematic break | **YES.** A rule inside an item is item content. | **WRONG. Fixed.** |
| 7 | 1040 | a new list item | **YES, and it already does.** A NESTED marker returns before reaching this line (round 4); this call runs only for a sibling or an outer item, which really does end the previous one. | Correct. |
| 8 | 1056 | a top-level line at indent 0 after a boundary | **YES, and it is the site that IMPLEMENTS the rule.** Its whole condition is `atBoundary && listContentColumn !== null && indent === 0`. | Correct. |
| 9 | 1069 | end of input | **NO.** There is no next line to be content of anything; a pending item must be emitted or it is lost. An unconditional flush here is the only correct behaviour. | Correct. |

Four sites wrong, and they are exactly V-4's four interrupters. Site 5 and site 9
are the two that legitimately flush without asking, and both are argued above
rather than left as an absence.

### 3. THE FIX: one predicate, consulted at every site that needs it

```ts
const continuesListItem = (indent: number): boolean =>
  listContentColumn !== null && indent > 0;
```

**The threshold is `indent > 0`, not `indent >= listContentColumn`, and that is a
decision rather than a shortcut.** Strict CommonMark would require
content-column indentation, so a fence at column 1 under an item whose content
starts at column 3 is formally a new block. Treating it as item content instead
keeps the unit LONGER, which is the safe direction, and it is the SAME threshold
the paragraph path (site 8) already uses to decide that a list has ended. Two
different thresholds for one question is how the next defect gets written.

Each of the four fixed sites now skips its line and leaves the item open, rather
than flushing. The interrupter's own text still belongs to no unit, exactly as at
top level, so the item's unit is its prose on both sides of the interrupter
joined. The alternative (pushing the interrupter's raw line into the unit) was
rejected: it would put ` ``` ` and `***` inside a string an author has to quote
verbatim.

Reproduced before, fixed after, all four kinds plus two controls
(`scratchpad/probe-v4.mjs`):

```
$ diff probe-v4-before.txt probe-v4-after.txt
2c2
< ["The item opens here.","and the item continues here.","A second item."]     # fence
---
> ["The item opens here. and the item continues here.","A second item."]
4c4
< ["The item opens here.","and the item continues here.","A second item."]     # ATX
---
> ["The item opens here. and the item continues here.","A second item."]
6c6
< ["The item opens here. An aside inside the item","and the item continues here.","A second item."]   # setext
---
> ["The item opens here. An aside inside the item and the item continues here.","A second item."]
8c8
< ["The item opens here.","and the item continues here.","A second item."]     # thematic break
---
> ["The item opens here. and the item continues here.","A second item."]
$ echo $?
1
```

The two CONTROLS in that probe are unchanged in the diff, which is the half that
matters: at top level a fence still ends a paragraph, and a heading at column
zero still ends a list. A "fix" that simply stopped flushing on these four line
types would have destroyed both, and the diff is what proves this one did not.

**No regression anywhere else.** The sixteen-shape probe is byte-identical to
round 4:

```
$ diff probe-r4.txt probe-r5.txt ; echo "exit=$?"
exit=0
```

and the three live records are unmoved, DR-0012 above all:

```
DR-0004-elevated-permissions units: 18
DR-0012-delegated-merge-authority units: 36
DR-0013-schema-validator-implementation units: 38
RESOLVES (all six of full's conditions)
```

### 4. WHAT THE ENUMERATION DID NOT COVER

1. **It is scoped to `quotableUnits`.** Its line span is computed, so the scope
   is exact and not a guess, but a unit-ending decision made OUTSIDE the function
   would not appear. There is none today: `quotableUnits` is the only producer of
   the set, and `modeConditionsQuoteGrantedBy` only reads it.
2. **Key 3 is `current = []`, spelled exactly.** A future edit that empties the
   accumulator another way (`current.length = 0`, `current.splice(0)`) would
   escape all four keys. The keys are literal, and that is their weakness as much
   as their strength.
3. **The keys are about ENDING a unit, not about DROPPING content.** Two `continue`
   statements (inside a fence, inside indented code) discard lines without ending
   a unit, so no key names them. They are correct today, but a defect there would
   silently shorten a unit rather than split it, and this enumeration would not
   find it.
4. **The four unmodelled block forms are untouched, by instruction.** Block
   quotes, HTML blocks, tables, link reference definitions. The docstring now
   carries the standing requirement that any future patch modelling one must
   answer BOTH questions (does its text belong to a unit; does it end the unit in
   progress or is it item content) and that a new `flush()` in this loop that
   does not consult `continuesListItem` is V-4 again.
5. **No CI run was observed on either arm** (T-009), for the fifth round running.

### 5. WITNESS: four members, one per interrupter kind

Behavior `mode-conditions-interrupters-inside-an-item-end-no-unit`, registered as
the exact test literal. Spec
`witness/checks-interrupters-inside-an-item-end-no-unit.json`.

**The record carries FOUR INDEPENDENT ITEMS, one per interrupter kind, and that
is the design that makes the members mean something.** The orchestrator's warning
was not to let a surviving guard elsewhere make a member pass. A single item
carrying all four interrupters would be held together by any one of the four
surviving guards, so three of four members would look green. With four separate
items, each member breaks assertions only IT can break, and every capture below
shows the other three items still whole:

```
member 0, FENCE       (SITE 2 guard -> if (false)) : build exit=0, node --test exit=1
  AssertionError: fence: the item is not one whole unit; got
    The fence item opens here. | and the fence item ends here. |
    The heading item opens here. and the heading item ends here. |
    The setext item opens here. An aside underlined inside the item and the setext item ends here. |
    The rule item opens here. and the rule item ends here.

member 1, ATX HEADING (SITE 3 guard -> if (false)) : build exit=0, node --test exit=1
  AssertionError: ATX heading: the item is not one whole unit; got
    The fence item opens here. and the fence item ends here. |
    The heading item opens here. | and the heading item ends here. |
    The setext item opens here. An aside underlined inside the item and the setext item ends here. |
    The rule item opens here. and the rule item ends here.

member 2, SETEXT      (SITE 4 guard -> if (false)) : build exit=0, node --test exit=1
  AssertionError: setext heading: the item is not one whole unit; got
    The fence item opens here. and the fence item ends here. |
    The heading item opens here. and the heading item ends here. |
    The setext item opens here. An aside underlined inside the item | and the setext item ends here. |
    The rule item opens here. and the rule item ends here.

member 3, THEMATIC    (SITE 5 guard -> if (false)) : build exit=0, node --test exit=1
  AssertionError: thematic break: the item is not one whole unit; got
    The fence item opens here. and the fence item ends here. |
    The heading item opens here. and the heading item ends here. |
    The setext item opens here. An aside underlined inside the item and the setext item ends here. |
    The rule item opens here. | and the rule item ends here.
```

Read the four messages together: in each one exactly one item has split and the
other three are whole, and it is a different item every time. Every member shows
`tests 1`, so `--test-name-pattern` selected exactly the named test, and every
member shows `npm run build exit=0`, so each dangerous state is one the type gate
would let through.

The test also asserts the round-3 guarantee has not been traded for the round-5
one: the fenced aside is still excluded from every unit, and the ATX and setext
asides are still not units of their own. A fix that stopped recognising these
line types would satisfy V-4 and break V-1, and those assertions are what forbid
it.

### 6. A ROUND-3 WITNESS THAT STOPPED MEANING WHAT IT SAID, found by taking it

This is the same class as round 4's moved `find`, one level up, and I found it
only because I re-took the neighbouring witnesses instead of assuming the fix was
local.

Round 3's `checks-heading-forms-not-quotable` record put its INDENTED ATX heading
directly under a list item. Once V-4 made an interrupter inside an item stop
ending the item, that heading became ITEM CONTENT rather than a top-level
heading. The witness still went red, so every gate stayed green:

```
member 0 (ATX_HEADING -> /^#/), BEFORE the record was fixed:
  AssertionError: the record's own list item is not a unit; extracted
    The only condition of this scratch record, which is a real list item. # An indented heading, which is not a condition | ...
```

Red, but on the wrong assertion: it was demonstrating "the item absorbed the
heading", not V-2's "an indented heading becomes a quotable unit". **A witness
can go on passing the gate while it has stopped witnessing the thing it names.**

Fixed by putting a top-level paragraph between the list and the heading, so the
list is closed before either heading and both arms are unambiguous, plus a new
assertion that the paragraph really is a unit, so the PREMISE is asserted instead
of assumed. Both members re-taken from scratch:

```
member 0 (indented ATX): build exit=0, node --test exit=1
  AssertionError: ... | # An indented heading, which is not a condition | ...
member 1 (setext discard -> flush): build exit=0, node --test exit=1
  AssertionError: ... | A setext heading, which is not a condition | ...
```

Each now leaks exactly the heading its member is about.

And the whole-repository sweep round 4 introduced was re-run, because one found
does not mean one exists:

```
$ node -e '<for every witness/*.json mutation member, count occurrences of its find in its file>'
mutation-kind members scanned: 97 problems: 0
```

97 members, every `find` resolving exactly once.

**That sweep would have reported 0 problems while the heading witness was
mis-meaning**, and the reason is structural rather than a matter of luck: the
round-5 failure lived in a TEST FIXTURE, and no witness `find` targets a test
file at all.

```
$ node -e '<every file targeted by a mutation-kind find, with counts>'
2       bin/tiphys.ts
2       schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json
1       schemas/role-model-config.schema.json
2       schemas/status-line.schema.json
32      src/checks.ts
2       src/cli.ts
2       src/commands/gates.ts
3       src/commands/mode.ts
1       src/commands/plan.ts
1       src/commands/status.ts
5       src/commands/validate.ts
1       src/gates/red-witness.ts
6       src/gates/run.ts
1       src/gates/schemas/witness-spec.schema.json
2       src/gates/validate.ts
6       src/modes.ts
5       src/plan.ts
5       src/status.ts
16      src/validate.ts
1       src/witness/run.ts
1       src/witness/spec.ts
distinct files targeted: 21
any target under test/ : false
```

So the two failure modes are distinct and only one of them has a mechanical
check: a `find` that no longer MATCHES is caught (round 4, and by this sweep); a
`find` that still matches a line whose MEANING changed underneath it is not.

The gate's own handling of a member is literal-text substitution end to end,
which is why:

```
$ grep -n 'find\b' src/witness/spec.ts src/witness/run.ts | grep -iv 'findings\|found\|\.find(' | head -20
src/witness/spec.ts:48:  | { kind: "mutation"; file: string; find: string; replace: string };
src/witness/spec.ts:104:  mutation: ["file", "find", "replace"],
src/witness/spec.ts:107:const MEMBER_ALL_FIELDS = ["ref", "patch", "file", "find", "replace"];
src/witness/run.ts:637: * The 1-based line numbers a find text occupies, across every occurrence,
src/witness/run.ts:641:export function findOccurrenceLines(body: string, find: string): number[] {
src/witness/run.ts:645:    const at = body.indexOf(find, from);
src/witness/run.ts:650:    const spanned = find.split("\n").length;
src/witness/run.ts:654:    from = at + Math.max(find.length, 1);
src/witness/run.ts:752:  if (!read.body.includes(member.find)) {
src/witness/run.ts:756:        `mutation find text ${JSON.stringify(member.find)} does not occur in ` +
src/witness/run.ts:760:  const lines = findOccurrenceLines(read.body, member.find);
src/witness/run.ts:761:  const mutated = read.body.split(member.find).join(member.replace);
src/witness/run.ts:1274:          const occupied = findOccurrenceLines(shown.stdout, member.find);
src/witness/run.ts:1315:            first.find === second.find
src/witness/run.ts:1367:          return path === member.file ? body.split(member.find).join(member.replace) : body;
```

Fifteen lines, the full output (the `head -20` did not truncate). Every use is a
field declaration, an `indexOf`, an `includes`, a `split`/`join`, or an equality
between two finds. Nothing reads the ASSERTION the member is supposed to redden, so a member
whose meaning has drifted is indistinguishable to the gate from one that has
not. **I did not find a mechanism in this repository that would catch it**, and I
am stating that as what I searched rather than as an absence: the search was the
two commands above, over `witness/` and over `src/witness/`. Re-taking the
witness by hand is what caught it this round.

Flagged, not built: changing this is a change to the red-witness gate, which is
on M2-P2's declaration and not on mine.

# Round 6 (fresh implementer): DR-0022 option A2, commonmark for structure, sourcepos slicing for text

- started: 2026-08-09
- branch: `claude/m3-p3-assurance-modes`, at `18c335a` when this section opened
- toolchain: node v26.6.0 (verified in every shell that ran a gate)

- this section is written incrementally (T-008); its mtime is the beacon

## Progress log

- [ ] step 0: read DR-0022, the option evidence, round-5 verification, current `quotableUnits`
- [ ] step 1: `commonmark` pinned exact as a runtime dependency, cost measured
- [ ] step 2: `quotableUnits` block loop replaced by AST structure + sourcepos slicing
- [ ] step 3: 40-shape exploit set re-derived and run

## Step 1: the dependency, measured

`commonmark` added as a RUNTIME dependency, PINNED EXACT the way `ajv` and
`yaml` are (DR-0013), with `npm install --save-exact commonmark@0.31.2`.

```
$ node --version
v26.6.0
$ npm ls commonmark --all
@tiphys/kernel@0.0.0 <worktree>
`-- commonmark@0.31.2
exit 0

$ node -e 'console.log(JSON.stringify(require("./package.json").dependencies))'
{"ajv":"8.20.0","commonmark":"0.31.2","yaml":"2.9.0"}
```

Transitive closure and cost, measured with `du -sb` and each package's own
`package.json`, not from memory:

| package | version | license | bytes | dependencies |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| commonmark | 0.31.2 | BSD-2-Clause | 673,094 | entities ~3.0.1, mdurl ~1.0.1, minimist ~1.2.8 |
| entities | 3.0.1 | BSD-2-Clause | 171,119 | none |
| mdurl | 1.0.1 | MIT | 22,039 | none |
| minimist | 1.2.8 | MIT | 54,477 | none |

- packages added: **4**; transitive dependencies: **3**; depth **2**.
- install size: **920,729 bytes (0.88 MiB)**.
- licenses: all permissive, none copyleft, compatible with the existing runtime
  surface (ajv MIT, yaml ISC).
- `@types/commonmark` is NOT installed and is not needed: the module is loaded
  through the lazy `createRequire` pattern `src/validate.ts` already uses, so it
  is never imported by specifier and `tsc -b` is clean against a local
  `CommonMarkNode` interface of six members.
- `npm run build:runtime-deps` walks `dependencies` transitively, so all four
  are copied into `dist/node_modules/` and ship.

## Step 2: what replaced the loop

`src/checks.ts` only. No new module, because the phase declaration's
`filesToTouch` carries `src/checks.ts` and does not carry a new source file, and
a scope amendment for a file that need not exist is not worth the round trip.

Deleted: `LIST_ITEM`, `FENCE`, `ATX_HEADING`, `SETEXT_UNDERLINE`,
`THEMATIC_BREAK`, `columnWidth`, `indentColumns`, `OpenFence`, `closesFence`,
and the 130-line block loop with its `flush`/`discard`/`continuesListItem`
state. Every one of them was reachable only from `quotableUnits`; verified by
`grep -n '\b<name>\b' src/checks.ts` on each before removal.

Added: `SourcePosition`, `CommonMarkNode` (six members, all structure),
`commonMarkModule()` behind `createRequire`, `NOT_QUOTABLE`, `sourceSlice`,
`paragraphsBeneath`, `collectUnits`, and a 7-line `quotableUnits`.

**`sourcepos` COLUMNS ARE CHARACTER OFFSETS, NOT DISPLAY COLUMNS, and this was
measured rather than assumed** because the library says "column" and a
display column would put every tab-indented slice three characters off. Measured
against `commonmark` 0.31.2, node v26.6.0:

```
"- item\n\n\tcontinuation with tab\n"   paragraph [[3,2],[3,22]]  slice(1)  -> "continuation with tab"
"  - a\n\n\t  b\n"                       paragraph [[3,4],[3,4]]   slice(3)  -> "b"
"-\tTab after the marker and more.\n"   paragraph [[1,3],[1,32]]  slice(2)  -> "Tab after the marker and more."
">\tquoted after tab\n"                 paragraph [[1,3],[1,18]]  slice(2)  -> "quoted after tab"
```

**The block-quote continuation marker is a real defect of naive slicing and it
was reproduced here, not taken on trust.** Measured:

```
input : "> 1. an item in a quote\n>    continued here\n"
paragraph sourcepos [[1,6],[2,19]]
sliced without stripping: "an item in a quote | >    continued here"
```

`sourceSlice` therefore strips up to `quoteDepth` markers from every
CONTINUATION line, written as a no-op when the marker is absent so that a LAZY
continuation (which carries none) is untouched. Verified: `"> quoted line
one\nlazy line two\n"` gives paragraph `[[1,3],[2,13]]` and the strip changes
nothing.

Line endings: `quotableUnits` splits on `/\r\n|\n|\r/`, the same expression
`commonmark` splits on, because `sourcepos` line numbers index the parser's own
line array. Splitting on `"\n"` alone puts every slice on the wrong line in a
lone-CR document.

## Step 3: the exploit set, re-derived

The 40 shapes were re-typed from the findings (`shapes.mjs`) rather than
imported from the probe branch, and **two oracles were rebuilt on different
derivations**:

- oracle 1, `markdown-it` 14.1.0 in its `commonmark` preset, consumed as a FLAT
  TOKEN STREAM with a nesting counter. Different library, different derivation,
  and its `inline` tokens carry the RAW SOURCE of the inline content, so this
  oracle checks the TEXT as well as the structure. **That is stronger than the
  DR-0022 report's oracle, which read inline text and therefore could not tell
  option A from option A2 at all.**
- oracle 2, `commonmark` 0.31.2's AST reading inline text. This IS option A.

```
$ node --version
v26.6.0

$ node run-set.mjs old
old: 32/40 shapes match oracle 1 (markdown-it) exactly
the two oracles agree on 40/40 shapes
  fail-open (admits a unit the oracle does not have): 8  ["S15-v4-setext-in-item",
    "S17-v5-fence-then-dedent","S18-v5-atx-then-dedent","S19-v5-thematic-then-dedent",
    "S20-v5-setext-then-dedent","S28-block-quote","S29-html-block",
    "S31-link-reference-definition"]
  over-strict only (misses a unit)                  : 0  []

$ node run-set.mjs new

the two oracles agree on 40/40 shapes
  fail-open (admits a unit the oracle does not have): 0  []
  over-strict only (misses a unit)                  : 0  []
```

The baseline reproduces the DR-0022 report exactly: same 32/40, same eight
shapes, same direction. All four previously unmodelled forms are now right:
block quote (S28), HTML block (S29), pipe table (S30, which never was a hazard),
link reference definition (S31).
