## Step 3b: THE DIFFERENTIAL FUZZ FOUND TWO DEFECTS IN THIS ROUND'S OWN CODE

Both were found by execution, neither by reading, and the second only became
visible AFTER the first was fixed. That is the load-bearing part: one fuzz run
is not a clearance, and this round would have shipped defect six and defect
seven on the evidence of a green 40/40 exploit set.

`fuzz.mjs` generates markdown from a block grammar (paragraphs with lazy
continuations at varying indent, backtick and tilde fences closed and unclosed,
ATX and setext headings, thematic breaks, tab- and space-indented code, HTML
blocks, link reference definitions, block quotes wrapping any other block,
nested lists with six marker styles including a tab-separated marker), and
counts a document only when BOTH oracles agree on it.

### Defect A: an EMPTY paragraph node keeps a `sourcepos` spanning the text the parser removed

```
seed 20260809 iterations 5000
  adjudicated (both oracles agree): 4973 of 5000
  old (round 5)  : 2055/4973  41.3%
  new (option A2): 4960/4973  99.7%
  NEW FAILURE: "[zeta]: https://example.invalid/delta\n---\n\n## iota alpha.\n\n"
      oracle: []
      actual: ["[zeta]: https://example.invalid/delta"]
```

Reduced and confirmed against the library directly:

```
input: "[zeta]: https://example.invalid/delta\n---\n"
html : "<p></p>\n<hr />"
AST  : paragraph [[1,1],[1,37]] firstChild=null
       thematic_break [[2,1],[2,3]]
```

The setext-heading start rule strips leading reference definitions from the
paragraph and then declines to make a heading because nothing is left, so the
document's own reference sweep never sees them and never advances the start
line. Fixed by `carriesProse()`, a STRUCTURE test (`firstChild !== null`), not
a text read: reading the inline text would settle the same case and would be
the first step back towards option A.

### Defect B: the start COLUMN is stale after the parser advances the start LINE

Only visible once defect A stopped masking it:

```
  new (option A2): 4969/4973  99.9%
  NEW FAILURE: "> [epsilon]: https://example.invalid/beta\ngamma alpha kappa eta delta eta. ..."
      oracle: ["gamma alpha kappa eta delta eta. ..."]
      actual: ["mma alpha kappa eta delta eta. ..."]
```

Reduced, and it has two structurally different members:

```
input: "> [eta]: https://example.invalid/theta\nepsilon eta.\n"
html : "<blockquote> <p>epsilon eta.</p> </blockquote>"
AST  : paragraph [[2,3],[2,12]]      line 2 is "epsilon eta.", 12 characters
       slice from index 2 -> "silon eta."

input: "- [a]: https://x.invalid/1\nreal text here\n"
html : "<ul> <li>real text here</li> </ul>"
AST  : paragraph [[2,3],[2,14]]      line 2 is "real text here", 14 characters
       slice from index 2 -> "al text here"
```

`sourcepos[0][0]` is advanced past the reference definitions; `sourcepos[0][1]`
is NOT. Column 3 came from `"> "` (member 1) or `"- "` (member 2) on line 1, and
line 2 shares neither prefix, because it is a LAZY continuation. Fixed by
`startOffset()`, which VERIFIES the column instead of trusting it: whatever the
column skips on the start line must itself BE a block prefix (quote markers,
indentation, at most one list marker), and when it is not, the line's own quote
markers are stripped exactly as a continuation line's are.

Note the severity direction. A truncated unit is not merely a wrong answer, it
is a string no condition can ever equal; the same defect one character further
along would silently make a FRAGMENT quotable, which is the fail-open direction
this check exists to close.

### After both fixes, four seeds

| seed | iterations | adjudicated | old (round 5) | new (A2) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 20260809 | 5000 | 4973 | 2055 (41.3%) | **4973 (100.0%)** |
| 11 | 5000 | 4973 | 2119 (42.6%) | **4973 (100.0%)** |
| 4242 | 5000 | 4965 | 2076 (41.8%) | **4965 (100.0%)** |
| 555 | 8000 | 7960 | 3297 (41.4%) | **7960 (100.0%)** |

**22,871 adjudicated documents, zero divergences.** The exploit set stayed at
40/40 and the 19 records stayed byte-identical across both fixes, re-run after
each.

The old implementation scores 41 to 43 per cent here against the DR-0022
report's 35 per cent. The grammars are not the same one (mine emits block quotes
wrapping arbitrary blocks and a tab-separated list marker; the report's emitted
a different mix), so the two numbers are not comparable and neither is quoted as
the other's confirmation. What reproduces exactly is the curated set: 32/40, the
same eight shapes.

# Round 6, completion pass (second implementer): the setext expectation, the witnesses DR-0022 orphaned, and the verification left unrun

- started: 2026-08-09, from `20ef5ae` on `claude/m3-p3-assurance-modes`
- toolchain: node v26.6.0, checked with `node --version` in every shell that ran
  a gate or a probe
- this section is written incrementally (T-008); its mtime is the beacon

**THE FIRST ROUND-6 IMPLEMENTER DIED HOLDING ITS WORK UNCOMMITTED.** The
orchestrator salvaged, committed and pushed it as `1a5b7ba`, whose message says
plainly that no gate had been run at that head and that every number in the
section above was that agent's own claim rather than a verified fact. I am a
second implementer and I completed the round: I did not rewrite its section,
because that section is the record of what it did and its incremental writing is
the reason the work survived at all. Everything below is mine, and where it
touches the dead implementer's numbers it RE-DERIVES them rather than citing
them.

Its progress log has step 6 (the live defect at `test/assurance-modes.test.ts`),
step 7 (witnesses re-taken) and step 8 (gates) unticked. Those three are exactly
what was left, and the second of them turned out to be the largest.

## 1. The one failure: the test was wrong, and what its assertion now claims

At `20ef5ae`, `node --test` was 503 tests, 502 pass, 1 fail:

```
$ node --version
v26.6.0
$ node --test test/assurance-modes.test.ts
exit=1
not ok a fence, an ATX heading, a setext underline or a thematic break inside a list
  item ends no unit, so the item stays whole
  AssertionError: setext heading: the item is not one whole unit; got
    The fence item opens here. and the fence item ends here. |
    The heading item opens here. and the heading item ends here. |
    The setext item opens here. and the setext item ends here. |
    The rule item opens here. and the rule item ends here.
```

DR-0022 predicted this one by name: a registered witness asserting an answer
both `commonmark` and `markdown-it` call wrong. The fixture item is

```
3. The setext item opens here.

   An aside underlined inside the item
   ----------------------------------

   and the setext item ends here.
```

and `WHOLE_ITEMS` demanded the item's single unit be
`"The setext item opens here. An aside underlined inside the item and the setext
item ends here."`, with the aside's text INSIDE it, twelve lines above an
assertion in the same test that the same aside is not a unit.

**MEASURED, both parsers, on that item alone** (`r6-probe/interrupters.mjs`,
node v26.6.0, `markdown-it` 14.1.0 in its `commonmark` preset and `commonmark`
0.31.2, rendered output byte-identical between the two):

```
<ol start="3">
<li>
<p>The setext item opens here.</p>
<h2>An aside underlined inside the item</h2>
<p>and the setext item ends here.</p>
</li>
</ol>

markdown-it token types:
ordered_list_open list_item_open paragraph_open inline("The setext item opens here.")
paragraph_close heading_open inline("An aside underlined inside the item") heading_close
paragraph_open inline("and the setext item ends here.") paragraph_close
list_item_close ordered_list_close

commonmark AST node types (block only):
list item paragraph heading(level=2) paragraph
```

**WHAT THE ASSERTION NOW CLAIMS**: the item's one unit is
`"The setext item opens here. and the setext item ends here."`, the two
PARAGRAPHS of the item joined across the heading. The `-----` underline still
ends nothing, which is the property this test exists for and the one round 5
added it for; what changed is that the heading's own TEXT is no longer claimed as
part of the item. That is not a new rule: it is the rule the ATX row of the same
constant encodes, applied to the other spelling of the same block type. The ATX
row has read `[ATX_ITEM_OPEN, ATX_ITEM_CLOSE]` with no aside in it since the
constant was introduced, which is settled by
`git log -L '/^const WHOLE_ITEMS/,/^];/:test/assurance-modes.test.ts'` and
recorded below in item 8. `commonmark`'s AST has one `heading` node type for both spellings, and
`src/checks.ts` names it once, in `NOT_QUOTABLE`.

Why no earlier round found it: the test WAS the specification. The hand-rolled
block loop it graded was written until this test passed, so no independent answer
existed to contradict it. A real parser is the first thing in this phase's
history that could.

The implementation was not touched. `git diff --stat src/checks.ts` is empty
across this whole pass.

## 2. The sibling arms, checked rather than assumed

The instruction was to ask whether the fence, ATX and thematic-break arms encode
a similar assumption that merely HAPPENS to agree with the parser. All three
claims of all four arms were put to oracle 1 (markdown-it) and oracle 2
(commonmark), over the whole fixture record:

```
=== each arm's three claims, judged by oracle 1 (markdown-it) ===
fence
  whole is a unit           : true   (also commonmark: true)
  opening half is a unit    : false
  interrupter text is a unit: false
ATX heading
  whole is a unit           : true   (also commonmark: true)
  opening half is a unit    : false
  interrupter text is a unit: false
setext heading (CURRENT expectation)
  whole is a unit           : false  (also commonmark: false)
  opening half is a unit    : false
  interrupter text is a unit: false
setext heading (PROPOSED expectation)
  whole is a unit           : true   (also commonmark: true)
  opening half is a unit    : false
  interrupter text is a unit: false
thematic break
  whole is a unit           : true   (also commonmark: true)
  opening half is a unit    : false
  interrupter text is a unit: false
```

and the full unit set of the fixture agrees three ways, oracle 1, oracle 2 and
`src/checks.ts`:

```
"The fence item opens here. and the fence item ends here."
"The heading item opens here. and the heading item ends here."
"The rule item opens here. and the rule item ends here."
"The setext item opens here. and the setext item ends here."
oracles agree: true
implementation matches oracle 1: true
```

So the setext row was the only wrong one. Two things are worth stating rather
than leaving to be inferred:

- **The thematic-break arm cannot express the defect at all.** `***` has no text,
  so "does the interrupter's own text join the item" is a question that arm is
  silent on. Its agreement with the parser is vacuous with respect to this
  mechanism, not evidence about it.
- **The ATX arm and the setext arm are the only two that CAN express it, and they
  disagreed with each other.** That is the shape to look for next time: within
  one table, two rows making the same kind of claim about the same block type in
  opposite directions. The fence arm is a third form, `code_block` rather than
  `heading`, and its exclusion is asserted separately and correctly by
  `assert.equal(units.has(FENCED_ASIDE), false, ...)` plus the substring check
  below it.

## 3. The test name: kept, and why

The name is
`"a fence, an ATX heading, a setext underline or a thematic break inside a list
item ends no unit, so the item stays whole"`.

It claims the interrupter ENDS NO UNIT and the item stays whole. All four arms
still assert exactly that: one item contributes one unit, and neither half of any
item is separately quotable. I did not find any wording in the name that speaks to
whether a heading's own text is prose; that is a different rule (`NOT_QUOTABLE`)
which the ATX arm asserts under this same name today, verified by reading the
name and the `WHOLE_ITEMS` rows side by side rather than by inference. So the
name still describes what the test asserts and I did not rename it.

Consequence: `test/behaviors.json` needed no edit. Verified rather than assumed,
`mode-conditions-interrupters-inside-an-item-end-no-unit` still maps character
for character to the live `test("...")` literal (line 508), and the suite gate's
behavior resolution re-checks it mechanically.

## 4. T-011: eleven dangerous states pointed into code DR-0022 deleted

This is the part the instruction flagged and it was bigger than one witness. The
round replaced the whole block loop, so every witness member anchored in it is
either broken or, worse, still matching something that no longer means what it
meant.

### THE DERIVATION, and it is the deliverable of this item

Not a grep for one function name: every dangerous state of every witness spec in
the repository, asked whether its `find` still occurs in the file it names, and
how many times (`r6-probe/witness-finds.mjs`, full output in
`r6-witness-finds.txt`, 105 lines, every state printed and not only the failures):

```
$ node witness-finds.mjs
...
BROKEN  checks-code-block-content-not-quotable[0]  src/checks.ts  find="      if (closesFence(line, fence)) {\n        fence = null;\n      }\n      continue;\n    }"
BROKEN  checks-code-block-content-not-quotable[1]  src/checks.ts  find="    if (atBoundary && indent >= (listContentColumn ?? 0) + 4) {"
BROKEN  checks-conditions-are-whole-quotes[1]     src/checks.ts  find="    if (unit !== \"\") {\n      units.add(unit);\n    }"
BROKEN  checks-heading-forms-not-quotable[0]      src/checks.ts  find="const ATX_HEADING = /^\\s*#/;"
BROKEN  checks-heading-forms-not-quotable[1]      src/checks.ts  find="      if (currentIsListItem) {"
BROKEN  checks-interrupters-inside-an-item-end-no-unit[0..3]  src/checks.ts  find="      if (continuesListItem(indentColumns(line))) {\n        /* SITE 2..5 ..."
BROKEN  checks-list-item-content-belongs-to-the-item[0]  src/checks.ts  find="      if (listContentColumn === null) {\n        flush();\n      } else {\n        blankPending = true;\n      }"
BROKEN  checks-list-item-content-belongs-to-the-item[1]  src/checks.ts  find="      if (listContentColumn !== null && markerIndent >= listContentColumn) {"

specs: 50  dangerous states: 104  no longer matching: 11
```

Eleven states across five specs. The other 93 all still match exactly once, and I
read the 22 of them that name `src/checks.ts` one by one to answer T-011's harder
half, a `find` that still matches a line whose MEANING drifted: they anchor in
`modeConditionsQuoteGrantedBy`'s comparison, the check registrations, the charter
enum comparison, the acceptance-id counting and the criterion/phase resolution,
none of which this round changed. `git diff` confirms the round's edit to
`src/checks.ts` is confined to the `quotableUnits` region and its new helpers.

### WHAT THE DERIVATION DID NOT COVER

- Witness specs only. A test can stop guarding for reasons no spec mentions, and
  nothing here looks for that.
- `find` strings that still match, where drift is judged BY READING. That is the
  case rule (d) cannot catch, and my reading of those 22 is the only evidence for
  them. I did not mutate each to prove it still reddens; that is 22 more clone-
  and-run cycles and it is the honest gap in this item.
- Files other than `src/checks.ts` were checked for existence and occurrence but
  not re-read for drift, because this round changed no other source file.
- The `patch`-kind members (five of them) are reported as `OK(patch)` by
  occurrence, not applied. A patch that no longer applies would be caught by the
  gate, not by this derivation.

### A FINDING THE RE-TAKE PRODUCED: no document I could find falsifies `NOT_QUOTABLE`

Trying to re-anchor the four interrupter members "one per interrupter kind", the
obvious mutation is to remove a type from
`const NOT_QUOTABLE = new Set(["code_block", "heading", "html_block", "thematic_break"])`.
It changes nothing. Every type in that set is a LEAF or carries only inline
children, so descending into one instead of skipping it pushes no paragraph and
adds no unit. Measured with the set emptied to `new Set<string>([])`:

```
20 decision records, old set versus empty set : 20 identical, 0 differing
40-shape exploit set, empty set               : 40/40 against oracle 1, 0 fail-open
differential fuzz, seed 20260809, 5000 docs   : 4973/4973 adjudicated, 100.0%
```

`NOT_QUOTABLE` is therefore DEFENSIVE, not load-bearing: it documents intent and
guards against a future node type that has block children, and no document
distinguishes the code with it from the code without it. This matters twice
over. It means no witness member may be anchored there, because such a member
would leave its test green and the red-witness gate would then report the
witness as no longer guarding its behavior. And it means the four-members-
one-per-interrupter-kind structure round 5 built is NO LONGER EXPRESSIBLE: the
four kinds were four lines of a hand-rolled loop and are now one set membership
test inside a library's parse.

### THE TWELVE RE-TAKEN MEMBERS

The classes are re-anchored on what the new implementation actually does: map
parser nodes to units. The mutation sites are the paragraph branch of
`collectUnits`, its non-quotable fallthrough, the paragraph push inside
`paragraphsBeneath`, that function's descent branch, its join, and the list
branch's per-item call. Every `find` was verified to occur exactly once before
being written into a spec.

Each member was TAKEN, not asserted: applied to `src/checks.ts` in the worktree,
the spec's named tests run with `--test-name-pattern` BEFORE the positional path
(standing warning 7), then the file restored by `cp` from a pristine copy taken
first. `git checkout --` was not used at any point in this pass, in any form.

```
$ node take-witness.mjs <the five specs>
=== GREEN BASELINE (no mutation) ===
  checks-code-block-content-not-quotable        exit=0 pass=1 fail=0
  checks-heading-forms-not-quotable             exit=0 pass=1 fail=0
  checks-list-item-content-belongs-to-the-item  exit=0 pass=1 fail=0
  checks-interrupters-inside-an-item-end-no-unit exit=0 pass=1 fail=0
  checks-conditions-are-whole-quotes            exit=0 pass=1 fail=0

=== checks-code-block-content-not-quotable ===
  member 0: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (paragraph branch admits code_block)
      reason: The first condition of this scratch record ... | The second condition ...
  member 1: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (fallthrough slices what it should skip)
      reason: # DR-9999: a scratch record carrying two forms of code block | ## What "clean" means | ...

=== checks-heading-forms-not-quotable ===
  member 0: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (paragraph branch admits heading)
  member 1: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (fallthrough slices what it should skip)

=== checks-list-item-content-belongs-to-the-item ===
  member 0: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (item unit truncated to its first block)
      reason: Run the first command, which opens this item. | The validator uses these policies: | A flat item ...
  member 1: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (nested sub-lists dropped from the item)
      reason: Run the first command, which opens this item. tiphys gates run --registry gate-registry.yaml --mode full | ...

=== checks-interrupters-inside-an-item-end-no-unit ===
  member 0: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (only the item's first paragraph survives)
      reason: fence: the item is not one whole unit; got The fence item opens here. | The heading item opens here. | ...
  member 1: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (a non-quotable child BREAKS the walk)
      reason: fence: the item is not one whole unit; got The fence item opens here. | ...
  member 2: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (interrupter text pushed into the unit)
      reason: fence: the item is not one whole unit; got The fence item opens here. ``` an illustrative command, not a condition ``` and the fence item ends here. | ...
  member 3: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (item's inner paragraphs also emitted alone)
      reason: fence: half the item is quotable; got The fence item opens here. | and the fence item ends here. | ...

=== checks-conditions-are-whole-quotes ===
  member 0: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (equality relaxed back to containment)
  member 1: RED exit=1 pass=0 fail=1   (word units added beside the whole unit)

ALL MEMBERS WITNESS
```

`pass=0 fail=1` matters and is why the harness reads TAP counts rather than the
exit code alone: a mutation that broke the module at load time would also exit 1,
with zero tests reported. The test RAN and FAILED in all twelve.

The four interrupter members redden with four DIFFERENT messages, which is the
evidence that they are four members and not one written four times: two report
the item split at its first paragraph, one reports the fenced aside's text leaked
INTO the unit (the opposite direction), one reports the halves standing as units
of their own.

**WHAT THIS RE-TAKE COSTS, stated plainly.** The class members no longer map one
to one onto the fixture's four interrupter kinds, because the implementation has
no per-kind guard to defang. The four kinds now live only in the FIXTURE, which
still carries all four and is exercised by every member. A reviewer who wants
per-kind coverage back will not get it from mutation members against this
implementation, and I did not pretend otherwise by writing four members that
differ only cosmetically.

## 6. The settlement item 1 promised

The claim that the ATX row of `WHOLE_ITEMS` excluded its aside from the start,
while the setext row included its own, is settled by the constant's whole
history. It has exactly one prior commit:

```
$ git log -L '/^const WHOLE_ITEMS/,/^];/:test/assurance-modes.test.ts' --format='%h %s'
2ca96c9 M3-P3 fix round 5: an interrupter inside a list item ends no unit

+const WHOLE_ITEMS: [string, string, string][] = [
+  ["fence", FENCE_ITEM_OPEN, `${FENCE_ITEM_OPEN} ${FENCE_ITEM_CLOSE}`],
+  ["ATX heading", ATX_ITEM_OPEN, `${ATX_ITEM_OPEN} ${ATX_ITEM_CLOSE}`],
+  [
+    "setext heading",
+    SETEXT_ITEM_OPEN,
+    `${SETEXT_ITEM_OPEN} ${SETEXT_ASIDE} ${SETEXT_ITEM_CLOSE}`,
+  ],
+  ["thematic break", BREAK_ITEM_OPEN, `${BREAK_ITEM_OPEN} ${BREAK_ITEM_CLOSE}`],
+];
exit=0
```

The two rows were born disagreeing, in the same commit, four lines apart. Round 5
wrote both and the loop it was grading agreed with both, because the loop was
tuned until they passed. Nothing in that arrangement can report the
contradiction, and that is the general lesson worth keeping from this pass: when
a test IS the specification, an internal inconsistency between two rows of one
table is invisible to every gate the project owns.

### CR-002 (medium): the witnesses, taken FIRST and RED against the shipped code

The arbitration ordered CR-002 before CR-001 and the order was load-bearing: a
witness taken after a fix is written to match the implementation, which is what
the red-witness rule exists to prevent. All three tests below were written and
demonstrated red BEFORE `src/checks.ts` was edited.

**The mechanism the review named, restated.** A behavior can be registered in
`test/behaviors.json` and resolve green with NO witness spec naming the code
that implements it. The registry couples a NAME to a test; the red-witness rule
couples a test to a DANGEROUS STATE; and nothing couples the second to the
first. Refusal rule (d) can only check specs that exist, so a round adding
thirty behavior rows and zero specs for its own helpers is SILENT rather than
red. That is why M04 (literally the pre-fix `startOffset`) and M01 (literally
the pre-fix `carriesProse`) both left the full suite at 501 pass, exit 0.

Three tests were added to `test/assurance-modes.test.ts`, three behavior rows to
`test/behaviors.json`, and three witness specs to `witness/`:

| behavior | witness spec | guards |
|---|---|---|
| `mode-conditions-multi-marker-lines-strip-every-marker` | `witness/checks-multi-marker-lines-strip-every-marker.json` | CR-001, both directions |
| `mode-conditions-start-column-verified-not-trusted` | `witness/checks-start-column-verified-not-trusted.json` | round 6's `startOffset` fix |
| `mode-conditions-emptied-paragraph-contributes-no-unit` | `witness/checks-emptied-paragraph-contributes-no-unit.json` | round 6's `carriesProse` fix |

**The matrix, measured.** Six mutations, each applied to a pristine copy of
`src/checks.ts` (md5 `21efa427ac503380f282addb5ee2362f`) and restored by `cp`,
never by `git checkout --` (CLAUDE.md warning 8). Each cell is one
`node --test --test-name-pattern "<name>" test/assurance-modes.test.ts` with the
pattern BEFORE the positional path (warning 7), and its OWN exit code, node
v26.6.0 verified in the shell that ran it.

```
mutation                                  test1        test2        test3
W1 SKIPPABLE_PREFIX back to one marker    exit=1 RED   exit=0       exit=0
W2 sourceSlice ignores startOffset        exit=1 RED   exit=0       exit=0
W3 startOffset trusts the column (M04)    exit=0       exit=1 RED   exit=0
W4 SKIPPABLE_PREFIX accepts anything(M20) exit=0       exit=1 RED   exit=0
W5 carriesProse returns true (M01)        exit=0       exit=0       exit=1 RED

It is a diagonal rather than a smear, which is the property that says each
witness is specific to its own mechanism. Each red cell reports `tests 1 pass 0

### CR-001 (medium): the mechanism, the derivation, and what the derivation did not cover

**1. The MECHANISM, not the finding.**

The finding is "nested list markers leak into the unit". The mechanism is:

> **ONE grammar, THREE models of it, and the guard decides between "the column
> is lying" and "the prefix is richer than my model" using the model that is
> incomplete.**

`startOffset` verifies the parser's start column rather than trusting it, which
is right. It verifies by testing the skipped span against `SKIPPABLE_PREFIX`,
which modelled quote markers plus AT MOST ONE list marker. CommonMark lets a
container prefix open any number of blocks on one line in any order, so a
CORRECT column whose prefix that model could not spell was indistinguishable
from a lying column, and both took the same recovery. The recovery consumes
`quoteDepth` quote markers, and on a line opening with a LIST marker
`quoteDepth` is 0, so it returned offset 0 and the slice was the entire raw
line. Fail-open (a fabricated condition equal to `- - x` is accepted) and
fail-closed (the real unit `x` is rejected) at once.

Widening the model by one marker would move the boundary to three markers and
leave the defect standing there. The fix makes the repetition UNBOUNDED, so
there is no boundary left to move, and the marker-depth sweep below is the
evidence for that rather than the four shapes the reviewer named.

**2. The DERIVATION, with its full output.**

Three commands. The first enumerates every call site of the mechanism; the
second asks whether any other code in the repository slices raw source by a
parser-reported position; the third enumerates every model of the grammar.

```
$ git grep -n -E 'startOffset|SKIPPABLE_PREFIX|sourceSlice' -- src bin scripts test witness
src/checks.ts:922: * a grammar is how the three models described under `SKIPPABLE_PREFIX` came to
src/checks.ts:924: * `SKIPPABLE_PREFIX`: see `startOffset`.
src/checks.ts:960:const SKIPPABLE_PREFIX = /^(?:[ \t]*(?:>[ \t]?|(?:[0-9]{1,9}[.)]|[-*+])[ \t]*))*[ \t]*$/;
src/checks.ts:996: * ENTIRE RAW LINE. Widening `SKIPPABLE_PREFIX` to the full container grammar
src/checks.ts:1018:function startOffset(text: string, startColumn: number, quoteDepth: number): number {
src/checks.ts:1020:  if (offset <= text.length && SKIPPABLE_PREFIX.test(text.slice(0, offset))) {
src/checks.ts:1034:function sourceSlice(
src/checks.ts:1043:    const from = line === startLine ? startOffset(text, startColumn, quoteDepth) : 0;
src/checks.ts:1080:          parts.push(sourceSlice(lines, child.sourcepos, depth));
src/checks.ts:1111:        ? normalizeProse(sourceSlice(lines, child.sourcepos, quoteDepth))
test/assurance-modes.test.ts:2543: * `startOffset` to trust the parser's column, and reverting `carriesProse` to
test/assurance-modes.test.ts:2587:     `startOffset` verifies the parser's start column instead of trusting it,
test/assurance-modes.test.ts:2722:     reverted `startOffset` to the pre-fix `if (offset <= text.length)` and the
witness/checks-code-block-content-not-quotable.json:19:      (a sourceSlice reference inside a mutation replacement)
witness/checks-heading-forms-not-quotable.json:19:      (a sourceSlice reference inside a mutation replacement)
witness/checks-interrupters-inside-an-item-end-no-unit.json:24,25: (likewise)

```

Call sites, read off that output: `startOffset` has exactly ONE caller
(src/checks.ts:1043, inside `sourceSlice`), and `sourceSlice` has exactly TWO
(src/checks.ts:1080 in `paragraphsBeneath`, src/checks.ts:1111 in
`collectUnits`). Nothing outside `src/checks.ts` calls either; the witness
occurrences are text inside mutation replacements, not call sites.

```
$ git grep -n 'sourcepos' -- src bin scripts test
src/checks.ts:806:  readonly sourcepos: SourcePosition;
src/checks.ts:847,878,901: (docstrings)
src/checks.ts:983:          parts.push(sourceSlice(lines, child.sourcepos, depth));
src/checks.ts:1014:        ? normalizeProse(sourceSlice(lines, child.sourcepos, quoteDepth))
src/checks.ts:1109,1117,1143: (docstrings)
grep exit=0
```

(line numbers as at `218fc12`, before this round's edit). No other module reads
`sourcepos`, so `sourceSlice` is the only raw-source slicer in the kernel and
the mechanism has no second home.

```
$ git grep -nE '\[-\*\+\]|>\[ \\t\]|0-9\]\{1,9\}' -- src bin scripts test
src/checks.ts:895:const SKIPPABLE_PREFIX = /^(?:[ \t]*>[ \t]?)*[ \t]*(?:(?:[0-9]{1,9}[.)]|[-*+])[ \t]*)?$/;
src/checks.ts:932:    const marker = /^[ \t]*>[ \t]?/.exec(text.slice(consumed));
src/checks.ts:955:        piece = piece.replace(/^[ \t]*>[ \t]?/, "");
grep exit=0
```

THREE models of one grammar, all in one file: the verifier at 895, the recovery
strip at 932, the continuation strip at 955. The last two were byte-identical
copies of each other, which is how a grammar comes to disagree with itself, so
they are now one named constant `QUOTE_MARKER`.

**3. What the derivation did NOT cover.**

- The three greps are over `src`, `bin`, `scripts`, `test` and `witness` at the
  repository root. They would miss a consumer that reached this code through the
  published package rather than through the source tree, and they would miss any
  raw-source slicing done by a DIFFERENT mechanism (an offset computed without
  the words `sourcepos`, `startOffset` or `sourceSlice`). I read `src/checks.ts`
  end to end to bound that second risk and found no other slicer, which is
  reading rather than a command, and it is stated as such.
- `delivery/**` is excluded from the greps above on purpose (it is paperwork,
  not code); the first grep run over the whole tree returns four extra hits, all
  in `delivery/work-history/m3-p3.md`, all prose.
- **Only `SKIPPABLE_PREFIX` was widened.** I did NOT change the two recovery
  strips, and the reasoning plus its measurement are in `startOffset`'s
  docstring: they consume quote markers bounded by `quoteDepth`, which is known
  structure, and an unbounded grammar-shaped strip there would have no such
  bound. An instrumented copy over the 6,000-document fuzz entered that fallback
  1,463 times with ZERO marker-carrying lines. **I did not find a way to force
  that arm with a marker-carrying line. That is a statement about my search and
  not a proof that no such input exists**, and the next reader should try. I
  chose not to widen it because a change no probe I could build reddens is a
  change no witness could guard, which is the thing CR-002 was raised about.
- The `commonmark` parser itself is the oracle's source, so anywhere
  `commonmark` 0.31.2 is itself wrong is invisible to every probe here. I did
  not run a second parser as an independent oracle this round; round 6 did.

**4. The change.**

```
-const SKIPPABLE_PREFIX = /^(?:[ \t]*>[ \t]?)*[ \t]*(?:(?:[0-9]{1,9}[.)]|[-*+])[ \t]*)?$/;
+const QUOTE_MARKER = /^[ \t]*>[ \t]?/;
+const SKIPPABLE_PREFIX = /^(?:[ \t]*(?:>[ \t]?|(?:[0-9]{1,9}[.)]|[-*+])[ \t]*))*[ \t]*$/;
```

plus the two recovery strips now using `QUOTE_MARKER`, and the docstrings.

**Why widening does not weaken the guard, argued rather than tabulated.** The
predicate is ANCHORED AT BOTH ENDS, so acceptance means every character of the
skipped span is marker-or-indentation. Such a span can contain no content of any
line, so skipping it is right whichever line the column came from. What markers
may repeat does not touch that argument. The measurement agrees: the four
column-is-lying spans the guard exists to reject ("ep", "re", "alp", "sil") are
still rejected, because a prose fragment contains characters no alternative can
consume.

**5. Effect, measured at the applied fix, each with its own exit code.**

- 32-shape probe (15 multi-marker members, 10 controls, 4 column-is-lying
  hazards, 3 line-ending shapes) against the project's `commonmark` oracle:
  **17/32 before, 32/32 after**, `EXIT=0`.
- 20-case adversarial set: **11/20 before, 19/20 after**, `EXIT=1`. The marker
  depth sweep is green at one, two, three, four, five, six and EIGHT markers,
  and on `> - > - > -`, which is what distinguishes a completed grammar from a
  boundary moved by one. The single remaining divergence is A18,
  `- \- escaped dash text`: the implementation yields `\- escaped dash text` and
  my inline-text oracle yields `- escaped dash text`. That is the A2-versus-A
  distinction DR-0022 settled, not a defect: A2 keeps the bytes AS WRITTEN, so a
  backslash escape stays in the unit, and my oracle reads inline text (option A)
  by construction. It fails identically before and after the fix. My generator is
  markup-free precisely so this cannot happen; A18 was hand-written and carries
  markup, and it is kept because it marks the oracle's boundary.
- 6,000-document differential fuzz, seed 20260809, markup-free grammar:
  **3,035 divergences before, 0 after**, `EXIT=0` after.

### CR-003 (low): docstrings, and the two witness specs

The round-6 claim is CONFIRMED and the reviewer's reasoning is stronger than
"no test covers it": all four `NOT_QUOTABLE` types are AST LEAVES under
`commonmark` 0.31.2, and both walkers emit a unit only for a `paragraph` child,
so emptying the set cannot change an answer. Per the arbitration the set is KEPT
as declared intent and the prose is corrected. Three docstrings changed:

- `NOT_QUOTABLE`'s own, which now opens by saying it is declared intent and not
  the mechanism, carries the measured child lists, and states the true sentence:
  these types cannot contribute a unit under this parser whether or not they
  appear here, and the set exists so a parser change cannot make them.
- `quotableUnits`' "HTML block: excluded, like any other non-prose block", which
  asserted the set performed the exclusion.
- `paragraphsBeneath`' "skipped by `NOT_QUOTABLE` without ending anything",
  same claim.

**The secondary observation, corrected in one direction and acted on in the
other.** The reviewer wrote that
`witness/checks-heading-forms-not-quotable.json` and
`witness/checks-code-block-content-not-quotable.json` declare BYTE-IDENTICAL
`dangerousStates` arrays. Measured, they do not: member 0 differs (`heading`
versus `code_block`). It is member 1 that was byte-identical, a generic "leak
every non-paragraph block" mutation, so each spec's second member proved the
shared branch and nothing type-specific.

```
$ diff <(jq -S .dangerousStates witness/checks-heading-forms-not-quotable.json) \
       <(jq -S .dangerousStates witness/checks-code-block-content-not-quotable.json)
6c6   (member 0 replace: "heading" versus "code_block")
DIFF_EXIT=1

Member 1 of each is now type-specific: it leaks ONLY that spec's own block type
through the `else` arm. Both rewritten members were re-measured and still

```
checks-heading-forms-not-quotable   member 0 exit=1  pass 0 fail 1
checks-heading-forms-not-quotable   member 1 exit=1  pass 0 fail 1
checks-code-block-content-not-quotable member 0 exit=1  pass 0 fail 1
checks-code-block-content-not-quotable member 1 exit=1  pass 0 fail 1
```

What this does NOT do: it does not make the two behaviours provable APART at the
level of the source, because CR-003's own finding is that this code treats
headings and code blocks by exactly the same mechanism. The members are now
distinct and each names its own type; they are not evidence of two independent
implementations, because there are not two.

### The MECHANISM, named before anything else (fix-round contract item 1)

**Deciding "is this span a block prefix" with an ANCHORED PATTERN CONTAINING
ADJACENT OPTIONAL-WHITESPACE QUANTIFIERS.** Not "the regex was slow", and not
"one particular alternation was ambiguous".

The instance V-1 names is real: round 7's pattern let an iteration's leading
`[ \t]*` and the trailing `[ \t]*` inside two of its three branches consume the
SAME run of whitespace, so every gap between markers doubled the search space
and a FAILING subject had to exhaust all of it. But the defect is not that one
pair. It is that the decision was expressed as a backtracking match at all, over
a grammar whose separators are optional whitespace. Any later widening of that
grammar re-creates the ambiguity somewhere else, which is precisely the shape
this phase keeps paying for: round 7 widened the pattern to close CR-001 and
shipped a HIGH while doing it.

So the fix is not a better pattern. The predicate is now a LINEAR SCAN
(`isSkippablePrefix`, built on `quoteMarkerLength` and `listMarkerLength`).
Each iteration consumes at least one character and never revisits one, so
acceptance and rejection cost the same and no widening of the grammar can
reintroduce ambiguity. That was the orchestrator's recommendation; I derived it
independently, agree with it, and record the alternative I rejected:

CLAIM-GREP HIT, settled here rather than left as an assertion. "Never revisits
one" is a property of the code, so it is read from the code AND measured. Read:
the loop's only two continuing branches are guarded by `quote > 0` and
`list > 0`, so each `continue` strictly increases `at`, and the third branch
returns. Measured, which is the part that is not an argument, at 100 markers of
each family:

```
$ node cost-after.mjs
bullet   m= 100 bytes=  439     1.180 ms units=1
ordered  m= 100 bytes=  739     1.504 ms units=1
EXIT=0
```

Flat at five times the witness documents' length, where round 7's pattern
doubles per marker. What I did NOT do is prove the bound formally or measure
past 739 bytes.

**A disambiguated pattern was considered and REJECTED WITH A MEASUREMENT.** A
tidier "flat alternation" form,

```
/^(?:[ \t]*(?:>|[-*+]|[0-9]{1,9}[.)])[ \t]*)*[ \t]*$/
```

accepts EXACTLY the same language and reads as an obvious simplification of
round 7's. Measured through `quotableUnits` on this round's own two witness
documents it takes **12,147 ms and 11,280 ms**. It is the same class in a
different arrangement, it is just as catastrophic, and a reviewer eyeballing it
would see nothing wrong. It is now dangerous-state member `[1]` of the new
witness for exactly that reason: the class has to be guarded, not the instance.

### The DERIVATION: every site of the mechanism (fix-round contract item 2)

The mechanism is "a regular expression used to decide or transform a span".
Enumerated, with FULL output, not a summary.

**A. Every regex operation left in `src/checks.ts` after the change.**

```
$ grep -nE '\.(test|exec|match|matchAll|search|split|replace|replaceAll)\(\s*/' src/checks.ts
774:  return text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
1385:  const lines = text.split(/\r\n|\n|\r/);
A_EXIT=0
```

Two, and both are linear: `\s+` under `.replace` has one quantifier and no
adjacent optional group, and the line split is a three-way alternation of
literals. **The prefix decision no longer appears in this list at all**, which
is the point: it is not a regex any more.

**B. The identifiers the old regex constants were reached through, so no call
site is left pointing at a removed object.**

```
$ grep -n 'SKIPPABLE_PREFIX\|QUOTE_MARKER' src/checks.ts
1133: * ENTIRE RAW LINE. Widening `SKIPPABLE_PREFIX` to the full container grammar
```
(one hit, and it is prose inside a docstring, since corrected)

```
$ git grep -n 'SKIPPABLE_PREFIX\|QUOTE_MARKER' -- . | grep -v '^delivery/'
witness/checks-multi-marker-lines-strip-every-marker.json:12,13
witness/checks-start-column-verified-not-trusted.json:12,18,19
```
Those five hits were the witness specs still naming the removed constants; all
three specs are rewritten below, and re-running the same command after the
rewrite returns NOTHING outside `delivery/`.

**C. The same mechanism ACROSS THE WHOLE PACKAGE, because a per-file search is
the scope error this project has been bitten by three times.** Every regex
literal carrying a quantified group anywhere under `src/` and `bin/`:

```
$ grep -rnE '/[^/ ]*\((\?:)?[^/]*\)[*+?]' src/ bin/ --include=*.ts
src/gates/citations.ts:453:const CITATION_SOURCE = String.raw`(?<![\p{L}\p{N}_./:\\-])([A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_./-]*\.(?:${CITATION_EXTENSIONS})):(\d+)(?:-(\d+))?(?:@sha256:([0-9a-zA-Z]+))?`;
src/witness/run.ts:335:    const match = /^\s*(not )?ok\s+\d+\s+-\s+(.*)$/.exec(line);
src/witness/run.ts:359:const STRING_LITERAL = /(["'`])((?:(?!\1)[^\\]|\\.)*)\1/g;
src/witness/run.ts:385:  /(?:const|let|var)\s+([A-Za-z_$][\w$]*)\s*=\s*(["'`])((?:(?!\2)[^\\]|\\.)*)\2\s*;?/g;
src/witness/run.ts:542:      const stringLiteral = /^\s*(["'`])((?:(?!\1)[^\\]|\\.)*)\1/.exec(rest);
src/commands/doctor.ts:78:  const match = /^>=\s*(\d+)(?:\.(\d+))?(?:\.(\d+))?$/.exec(range.trim());
C_EXIT=0
```

Six sites, all outside this phase's files-to-touch list, and I read each one
rather than assuming. The three `witness/run.ts` string-literal patterns
alternate `(?!\1)[^\\]` against `\\.`, which are DISJOINT on the first
character, so there is no ambiguity to explore. `doctor.ts` separates its `\d+`
groups with a literal `.`, likewise disjoint. `citations.ts` has one genuinely
ambiguous star (`[A-Za-z0-9_./-]*\.` where the class contains `.`), which is
polynomial rather than exponential because there is a single star and no nesting.
**I did not TIME any of the six.** That is stated as non-coverage below rather
than presented as a clearance, and none of them is on this phase's declaration,
so acting on them here would be out of scope.

### What the derivation did NOT cover (fix-round contract item 3, READ FIRST)

T-012's lesson is to state the AXES, not only the gaps, so the axes come first.

**Axis 1, CORRECTNESS: covered, by exhaustive enumeration.** See below.
**Axis 2, COST: covered, on the near-miss arm, at two marker families.**
**Axis 3, FAILURE MODE: covered only for the arm this round touched.** I did not
re-examine what happens when the fallback path itself is entered, beyond
confirming the existing tests for it stay green and its two witness members
still redden it.

The gaps:

1. **CI.** Every number here is LOCAL, node v26.6.0 from the scratch prefix, in
   this container. I observed no GitHub Actions run: neither the `pull_request`
   arm nor the post-merge `push` arm. T-009 requires both separately and nothing
   local discharges either.
2. **The six quantified-group sites outside `src/checks.ts` were READ, NOT
   TIMED.** I argued each is unambiguous or polynomial from its structure. That
   is an argument, not a measurement, and an argument of exactly the kind T-012
   says to distrust. They are out of this phase's scope, so the honest form is:
   unmeasured, and named here so a later reader knows where to look.
3. **The scan's cost was measured on the near-miss arm and on accepting spans,
   not on adversarial input designed against the SCAN.** A scan cannot backtrack,
   so the class is closed by construction rather than by measurement; what I did
   not do is search for a different pathological class (memory, `commonmark`'s
   own parse time on deep nesting, the per-record cache).
4. **`commonmark` itself.** My documents put the marker run in CONTINUATION
   text, where no nesting limit applies. I did not measure `commonmark`'s cost
   on deep real nesting, and a document that is expensive to PARSE would not be
   caught by anything here.
5. **Everything in the phase that is not `quotableUnits` and its helpers.**
   `src/modes.ts`, `src/commands/mode.ts`, the schemas, the CLI wiring: round 8
   does not touch them and I did not review them.
6. **The eleven accepted survivors (M03, M06, M07, M09, M10, M11, M12, M13,
   M16, M18, M19).** Decided out of scope by the arbitration. I did not
   re-measure them and this round makes no claim about them.
7. **`src/checks.ts` under a JavaScript engine other than V8.** The exponential
   arm and its absence are both engine behaviour. The scan is engine-independent
   by construction, but "the old pattern was catastrophic" is a V8 measurement.
8. **Process.** I did not verify anything about how earlier rounds were run,
   only that the artifacts I cite say what I say they say.

### V-1 (HIGH): the fix, and the TIME WITNESS

**Axis 1, CORRECTNESS. The language is UNCHANGED, enumerated rather than
argued.** The scan is compared against the round-7 pattern over an alphabet of
the characters that can appear in a prefix, plus one that cannot:

```
alphabet = [space tab > - * + 1 2 . ) x]
maxlen 6:  1,948,717 strings,   304 ms,  MISMATCHES=0,  EQUIV_EXIT=0
maxlen 7: 21,435,888 strings, 3,315 ms,  MISMATCHES=0,  EQUIV_EXIT=0
```

Length 7 cannot reach a ten-digit run (that needs eleven characters), which is
the one place the `[0-9]{1,9}` cap could differ. CLAIM-GREP HIT ("is covered"),
and what settles it is the captured run immediately below rather than the
sentence: the boundary set names that region's cases explicitly and the two
randomised sweeps exit 0.

```
boundary cases=40 mismatches=0
   (including "123456789.", "1234567890.", "12345678901.", "0000000000.",
    "1234567890) ", "> 1234567890. ", "", " ", "\t", ">>", "> >", "-x")
random lengths 8..20      checked=400000  accepted-by-both=1207    mismatches=0
biased marker-shaped      checked=400000  accepted-by-both=192674  mismatches=0
EQUIVLONG_EXIT=0
```

The biased generator matters: 192,674 of its spans are ACCEPTED by both, so the
comparison is not dominated by trivial rejections.

**Axis 2, COST. Both implementations on the SAME documents.** `986f58a`'s

Round 7 doubles per added marker in BOTH families; round 8 is flat and
sub-millisecond, over documents from 71 to 207 bytes. **`identical-units=true`
on every single row**, and the probe exits nonzero the moment it is not, which
is why no equality assertion anywhere in this repository could have seen V-1.
Flatness holds well past the witness parameters: at 439 and 739 bytes (100
markers of each family) the scan is 1.180 ms and 1.504 ms.

**The TIME WITNESS.** `test/assurance-modes.test.ts`, test name

> a long near-miss block prefix is rejected in bounded time, for a bullet run
> and for an ordered run

It builds a two-line document whose start-column verification is handed a span
that parses as markers until its very last character, times `quotableUnits`
through the same entry point the CLI uses, and asserts `elapsed < 1000 ms`. It
then asserts the unit set, so a "fix" that is fast because it stopped working
cannot pass. Two structurally different members, a bullet run and an ordered
run.

**RED against `986f58a`, which is the load-bearing demonstration.** The
dangerous state is the round-7 pattern restored verbatim, applied to
`src/checks.ts` and the whole file re-run:

```
MUTANT=R7 applied; md5 now ba50c461bc4dcc0772ebf690281c2e64
MUTANT=R7 scope=file EXIT=1
i tests 34  i pass 33  i fail 1  i skipped 0
x a long near-miss block prefix is rejected in bounded time, for a bullet run
  and for an ordered run (11396.869945ms)
RESTORED md5 9697035ed2073c1ee165b5d1e0107cc5 (pristine 9697035ed2073c1ee165b5d1e0107cc5)
```

**One test red out of thirty-four, and it is this one.** The witness is not
merely red, it is SPECIFIC: restoring the exact defect V-1 reported reddens
exactly the test written for it and nothing else.

Member `[1]`, the flat alternation, same treatment:

```
MUTANT=FLAT applied; md5 now 16499fce59519e7530b23ac49a6ae015
MUTANT=FLAT scope=file EXIT=1
i tests 34  i pass 33  i fail 1  i skipped 0
x a long near-miss block prefix ... (12022.736529ms)
RESTORED md5 9697035ed2073c1ee165b5d1e0107cc5
```

**GREEN after the fix:** `node --test --test-name-pattern 'near-miss' ...` runs
it in **3.8 ms for both shapes**, and in the full built suite the file is green.
So the assertion sits about 1,400 times above the honest cost and about eleven
times below the pathological one. Breaking the green arm would take a runner
roughly 1,400 times SLOWER than this container; breaking the red arm would take
one roughly eleven times FASTER, and no runner is eleven times faster than an
exponential. The pathological arm also grows with the marker count while the
honest arm does not, so deepening the fixture later widens the gap rather than
narrowing it.

**A third candidate member was measured and REJECTED, recorded because the
reason is not obvious.** A nested-quantifier form,
`/^(?:(?:>|[-*+]|[0-9]{1,9}[.)]|[ \t])+)*$/`, is the textbook `(a+)*` shape and
is catastrophic here too. CLAIM-GREP HIT ("cannot be demonstrated red"), and the
honest form is a statement about my run rather than about the world: I started
it against `test/assurance-modes.test.ts` and it had NOT returned when my
harness killed it after roughly nine minutes, so I do not know what it would
cost, only that it exceeds nine minutes here.

```
================= NESTED =================
MUTANT=NESTED applied; md5 now df3c3013cb3be62823fabfaa9d9e0a88
(no completion line: killed by the harness at ~9 min, no suite summary emitted)
```

The rule I applied is definitional rather than empirical: the red-witness
contract requires a member to be DEMONSTRATED red, and a run that does not
finish demonstrates nothing either way, so I did not adopt it as a member. That
kill also left the mutant in the tree, which is how the md5-after-restore
discipline below came to be enforced by a shell trap.

"at two, three and five markers" and the fixture carries exactly that.

The claim is measured in both directions. A `{0,3}`-bounded model, which is the

MUTANT=BOUNDED3 | i tests 34  pass 33  fail 1
   x a line opening more than one block marker leaves no marker in the unit,
     at two, three and five markers and with a quote after a list marker (6.4 ms)
MUTANT=BOUNDED4 | i tests 34  pass 33  fail 1
   x (the same test) (27.3 ms)

### V-6 (LOW): the shared `QUOTE_MARKER` object is REMOVED, not guarded

The arbitration offered two options, add a witness or state why the risk is
accepted. I took neither, and the reason is a derivation rather than a
preference, so it is written out.

**I DID NOT FIND A WAY to build a behavioural witness for the `g`-flag hazard,
and that sentence is deliberately about my search rather than about what
exists.** The hazard is that `lastIndex` lives on the regex OBJECT, so with `g`
the SECOND `.exec` in `startOffset`'s fallback loop would start past position 0
and fail against the `^` anchor. Reaching it needs the fallback to be entered
with a line CARRYING at least two quote markers. Round 7 instrumented that
fallback over a 6,000-document differential fuzz and reported 1,463 entries with
ZERO marker-carrying lines; that is ROUND 7's measurement, not mine, and I did
not re-run it. The `.replace` site is a separate question and there I do have a
reading rather than a measurement: a global regex has `lastIndex` reset by
`RegExp.prototype[Symbol.replace]`, and `^` without `m` matches only at index 0,
so the strip should behave identically with or without `g`. I did not construct
an input that tests that reading. So the two options the arbitration offered
were a witness I could not build and a risk accepted on someone else's
measurement.

**The third option removes the class instead.** Writing the V-1 fix required
consuming the quote-marker grammar inside the scan, which would have created a
FOURTH copy of a grammar round 7 had just deduplicated. Expressing it once, as
`quoteMarkerLength(text, from)`, serves the scan AND both recovery strips, keeps
"one grammar, one definition", and has no `lastIndex` to poison. The `g`-flag
mutation can no longer be written against this module at all:

```
$ git grep -n 'SKIPPABLE_PREFIX\|QUOTE_MARKER' -- . | grep -v '^delivery/'
GITGREP_EXIT=1
```

That command returned ONE hit on the first attempt, a test comment still naming
the old constant while explaining what V-2 found at `986f58a`. It was prose
rather than a live reference, but leaving it would have made this captured
output a lie by omission, so the comment was reworded to "the block-prefix
predicate" and the command re-run. Exit 1 is "no match", which is the wanted
result.

The two call sites changed shape with it: `startOffset`'s loop is
`consumed += quoteMarkerLength(text, consumed)` and `sourceSlice`'s strip is
`piece = piece.slice(quoteMarkerLength(piece, 0))`. Both are exactly the old
semantics, because the old regex matched as a whole or not at all and the
function returns 0 in the not-at-all case; the "indentation with no `>` returns
0, not the indentation's length" case is called out in the docstring because it
is the one way a careless rewrite would differ.

### Gates, each with its own exit code

Toolchain: scratch prefix node v26.6.0 FIRST on PATH, npm 11.18.0, confirmed
with `node --version` in the shell that ran each command. Head `8b0341c`, base
`3c60acb` (`git merge-base HEAD origin/main`), phase `m3-p3`, branch
`claude/m3-p3-assurance-modes`.

The standing three:

```
npm ci                     EXIT=0
npm run build              EXIT=0    git status --porcelain after build: 0 lines
npm test                   EXIT=0    505 tests, 505 pass, 0 fail, 0 SKIPPED
```

**The SKIPPED count is quoted deliberately.** Run WITHOUT `dist/`, the same
suite is 505 tests, 496 pass, 0 fail and NINE SKIPPED, and it still exits 0.
Both numbers were taken in this working tree at this round's code:

```
without dist/   EXIT=0   tests 505  pass 496  fail 0  skipped 9   duration 181,639 ms
with    dist/   EXIT=0   tests 505  pass 505  fail 0  skipped 0   duration 176,292 ms
```

Registry gates, run as ONE bundle and then again ONE AT A TIME so each carries
its own exit code rather than inheriting the bundle's:

```
$ node bin/tiphys.ts gates run --registry gate-registry.yaml --mode direct-pr \
    --evidence <dir> --base 3c60acb --head 8b0341c --phase m3-p3

manifest-self-check   green              8 schema documents validated     EXIT=0
coverage              green            115 finding ids checked            EXIT=0
credential-scrub      green              7 credential sources probed      EXIT=0
credential-token      not-applicable     0 tokens probed                  EXIT=21
suite                 green            505 tests reported                 EXIT=0
citations             not-applicable     0 citations resolved             EXIT=21
scope                 green             41 changed paths audited          EXIT=0
clause-map            green             18 clause-map rows checked        EXIT=0
red-witness           green             35 witnesses evaluated            EXIT=0
agent-rules-drift     green             17 rendered gate rows compared    EXIT=0

bundle: declared 10 applicable 8 verdict 8 green 8 red 0 not-applicable 2
        error 0 vacuous 0
BUNDLE_EXIT=20    "required gate(s) not applicable: citations"
```

**The two exit 21s are both structural and neither is this round's doing.**

- `credential-token` is CONDITIONAL and its precondition is unmet here by
  design: `TIPHYS_IMPLEMENTER_TOKEN` is not set in this container (owner action
  A-3). Its own detail says so.
- `citations` reports `no changed path under the configured documents globs (41
  changed path(s) total)`. Its precondition is a path PREFIX over
  `delivery/plan/`, `delivery/verification/`, `delivery/decisions/`,
  `delivery/tuition/`, `delivery/requirements/` and `delivery/STATE.md`; the
  branch touches one of those trees so the precondition is met, and none of the
  41 paths is a configured citations DOCUMENT, so the gate has nothing to
  resolve. Round 7 recorded the identical status at `986f58a` over 40 paths and
  showed it also holds at `218fc12`. **I did NOT independently re-run the gate
  at either of those heads.** What I can show from my own run is that round 8
  changed the path set by exactly ONE entry, and it is not under any of those
  trees:

```
$ git diff --name-only 986f58a HEAD
delivery/work-history/m3-p3.md
src/checks.ts
test/assurance-modes.test.ts
test/behaviors.json
witness/checks-multi-marker-lines-strip-every-marker.json
witness/checks-near-miss-prefix-bounded-time.json
witness/checks-start-column-verified-not-trusted.json
```

`red-witness` green is the one that matters most here, and its detail is
explicit rather than a count: **"35 witness(es) evaluated (22 own, 13 stored
re-evaluated in 131068ms); every witness red against every declared dangerous
state and green at head"**.

**RULE (g), and it cost a red gate before it was understood.** The first bundle
run at `47dc488` was RED on `red-witness` with:

```
witness checks-multi-marker-lines-strip-every-marker: red: rule (g): members 0
and 1 mutate the same text of src/checks.ts and count as one member (collapse);
witness checks-near-miss-prefix-bounded-time: red: rule (g): ... (collapse)
```

Both new specs had paired two rewrites of the SAME function, which the gate
treats, correctly, as one member wearing two hats: that is CLAUDE.md's "one
witness is not a class" enforced mechanically. Each spec now pairs a
whole-function member with a member that inlines the defect AT THE DECISION
POINT (`startOffset`'s `if`), which is a second genuine way the class returns,
and both were measured red on exactly their own test before the specs were
rewritten:

```
MUTANT=CALLSITE_FLAT  | i tests 34  pass 33  fail 1

  a pristine copy, runs the suite, and restores by `cp` from that copy with an
  `md5sum` printed after every restore, under a shell trap so a killed run
  cannot leave a mutant behind. Never `git checkout --`. CLAIM-GREP HIT
  ("cannot leave", "never"): the trap is what makes the first true and it was
  added AFTER a killed run left the NESTED mutant in the tree, which is
  recorded above with the md5 that caught it; every mutant run in this section
  prints `RESTORED md5 9697035ed2073c1ee165b5d1e0107cc5` and that value is the

**Probes live OUTSIDE the repository.** Two probe copies were briefly written to
`src/` and moved out after the orchestrator flagged it; `tsc -b` compiles
everything under `src/`, the scope gate audits changed paths, and `git status`
must be clean after the build, so a stray `.ts` there fails three things at
once. The scratch directory carries symlinks to the sibling `src/` modules and
to `node_modules` so the relative imports and the `commonmark` require resolve
from outside the tree.

### One thing this round broke and repaired, recorded rather than hidden

While trimming a mis-pasted block out of this file I ran
`head -n "$n" <file> > tmp && mv tmp <file>` where `$n` had come from a grep
that matched nothing. `head` failed, `tmp` was empty, and the `mv` replaced this
work history with an empty file, destroying an hour of uncommitted round-8
notes. It was restored with `git show HEAD:<path> > <path>` and rewritten from
the captured command output, which survived because every number in this section
came from a command whose output was printed rather than from memory.

The lesson is not "be careful with `head`". It is the one CLAUDE.md already
records one level up: **a shell pipeline that consumes a computed value must
fail when the value is empty, and `>` truncates before the producer has had a
chance to fail.** The same shape as `head` eating grep's exit status, and the
same shape as the mutant left in the tree by a killed harness. Both were caught
here by printing state (`wc -l`, `md5sum`) rather than by noticing.

#### Feasibility of a DATA dangerous state, settled BEFORE any edit

The brief required this to be answered early, with a STOP if the harness or
rule (g) refuse a dangerous state whose `file` is a YAML data document rather
than a source file. Answer: **the harness accepts it, structurally.** Four
independent facts, each a command or a cited line, not a reading:

1. No extension or root restriction exists on a `mutation` member's `file`.
   The mutation path is `const target = join(cloneDir, member.file)` at
   src/witness/run.ts:741, and the only `startsWith("src/")` in the whole file
   is src/witness/run.ts:420, which belongs to `isDocumentPathLiteral` (the
   text-asserting detector) and not to the mutation applier. Captured:

   ```
   $ grep -n 'endsWith\|startsWith' src/witness/run.ts
   251:    if (line.startsWith("+++ /dev/null")) {
   324:  if (!first.startsWith("TAP version")) {
   420:  if (value.startsWith("src/") || value.startsWith("test/")) {
   835:    if (value === undefined || name.startsWith("NODE_TEST") || name === "NODE_OPTIONS") {
   1676:    if (!path.endsWith(".test.ts")) {
   ```

2. The witness-spec schema constrains `file` to `"pattern": "\\S"` only
   (src/gates/schemas/witness-spec.schema.json:93), so a `.yaml` path is
   as legal as a `.ts` path.

3. Rule (d) is the one that could have refused it: for a phase's OWN witness a
   mutation must touch a file in the phase diff AND its `find` text must occur
   on a line inside a CHANGED HUNK (src/witness/run.ts:1266-1286). Measured:

   ```
   $ BASE=$(git merge-base HEAD origin/main); echo $BASE
   3c60acbee541711aca2b046269aa35a03f22bb8e
   $ git diff --stat $BASE HEAD -- assurance-modes.yaml
    assurance-modes.yaml | 220 +++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++++
    1 file changed, 220 insertions(+)
   ```

   The file is wholly added by this phase, so EVERY line of it is inside a
   changed hunk and rule (d) is satisfied for any `find` text in it.

4. The mutation reaches the tests. `test/assurance-modes.test.ts:32` computes
   `repoRoot` from the test file's own URL and reads the document at
   `join(repoRoot, "assurance-modes.yaml")` (test/assurance-modes.test.ts:35),
   and `runCli` spawns `bin/tiphys.ts` with `cwd: repoRoot`
   (test/assurance-modes.test.ts:93-99). In the harness clone both resolve to
   the clone, so a mutated document is the one under test. No build is needed
   in the clone because the CLI entry is run under type stripping.

That run is below and its exit code is quoted. Nothing was handed back.

### CR-002: the mechanism, the fix, and the three members re-measured

**THE MECHANISM, NOT THE FINDING.** The finding is "mode show can be made to
print a falsehood". The mechanism is **a set constrained in one direction
only, whose cardinality is then consumed as a proxy for an identity.**

Two halves, and each is worthless without the other:

- `mode-no-undeclared-downgrade` (src/checks.ts:417) asked whether every stage
  `full` runs and this mode omits is DECLARED in `skips[]`. It never asked the
  converse, whether every stage in `skips[]` is actually omitted. A set checked
  in one direction is not constrained, and `skips[]` is shipped DATA.
- `executionStatus` (src/modes.ts:213) then used `mode.skips.length === 0` as
  its proxy for "this is the un-downgraded mode the project follows". A
  CARDINALITY standing in for an IDENTITY. It held only because `full` happened
  to be the only mode with an empty list.

**The fix, both halves.**

1. src/checks.ts:428, a soundness predicate over the two sets the check
   already computes: a stage present in BOTH `skips[]` and `pipeline[]` is
   invalid. It runs over EVERY mode INCLUDING the reference, and it runs BEFORE
   the reference is resolved so an absent `full` cannot swallow it.
2. src/modes.ts:221, `executionStatus` keys off `mode.id === "full"`,
   because blueprint section 8 defines `full` as the un-downgraded process BY
   NAME. The skip count is still PRINTED, as a fact about the mode rather than
   as the ground of the claim.

**Why the predicate is not inside the completeness loop, which is the part a
reader should check.** That loop `continue`s past the reference mode, because a
mode cannot omit a stage relative to itself. A soundness predicate written
inside it would have been GREEN against member 1, which is the sharpest of the
three and the one that targets `full`. This is the T-008 shape one level up: a
guard placed where it cannot see the thing it was written for.

**The converse burden the fix creates, discharged rather than left open.**
Keying off the NAME is only honest while the mode carrying that name really is
un-downgraded. So test/assurance-modes.test.ts now asserts that the shipped
`full` declares NO skipped stage at all, and that assertion is the one the DATA
dangerous state of the execution-status witness reddens. Without it, the fix
would have replaced an unsound proxy with a naked assertion.

#### The three members, re-measured against the round-9 code

Isolated staged install at `scratchpad/r9-inst` (a copy of `dist/`, `schemas/`,
the three shipped YAML documents and `delivery/`), so the git worktree was never
mutated for any of these. Restores are on a `trap ... EXIT`, by `cp` from a
pristine copy, never `git checkout --`, with md5 printed AND COMPARED (T-013).
Scripts: `scratchpad/r9-members.sh` and `scratchpad/r9-member2.sh`. Both printed
`RESTORE pre=c86e479ce48048edf1f61bff6a43b0cd post=c86e479ce48048edf1f61bff6a43b0cd match=YES`.

CONTROL FIRST, the unmutated shipped document, node v26.6.0:

```
CTRL_VALIDATE_EXIT=0
CTRL_SHOW_FULL_EXIT=0
execution-status: this mode is full, which blueprint section 8 defines by name as the un-downgraded process, and it is the one the tiphys project follows for its own delivery.
CTRL_SHOW_DPR_EXIT=0
execution-status: DECLARED AND VALIDATED, NEVER EXERCISED. This mode is not full, which blueprint section 8 defines by name as the un-downgraded process, so no phase of the tiphys project has ever been delivered under it. It declares 7 skipped stage(s). Its pipeline and its gate selection are checked by validation only (DR-0020).
```

MEMBER 1 (the sharp one: `full` keeps its complete twelve-stage pipeline and
gains one bogus `skips[]` entry; the reviewer's `sed` edit byte for byte). Was
`VALIDATE_EXIT=0` with the false sentence; now:

```

VALIDATE_EXIT=1
1
INVALID #/modes/0/skips mode full declares stage deploy-verify in skips while its own pipeline runs it, so skips does not describe what this mode omits (check: mode-no-undeclared-downgrade)
SHOW_EXIT=1

MEMBER 3 (`local-only` declares `implement` while `implement` is in its own

SHOW_EXIT=1
```

MEMBER 2 (`direct-pr` given `full`'s pipeline, an EMPTY `skips[]` and a
`review-contracts[]`, which the schema requires once the pipeline contains

be**: a mode that runs MORE stages than `full` is not a violation of anything,

```

INVALID_LINES=0
SHOW_EXIT=0

This mode is not full, which blueprint section 8 defines by name as the un-downgraded process, so no phase of the tiphys project has ever been delivered under it. It declares 0 skipped stage(s). Its pipeline and its gate selection are checked by validation only (DR-0020).
merge-authority: owner
```

The claim "it is the one the tiphys project follows", printed above
`merge-authority: owner`, is gone. This is why BOTH halves of the fix were
needed: the predicate alone leaves member 2 open, and the id key alone leaves
members 1 and 3 open.

### The CR-002 witness: a DATA dangerous state, ACCEPTED and RED

The reviewer's sharpest point was not the defect, it was the guard:
`witness/modes-execution-status-derived.json` declared two dangerous states and
BOTH were CODE mutations, so it guarded "someone breaks the function" and not
"someone edits the data", when a data edit is this artifact's entire threat
model. No witness in this repository did a DATA dangerous state before this
round.

**TRANSLITERATION NOTICE (CLAUDE.md convention 3).** Node's test reporter prints
non-ASCII glyphs. Every captured `node --test` excerpt quoted in this round-9
section has been transliterated as follows and in no other way: U+2716 (heavy
multiplication x, the failing-test mark) rendered `x`, 6 occurrences; U+2139
(information source, the summary-line mark) rendered `i`, 24 occurrences. No
other byte of any captured output in this section was changed, nothing was
hand-written to avoid a glyph, and the untransliterated originals are at
`scratchpad/r9-datared-*.txt`.

#### Two witnesses, four DATA members between them

`witness/checks-mode-skips-sound.json`, new, behavior
`mode-skips-describe-only-omitted-stages`:

| member | kind | file | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | mutation | `assurance-modes.yaml` | `full` gains `deploy-verify` in `skips[]` while still running it (CR-002 member 1) |
| 1 | mutation | `assurance-modes.yaml` | `local-only` gains `implement` in `skips[]` while still running it (CR-002 member 3) |
| 2 | mutation | `src/checks.ts` | the soundness predicate's condition defanged to `if (false)` |

`witness/modes-execution-status-derived.json`, amended:

| member | kind | file | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | mutation | `src/modes.ts` | the id key defanged to `if (true)` (replaces the retired `skips.length === 0` mutation, whose find text no longer exists) |
| 1 | mutation | `src/modes.ts` | `if (!context.shippedDocument)` defanged to `if (false)` (unchanged) |
| 2 | mutation | `assurance-modes.yaml` | `deploy-verify` moved OUT of `full`'s pipeline and INTO its `skips[]`, so `full` becomes a genuine declared downgrade while keeping the name |

Members 0 and 1 of the new witness are structurally different in the sense rule
(g) means it, not by relabelling: one targets the REFERENCE mode, which the
completeness loop `continue`s past, and one targets a non-reference mode, which
that loop traverses.

**So the answer to the question the brief told me to settle early is: YES, the
