#### WHY each DATA member reddens, which an exit code cannot tell you

An exit code says a test failed. It does not say the test failed for the reason
the witness is about, and a member that reddens for the wrong reason proves
nothing (I produced one earlier in this round and recorded it above). So each
red arm was reproduced BY HAND, in a `git archive` extraction outside the
repository with `node_modules` symlinked in and the scratch tree removed on a
trap: `scratchpad/r9-datared.sh`. It printed `CLEANUP done, LAB present after=NO`.

CONTROL, the unmutated archive: `TEST_EXIT=0` for both named tests.

DATA member 0 of the new witness, reddening at the CONTROL assertion, which is
the assertion that says the shipped document is clean:

```
x a mode declaring a stage in skips that its own pipeline runs is rejected, and the shipped document is green (912.749529ms)
  AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: Expected values to be strictly equal:
  1 !== 0
      at TestContext.<anonymous> (.../test/assurance-modes.test.ts:468:12)
    actual: 1, expected: 0, operator: 'strictEqual'
```

DATA member 1, reddening at the fixture's own attributability assertion (the
fixture is built FROM the shipped document, so a second contradiction elsewhere
in the data makes it no longer the-document-with-one-thing-changed):

```
x a mode declaring a stage in skips that its own pipeline runs is rejected, and the shipped document is green (492.82285ms)
  AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: INVALID #/modes/0/skips mode full declares stage deploy-verify in skips while its own pipeline runs it, so skips does not describe what this mode omits (check: mode-no-undeclared-downgrade)
  INVALID #/modes/2/skips mode local-only declares stage implement in skips while its own pipeline runs it, so skips does not describe what this mode omits (check: mode-no-undeclared-downgrade)
  2 !== 1
      at TestContext.<anonymous> (.../test/assurance-modes.test.ts:396:12)
```

**The status witness's DATA member is the load-bearing one and it needed a
control of its own**, because it must redden through the ANNOTATION and not
through the document becoming invalid. Measured, in the same lab:

```
--- is the mutated document still VALID? ---
MUTATED_VALIDATE_EXIT=0
MUTATED_SHOW_EXIT=0
execution-status: this mode is full, which blueprint section 8 defines by name as the un-downgraded process, and it is the one the tiphys project follows for its own delivery.
```

The document is VALID, `mode show` serves it at exit 0, and the sentence it
prints is FALSE, because `full` now declares a skipped stage. That is the
residual hazard the id key creates, and it is exactly what the new assertion
catches:

```
x mode show says which mode is the un-downgraded process and which is a declared downgrade never exercised (428.398455ms)
  AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: full is annotated as the un-downgraded process while declaring skips: deploy-verify
  + actual - expected
  + [ 'deploy-verify' ]
  - []
      at TestContext.<anonymous> (.../test/assurance-modes.test.ts:2123:14)
```

### CR-001 (low): the wall-clock bound, MEASURED under load and then hardened

The reviewer measured a ~1,400x margin, could not force the green arm red, said
so honestly, and recorded that two OTHER wall-clock tests failed on this box at
load average 10.8. The brief said not to leave it silent. I did both: I measured
the axis nobody had (failure mode under load), and I hardened the assertion in a
way that costs the red arm nothing.

**The measurement.** `scratchpad/r9-cr001.mjs` reproduces the test's own fixture
and its own timing region exactly (warm up `commonmark`, then time
`quotableUnits` on the two members), nine samples per member per arm, reading
`src/checks.ts` from a tree path so nothing is mutated. Load was produced by 24
busy loops on a 4-core box, a six-times oversubscription, and the burners were
killed afterwards.

```
quiet   loadavg=0.24,0.52,0.52  bullet: bytes=151 min=0.28ms median=0.42ms max=2.62ms   ordered: bytes=207 min=0.11ms median=0.20ms max=1.15ms
loaded  loadavg=12.94,3.84,1.64 bullet: bytes=151 min=0.25ms median=0.31ms max=12.68ms  ordered: bytes=207 min=0.08ms median=0.21ms max=32.79ms
loaded2 loadavg=12.94,3.84,1.64 bullet: bytes=151 min=0.25ms median=0.40ms max=17.91ms  ordered: bytes=207 min=0.16ms median=0.21ms max=29.18ms
ARM_A_EXIT=0  ARM_B_EXIT=0  ARM_B2_EXIT=0
```

At load average 12.94, which is HIGHER than the 10.8 that broke the other two
tests, the worst single sample is 32.79 ms against a 1000 ms budget: **3.3% of
the bound, a 30x margin remaining after the load.** Load inflated the worst
sample by about 12x and would need roughly another 30x on top of that to breach
it. The two tests that did flake are structurally different, and naming the
difference is the point: they wait a fixed wall-clock window and count events
that must arrive inside it, so descheduling REMOVES events, while this one
measures the DURATION of a single CPU-bound call.

**The hardening, and the argument that it cannot create a false green.** Load
can only make a sample slower, never faster. So the MINIMUM over repeated
samples of the same deterministic workload is a lower-bound estimator of its
true cost, and taking the minimum can only remove false REDS: if the minimum is
under budget, some run of the real workload really did complete under budget.
The test now takes up to three samples and asserts on the minimum.

**Resampling is gated by TIME, not by a count, so the red arm costs what it
always did.** A sample four times over budget is accepted as decisive without
resampling. Measured, both of this witness's declared dangerous states, applied
to a `git archive` extraction outside the repository
(`scratchpad/r9-nearmiss-witness.sh`, scratch tree removed on a trap, which
printed `CLEANUP done, LAB present after=NO`):

```
--- control: no dangerous state ---
  TEST_EXIT=0  wall_ms=318
--- dangerous state 0: the anchored backtracking isSkippablePrefix ---
  find occurrences=1
  TEST_EXIT=1  wall_ms=21453
  AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: bullet run: rejecting a 151-byte near miss took 21138.3 ms (best of 1: 21138.3), over the 1000 ms budget; the block-prefix test is backtracking rather than scanning
--- dangerous state 1: the inline backtracking pattern at the call site ---
  find occurrences=1
  TEST_EXIT=1  wall_ms=12578
  AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: bullet run: rejecting a 151-byte near miss took 12206.1 ms (best of 1: 12206.1), over the 1000 ms budget; the block-prefix test is backtracking rather than scanning
```

`best of 1` in both red arms is the load-bearing detail: the ceiling gate did
what it is for, so the witness costs one sample exactly as before, and the
harness's 120,000 ms per-run timeout is not brought closer.

**What this does NOT establish.** It is one container, one engine, one kernel
scheduler, and synthetic CPU load only. I did not measure under I/O pressure,
under memory pressure, or on a CI runner, and I did not observe the test on
GitHub Actions at all. If it flakes there the failure message now carries every
sample it took, so the next reader is not left guessing whether it was time
based.

### CR-003 (low): the edit count re-derived, and WHY THE CORRECTION IS NOT ON THIS BRANCH

**Re-derived independently, one edit at a time**, in a `git archive` extraction
outside the repository, with the three affected validations run after each edit
and each exit code printed separately (`scratchpad/r9-cr003.sh`; it printed
`CLEANUP done, LAB present after=NO`). A fourth mode id `review-only` was added
and taken to green.

| # | edit | named by the amendment? | measured effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `assurance-modes.yaml` declares the mode | YES | `MODES_VALIDATE_EXIT=1` |
| 2 | `schemas/assurance-modes.schema.json`, mode-id `enum` | **NO** | this is the FIRST failure: `INVALID #/modes/3/id value "review-only" is not one of the permitted values "full", "direct-pr", "local-only"` |
| 3 | `gate-registry.yaml`, the `modes` list of every gate the mode selects | **NO** | unavoidable: `gate-sets` has `minItems: 1`, and `mode-gate-sets-resolve` reddens while the registry does not name the mode |
| 4 | `schemas/gate-registry.schema.json`, its own `modes` item `enum` | **NO** | `REGISTRY_VALIDATE_EXIT=1`, `INVALID #/gates/0/modes/3 value "review-only" is not one of the permitted values` |
| 5 | `schemas/charter.schema.json`, `delivery-mode` | YES | still red on the sibling: see below |
| 6 | `schemas/charter.schema.json`, `assurance-tier` | YES | `MODES_VALIDATE_EXIT=0` |

The half-fix trap the amendment predicts is real and was reproduced:

```
--- after edit 5 : schemas/charter.schema.json delivery-mode only (the HALF FIX) ---
  MODES_VALIDATE_EXIT=1
    INVALID #/modes assurance-modes.yaml declares mode ids [direct-pr, full, local-only, review-only] and the assurance-tier enum in schemas/charter.schema.json is [direct-pr, full, local-only]; the two must be equal (check: charter-mode-enum-matches-modes)
--- after edit 6 : schemas/charter.schema.json assurance-tier too ---
  MODES_VALIDATE_EXIT=0
  REGISTRY_VALIDATE_EXIT=0
  CHARTER_VALIDATE_EXIT=0
--- and the new mode is reachable ---
  SHOW_EXIT=0
```

**SO: SIX edits, and THREE of them are unnamed, not two.** The review's own
headline says "the two it omits" while its numbered body lists three unnamed
(items 2, 5 and 6 in its numbering). My measurement agrees with the review's
BODY. Edit 2 is the first thing that fails, before any edit the amendment names
is reached, so a reader budgeting the amendment's four lands red on a path the
amendment does not mention.

**A FAILED FIRST RUN, recorded rather than quietly redone.** My first
derivation script patched `gate-registry.yaml` with the wrong line shape and
printed `gate-registry.yaml modes rows patched: 0`. Edit 3 therefore never
happened and the run's conclusion about edits 3 and 4 would have been an
artifact of my own broken patch. It was caught by PRINTING the patch count
rather than assuming the edit landed. The corrected run is the one above.

#### The correction itself is OUT OF SCOPE for this branch, and that is MEASURED

The amendment lives at delivery/plan/kernel-plan-m3.md:2553.

the two standing extras, which are `test/behaviors.json` and
`delivery/work-history/<phase>.md` (src/gates/scope.ts:739). Rather than assume

=== PROBE: same head plus one line in delivery/plan/kernel-plan-m3.md ===

```

The clone was deleted afterwards. **Per the brief's own instruction, I am

> reads as ONE edit. Measured, adding a fourth mode id is SIX coordinated
> edits, and three of them are on paths this amendment used to omit:

### THE DERIVATION, and WHAT IT DID NOT COVER. READ THIS FIRST.

The fix-round contract requires the exact command that enumerates every site of
the mechanism, its FULL output, and the regions the search excluded. T-012 adds
that the AXES must be named, not only the gaps, because a document silent on an
axis asserts nothing about it and the reader will not notice the silence.

#### The derivation, with full output

The mechanism has two halves, so it needs two enumerations.

D1, every read of `skips` in shipped code. The scope is `src/` and `bin/`
because those are what ships; `test/` is excluded deliberately and revisited
below.

```
$ grep -rn 'skips' src/ bin/
src/modes.ts:65:  skips: string[];
src/modes.ts:133:      skips: strings(record, "skips"),
src/modes.ts:167: * declared, never improvised", so a mode with a non-empty `skips` list is BY

src/modes.ts:192:    `DECLARED AND VALIDATED, NEVER EXERCISED. This mode declares ${String(mode.skips.length)} ` +
src/modes.ts:247:  section("skips", mode.skips);
src/gates/validate.ts:35: * ignored, because a validator that silently skips `oneOf` reports a

Line numbers are the PRE-FIX ones, which is the point: this is the enumeration
the fix was derived from. Classified, every hit, none dropped silently:

| site | is it the mechanism? | why |
|---|---|---|
| `src/modes.ts:65`, `:133` | no | the type and the decode. They carry the value, they derive nothing from it |
| `src/modes.ts:185`, `:192` | **YES** | the cardinality proxy. FIXED |
| `src/modes.ts:247` | no | `section("skips", mode.skips)` prints the list. It reports the data, it makes no claim beyond it |
| `src/gates/validate.ts:35` | no | the word "skips" in a comment about `oneOf` |
| `src/gates/suite.ts` x6 | no | SKIPPED TESTS. A different `skips` entirely, and saying so is the point of listing them |
| `src/checks.ts:372`-`:389` | no | comments on the check |
| `src/checks.ts:417`, `:423`, `:424` | **YES** | the completeness half. FIXED |
| `src/checks.ts:463`, `:465`, `:466` | **examined and NOT a defect** | see below |

`src/checks.ts:463` is the third site and it deserves more than a row, because
it has the same SHAPE: `mode-stage-order` lets a mode that runs `implement`
without `adversarial-plan-review` off the hook when it declares that stage in
`skips[]`. That is an unsound `skips[]` entry excusing a real violation, which
is the mechanism exactly. It is NOT exploitable, and the reason is structural
rather than lucky: the arm is reached only when `review === -1`, that is, only
when the stage genuinely is absent from the pipeline, so the declaration it
consults is sound BY CONSTRUCTION at that point. The other arm (`review >
implement`) does not consult `skips[]` at all. I checked this rather than
assuming the two sites were the whole story.

D2, every place a claim is derived from a set CARDINALITY in the two modules,
which is the generalisation of the second half:

```
$ grep -rnE '\.length === 0|\.length !== 0|\.length > 0|\.size === 0' src/modes.ts src/checks.ts
src/modes.ts:185:  if (mode.skips.length === 0) {
src/modes.ts:238:    if (items.length === 0) {
src/checks.ts:162:    const dispatchable = unfilled.length === 0;
src/checks.ts:399:    if (rows.length === 0) {
src/checks.ts:1436:      if (matches.length === 0) {
src/checks.ts:1458:      if (conditions.length === 0) {
src/checks.ts:1576:    failed: violationLines.length > 0 || skippedLines.length > 0,
D2_GREP_EXIT=0
```

Of these, `src/modes.ts:185` is the defect. `src/modes.ts:238` prints `(none)`
for an empty section, which is a statement about the list itself. The four
`src/checks.ts` sites test emptiness of a set to decide whether a check has
anything to do, not to infer an identity from a count. None of them stands a
count in for a name.

#### WHAT THE DERIVATION DID NOT COVER, and why

Stated as AXES first, then as regions, because a gap list that names only
regions is what T-012 was written about.

**AXES.**

1. **CORRECTNESS: covered.** All three CR-002 members re-measured with exit
   codes, plus the whole suite and the whole registry.
2. **COST: covered for the near-miss bound, PARTIALLY covered elsewhere.** The
   soundness predicate is one `Set` construction and one membership test per
   declared skip, over a document with three modes and at most ten skips, so it
   is not a plausible cost change and I did not time it. That is a judgement,
   not a measurement, and I am naming it as such rather than letting silence
   imply otherwise.
3. **FAILURE MODE: covered for CR-001 under CPU load; NOT covered under I/O or
   memory pressure, and not covered on a CI runner at all.**
4. **CI, BOTH ARMS: NOT COVERED. I observed no GitHub Actions run**, neither the
   `pull_request` arm nor the post-merge `push` arm. T-009 requires both
   separately and nothing here discharges either. Every number in this section
   is local, one container, one engine, node v26.6.0.

**REGIONS.**

5. **`test/` was excluded from D1 on purpose, and that exclusion had a real
   cost.** The unsound proxy existed in the TEST as well as in the code: the
   execution-status test derived its expectation from the skip count, so it
   agreed with the implementation by construction. I found that by reading the
   test the fix would break, not from the grep. A `grep -rn 'skips' test/` would
   have surfaced it directly, and a future derivation of this shape should
   include the test tree for exactly that reason.
6. **Other documents with the same shape were not audited.**
   `role-model-config.yaml` and `gate-registry.yaml` also carry lists that other
   fields are compared against. I did not enumerate one-directional set checks
   across the whole schema surface; the derivation was scoped to `skips[]`,
   which is what CR-002 is about.
7. **The eleven accepted unwitnessed mutants and the `collectUnits` RangeError
   were not touched**, per the brief. They are recorded non-coverage and M3-P4's
   respectively, not findings of this round.
8. **Rule (g) comparing witness `find` TEXT rather than mutation EFFECT** is
   M2-P2's and untouched.
9. **I did not re-walk the acceptance criteria table.** Round 8's criteria
   review executed all of them and this round changes two source lines and two
   tests; I re-ran the whole suite and the whole registry instead, which covers
   the criteria only insofar as they are registered tests.

#### TWO OBSERVATIONS I AM NOT ACTING ON, reported rather than swallowed

1. **`tiphys mode show` dies with an unhandled EPIPE when its stdout is closed
   early**, for instance by `| head -3`. Captured while building the staged
   install: `Error: write EPIPE ... at cmdMode (dist/src/commands/mode.js:131:24)`,
   exit 1. It is pre-existing (the write path is not part of this round), it is
   outside the fixed scope of this round, and no reviewer has raised it. Naming
   it here so it is not rediscovered as new.
2. **The red-witness harness pins `src`, `test` and `bin` only.**
   `pinRoots` at src/witness/run.ts:780 builds the residue-detection pin from
   those three directories, so a DATA dangerous state on a root-level document
   such as `assurance-modes.yaml` is OUTSIDE the pinned set and a mid-run
   tampering of it would not be caught by the pin comparison. This does not
   affect the repository (the harness works in a scratch clone that it deletes),
   and it did not affect this round's results, but it is a real limit of DATA
   dangerous states as a mechanism and it belongs with M2-P2's file rather than
   with this phase. I did not change it.

#### The one claim the grep caught that I had to weaken

"Load can only make a sample slower, never faster" is the argument the CR-001
hardening rests on, and it is a MODEL of the scheduler, not something I
measured. My own numbers do not fully support it as stated: the loaded arm's
minimum for the bullet member is **0.25 ms and the quiet arm's is 0.28 ms**, so
the loaded minimum came out marginally LOWER. That is JIT and cache variance
rather than load making work faster, but the honest form of the sentence is:

> Across nine samples per arm I did not observe load reducing the cost of this
> workload by any amount that matters, and the minimum moved by 0.03 ms in the
> direction the model does not predict, which is within this measurement's own
> noise. I did not establish the monotonicity as a law and I did not try to.

**The hardening does not actually need the strong form.** What it needs is far
weaker and is directly measured: the minimum of three samples is never larger
than a single sample, so the change cannot turn a passing arm into a failing
one; and both declared dangerous states measure 12.2 s and 21.1 s, which is
twelve to twenty-one times the budget, so no sampling policy over three
attempts brings either under 1000 ms. Those two facts are what make it safe, and
both are captured above.
